Mahāsatipaṭṭhānasuttaṃ

Dīgha Nikāya 9
← Previous: Sakkapañhasuttaṃ Home Next →
372
<p n="372" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">372</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Evaṃ <pb ed="T" n="2.0325"/><pb ed="M" n="2.0231"/><pb ed="V" n="2.0214"/> me <pb ed="P" n="2.0290"/> sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā kurūsu viharati kammāsadhammaṃ nāma kurūnaṃ nigamo. Tatra kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – ‘‘bhikkhavo’’ti. ‘‘Bhaddante’’ti <note>bhadanteti (sī. syā. pī.)</note> te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ. Bhagavā etadavoca –</p> <p rend="subhead">Uddeso</p>
Thus have I heard. On one occasion the Blessed One was living among the Kurus, where there was a town of the Kurus named Kammāsadhamma.

There the Fortunate One addressed the monks, saying: “Monks!”

“Venerable sir,” those *bhikṣus* replied to the Blessed One.

The Blessed One said this:

Outline.
373
<p n="373" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">373</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Ekāyano ayaṃ, bhikkhave, maggo sattānaṃ visuddhiyā, sokaparidevānaṃ samatikkamāya dukkhadomanassānaṃ atthaṅgamāya ñāyassa adhigamāya nibbānassa sacchikiriyāya, yadidaṃ cattāro satipaṭṭhānā.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Katame cattāro? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāye kāyānupassī viharati ātāpī sampajāno satimā vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ, vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharati ātāpī sampajāno satimā, vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ, citte cittānupassī viharati ātāpī sampajāno satimā vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ, dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati ātāpī sampajāno satimā vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ.</p> <p rend="centre">Uddeso niṭṭhito.</p> <p rend="subhead">Kāyānupassanā ānāpānapabbaṃ</p>
“Monks, this is the direct path for the purification of beings, for overcoming sorrow and lamentation, for the disappearance of pain and sadness, for gaining the right path, for the realization of *Nirvana*: that is to say, the four foundations of mindfulness.

"Which four? Here, monks, a monk dwells contemplating the body in the body (*kāye kāyānupassī viharati*), ardent, clearly comprehending, mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in regard to the world. He dwells contemplating feelings in feelings (*vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharati*), ardent, clearly comprehending, mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in regard to the world. He dwells contemplating mind in mind (*citte cittānupassī viharati*), ardent, clearly comprehending, mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in regard to the world. He dwells contemplating mind-objects in mind-objects (*dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati*), ardent, clearly comprehending, mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in regard to the world.

The summary is finished.

Contemplation of the Body: The Section on Mindfulness of Breathing.
<p n="373" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">373</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Evaṃ</hi><pb ed="P" n="3.0741"/><pb ed="V" n="2.0299"/><pb ed="M" n="2.0334"/><hi rend="bold">me suta</hi>nti mahāsatipaṭṭhānasuttaṃ. Tatrāyamapubbapadavaṇṇanā – <hi rend="bold">ekāyano ayaṃ, bhikkhave, maggo</hi>ti kasmā bhagavā idaṃ suttamabhāsi? Kururaṭṭhavāsīnaṃ gambhīradesanāpaṭiggahaṇasamatthatāya. Kururaṭṭhavāsino kira bhikkhū bhikkhuniyo upāsakā upāsikāyo utupaccayādisampannattā tassa raṭṭhassa sappāyautupaccayasevanena niccaṃ kallasarīrā kallacittā ca honti. Te cittasarīrakallatāya anuggahitapaññābalā gambhīrakathaṃ paṭiggahetuṃ samatthā honti. Tena nesaṃ bhagavā imaṃ gambhīradesanāpaṭiggahaṇasamatthataṃ sampassanto ekavīsatiyā ṭhānesu kammaṭṭhānaṃ arahatte pakkhipitvā idaṃ gambhīratthaṃ mahāsatipaṭṭhānasuttaṃ abhāsi. Yathā hi puriso suvaṇṇacaṅkoṭakaṃ labhitvā tattha nānāpupphāni pakkhipeyya, suvaṇṇamañjūsaṃ vā pana labhitvā sattaratanāni pakkhipeyya, evaṃ bhagavā kururaṭṭhavāsiparisaṃ labhitvā gambhīradesanaṃ desesi. Tenevettha aññānipi gambhīratthāni imasmiṃ dīghanikāye <hi rend="bold">mahānidānaṃ</hi> majjhimanikāye <pb ed="P" n="3.0742"/><hi rend="bold">satipaṭṭhānaṃ, sāropamaṃ, rukkhopamaṃ, raṭṭhapālaṃ, māgaṇḍiyaṃ, āneñjasappāya</hi>nti aññānipi suttāni desesi.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Apica tasmiṃ janapade catasso parisā pakatiyāva satipaṭṭhānabhāvanānuyogamanuyuttā viharanti, antamaso dāsakammakaraparijānāpi satipaṭṭhānapaṭisaṃyuttameva kathaṃ kathenti. Udakatitthasuttakantanaṭṭhānādīsupi niratthakakathā nāma nappavattati. Sace kāci itthī ‘‘amma, tvaṃ kataraṃ satipaṭṭhānabhāvanaṃ manasikarosī’’ti pucchitā ‘‘na kiñcī’’ti vadati, taṃ garahanti ‘‘dhiratthu tava jīvitaṃ, jīvamānāpi tvaṃ matasadisā’’ti. Atha naṃ ‘‘mā dāni puna evamakāsī’’ti ovaditvā aññataraṃ satipaṭṭhānaṃ uggaṇhāpenti. Yā pana ‘‘ahaṃ asukasatipaṭṭhānaṃ nāma manasikaromī’’ti vadati, tassā ‘‘sādhu sādhū’’ti sādhukāraṃ katvā ‘‘tava <pb ed="V" n="2.0300"/> jīvitaṃ sujīvitaṃ, tvaṃ nāma manussattaṃ pattā, tavatthāya sammāsambuddho uppanno’’tiādīhi pasaṃsanti. Na kevalañcettha manussajātikāva satipaṭṭhānamanasikārayuttā, te nissāya viharantā tiracchānagatāpi.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0300"/> <p rend="bodytext">Tatridaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0335"/> vatthu – eko kira naṭako suvapotakaṃ gahetvā sikkhāpento vicarati. So bhikkhunupassayaṃ upanissāya vasitvā gamanakāle suvapotakaṃ pamussitvā gato. Taṃ sāmaṇeriyo gahetvā paṭijaggiṃsu. Buddharakkhito tissa nāmaṃ akaṃsu. Taṃ ekadivasaṃ purato nisinnaṃ disvā mahātherī āha – ‘‘buddharakkhitā’’ti. Kiṃ, ayyeti? Atthi te koci bhāvanāmanasikāroti? Natthi, ayyeti. Āvuso, pabbajitānaṃ santike vasantena nāma vissaṭṭhaattabhāvena bhavituṃ na vaṭṭati, kocideva manasikāro icchitabbo, tvaṃ pana aññaṃ na sakkhissasi, ‘‘aṭṭhi aṭṭhī’’ti sajjhāyaṃ karohīti. So theriyā ovāde ṭhatvā ‘‘aṭṭhi aṭṭhī’’ti sajjhāyanto carati.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0335"/> <p rend="bodytext">Taṃ ekadivasaṃ pātova toraṇagge nisīditvā bālātapaṃ tapamānaṃ eko sakuṇo nakhapañjarena aggahesi. So ‘‘kiri kirī’’ti saddamakāsi. Sāmaṇeriyo sutvā ‘‘ayye buddharakkhito sakuṇena gahito, mocema na’’nti leḍḍuādīni gahetvā anubandhitvā mocesuṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0743"/>. Taṃ ānetvā purato ṭhapitaṃ therī āha – ‘‘buddharakkhita, sakuṇena gahitakāle kiṃ cintesī’’ti? Na, ayye, aññaṃ kiñci cintesiṃ, aṭṭhipuñjova aṭṭhipuñjaṃ gahetvā gacchati, katarasmiṃ ṭhāne vippakirissatīti, evaṃ ayye aṭṭhipuñjameva cintesinti. Sādhu, sādhu, buddharakkhita, anāgate bhavakkhayassa te paccayo bhavissatīti. Evaṃ tattha tiracchānagatāpi satipaṭṭhānamanasikārayuttā. Tasmā nesaṃ bhagavā satipaṭṭhānabuddhimeva janento idaṃ suttamabhāsi.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0743"/> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha <hi rend="bold">ekāyano</hi>ti ekamaggo. Maggassa hi –</p> <hi rend="bold">ekāyano</hi> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Maggo pantho patho pajjo, añjasaṃ vaṭumāyanaṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Nāvā uttarasetū ca, kullo ca bhisisaṅkamo’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Bahūni nāmāni. Svāyamidha ayananāmena vutto, tasmā <hi rend="bold">ekāyano ayaṃ, bhikkhave, maggo</hi>ti ettha ekamaggo ayaṃ, bhikkhave, maggo na dvidhā pathabhūtoti evamattho daṭṭhabbo. Atha vā <pb ed="V" n="2.0301"/> ekena ayitabboti ekāyano. Ekenāti gaṇasaṅgaṇikaṃ pahāya vūpakaṭṭhena pavivittacittena. Ayitabbo paṭipajjitabbo, ayanti vā etenāti ayano, saṃsārato <pb ed="M" n="2.0336"/> nibbānaṃ gacchantīti attho. Ekassa ayano ekāyano. <hi rend="bold">Ekassā</hi>ti seṭṭhassa. Sabbasattaseṭṭho ca bhagavā, tasmā bhagavatoti vuttaṃ hoti. Kiñcāpi hi tena aññepi ayanti, evaṃ santepi bhagavatova so ayano tena uppāditattā. Yathāha ‘‘so hi, brāhmaṇa, bhagavā anuppannassa maggassa uppādetā’’tiādi (ma. ni. 3.79). Ayatīti vā ayano, gacchati pavattatīti attho. Ekasmiṃ ayanoti ekāyano, imasmiññeva dhammavinaye pavattati, na aññatthāti vuttaṃ hoti. Yathāha – ‘‘imasmiṃ kho, subhadda, dhammavinaye ariyo aṭṭhaṅgiko maggo upalabbhatī’’ti (dī. ni. 2.214). Desanābhedoyeva heso, atthato pana ekova. Apica ekaṃ ayatīti ekāyano. Pubbabhāge nānāmukhabhāvanānayappavattopi aparabhāge ekaṃ nibbānameva <pb ed="P" n="3.0744"/> gacchatīti vuttaṃ hoti. Yathāha brahmā sahampati –</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0301"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0336"/> <hi rend="bold">Ekassā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0744"/> <p rend="gatha1">Ekāyanaṃ jātikhayantadassī,</p> <p rend="gatha2">Maggaṃ pajānāti hitānukampī;</p> <p rend="gatha3">Etena maggena tariṃsu pubbe,</p> <p rend="gathalast">Tarissanti ye ca taranti oghanti. (saṃ. ni. 5.409);</p> <p rend="bodytext">Keci pana ‘‘na pāraṃ diguṇaṃ yantī’’ti gāthānayena yasmā ekavāraṃ nibbānaṃ gacchati, tasmā ‘‘ekāyano’’ti vadanti, taṃ na yujjati. Imassa hi atthassa sakiṃ ayanoti iminā byañjanena bhavitabbaṃ. Yadi pana ekaṃ ayanamassa ekā gati pavattīti evaṃ atthaṃ yojetvā vucceyya, byañjanaṃ yujjeyya, attho pana ubhayathāpi na yujjati. Kasmā? Idha pubbabhāgamaggassa adhippetattā. Kāyādicatuārammaṇappavatto hi pubbabhāgasatipaṭṭhānamaggo idhādhippeto, na lokuttaro, so ca anekavārampi ayati, anekañcassa ayanaṃ hoti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Pubbepi ca imasmiṃ pade mahātherānaṃ sākacchā ahosiyeva. Tipiṭakacūḷanāgatthero pubbabhāgasatipaṭṭhānamaggoti āha. Ācariyo panassa tipiṭakacūḷasumatthero missakamaggoti āha. Pubbabhāgo bhanteti? Missako, āvusoti. Ācariye pana punappunaṃ bhaṇante appaṭibāhitvā <pb ed="M" n="2.0337"/> tuṇhī ahosi. Pañhaṃ avinicchinitvāva uṭṭhahiṃsu. Athācariyatthero nahānakoṭṭhakaṃ gacchanto ‘‘mayā missakamaggo kathito, cūḷanāgo pubbabhāgamaggoti ādāya voharati, ko nu kho ettha nicchayo’’ti suttantaṃ ādito paṭṭhāya parivattento ‘‘yo hi koci <pb ed="V" n="2.0302"/>, bhikkhave, ime cattāro satipaṭṭhāne evaṃ bhāveyya satta vassānī’’ti imasmiṃ ṭhāne sallakkhesi. Lokuttaramaggo uppajjitvā satta vassāni tiṭṭhamāno nāma natthi, mayā vutto missakamaggo na labbhati. Cūḷanāgena diṭṭho pubbabhāgamaggova labbhatīti ñatvā aṭṭhamiyaṃ dhammasavane saṅghuṭṭhe agamāsi.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0337"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0302"/> <p rend="bodytext">Porāṇakattherā kira piyadhammasavanā honti, saddaṃ sutvāva ‘‘ahaṃ paṭhamaṃ, ahaṃ paṭhama’’nti ekappahāreneva osaranti. Tasmiñca divase cūḷanāgattherassa vāro, tena <pb ed="P" n="3.0745"/> dhammāsane nisīditvā bījaniṃ gahetvā pubbagāthāsu vuttāsu therassa āsanapiṭṭhiyaṃ ṭhitassa etadahosi – ‘‘raho nisīditvā na vakkhāmī’’ti. Porāṇakattherā hi anusūyakā honti. Na attano rucimeva ucchubhāraṃ viya evaṃ ukkhipitvā vicaranti, kāraṇameva gaṇhanti, akāraṇaṃ vissajjenti. Tasmā thero ‘‘āvuso, cūḷanāgā’’ti āha. So ācariyassa viya saddoti dhammaṃ ṭhapetvā ‘‘kiṃ bhante’’ti āha. Āvuso, cūḷanāga, mayā vutto missakamaggo na labbhati, tayā vutto pubbabhāgasatipaṭṭhānamaggova labbhatīti. Thero cintesi – ‘‘amhākaṃ ācariyo sabbapariyattiko tepiṭako sutabuddho, evarūpassāpi nāma bhikkhuno ayaṃ pañho āluḷeti, anāgate mama bhātikā imaṃ pañhaṃ āluḷessantīti suttaṃ gahetvā imaṃ pañhaṃ niccalaṃ karissāmī’’ti paṭisambhidāmaggato ‘‘ekāyanamaggo vuccati pubbabhāgasatipaṭṭhānamaggo’’.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0745"/> <p rend="gatha1">Maggānaṭṭhaṅgiko seṭṭho, saccānaṃ caturo padā;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Virāgo seṭṭho dhammānaṃ, dvipadānañca cakkhumā.</p> <p rend="gatha1">Eseva maggo natthañño, dassanassa visuddhiyā;</p> <p rend="gatha2">Etañhi tumhe paṭipajjatha, mārasenappamaddanaṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Etañhi tumhe paṭipannā, dukkhassantaṃ karissathāti. –</p> <p rend="bodytext">Suttaṃ āharitvā ṭhapesi.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Maggo</hi>ti <pb ed="M" n="2.0338"/> kenaṭṭhena maggo? Nibbānagamanaṭṭhena nibbānatthikehi magganīyaṭṭhena ca. <hi rend="bold">Sattānaṃ visuddhiyā</hi>ti rāgādīhi malehi abhijjhāvisamalobhādīhi ca upakkilesehi kiliṭṭhacittānaṃ sattānaṃ visuddhatthāya. Tathā hi imināva maggena ito satasahassakappādhikānaṃ catunnaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0746"/> asaṅkhyeyyānaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0303"/> upari ekasmiṃyeva kappe nibbatte taṇhaṅkaramedhaṅkarasaraṇaṅkaradīpaṅkaranāmake buddhe ādiṃ katvā sakyamunipariyosānā aneke sammāsambuddhā anekasatā paccekabuddhā gaṇanapathaṃ vītivattā ariyasāvakā cāti ime sattā sabbe cittamalaṃ pavāhetvā paramavisuddhiṃ pattā. Rūpamalavasena pana saṃkilesavodānapaññattiyeva natthi. Tathā hi –</p> <hi rend="bold">Maggo</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0338"/> <hi rend="bold">Sattānaṃ visuddhiyā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0746"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0303"/> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Rūpena saṃkiliṭṭhena, saṃkilissanti māṇavā;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Rūpe suddhe visujjhanti, anakkhātaṃ mahesinā.</p> <p rend="gatha1">Cittena saṃkiliṭṭhena, saṃkilissanti māṇavā;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Citte suddhe visujjhanti, iti vuttaṃ mahesinā’’.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Yathāha – ‘‘cittasaṃkilesā, bhikkhave, sattā saṃkilissanti, cittavodānā visujjhantī’’ti. Tañca cittavodānaṃ iminā satipaṭṭhānamaggena hoti. Tenāha ‘‘sattānaṃ visuddhiyā’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Sokaparidevānaṃ samatikkamāyā</hi>ti sokassa ca paridevassa ca samatikkamāya pahānāyāti attho, ayañhi maggo bhāvito <hi rend="bold">santatimahāmattā</hi>dīnaṃ viya sokasamatikkamāya, <hi rend="bold">paṭācārā</hi>dīnaṃ viya paridevasamatikkamāya saṃvattati. Tenāha ‘‘sokaparidevānaṃ samatikkamāyā’’ti. Kiñcāpi hi santatimahāmatto –</p> <hi rend="bold">Sokaparidevānaṃ samatikkamāyā</hi> <hi rend="bold">santatimahāmattā</hi> <hi rend="bold">paṭācārā</hi> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Yaṃ pubbe taṃ visodhehi, pacchā te mātu kiñcanaṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Majjhe ce no gahessasi, upasanto carissasī’’ti. (su. ni. 945);</p> <p rend="bodytext">Imaṃ gāthaṃ sutvāva saha paṭisambhidāhi arahattaṃ patto. Paṭācārā –</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Na santi puttā tāṇāya, na pitā nāpi bandhavā;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Antakenādhipannassa, natthi ñātīsu tāṇatā’’ti. (dha. pa. 288);</p> <p rend="bodytext">Imaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0747"/><pb ed="M" n="2.0339"/> gāthaṃ sutvā sotāpattiphale patiṭṭhitā. Yasmā pana kāyavedanācittadhammesu kañci dhammaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0304"/> anāmasitvā bhāvanā nāma natthi, tasmā tepi imināva maggena sokaparideve samatikkantāti veditabbā.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0747"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0339"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0304"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Dukkhadomanassānaṃ atthaṅgamāyā</hi>ti kāyikadukkhassa cetasikadomanassassa cāti imesaṃ dvinnaṃ atthaṅgamāya, nirodhāyāti attho. Ayañhi maggo bhāvito tissattherādīnaṃ viya dukkhassa, sakkādīnaṃ viya ca domanassassa atthaṅgamāya saṃvattati.</p> <hi rend="bold">Dukkhadomanassānaṃ atthaṅgamāyā</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Tatrāyaṃ atthadīpanā – sāvatthiyaṃ kira <hi rend="bold">tisso</hi> nāma kuṭumbikaputto cattālīsa hiraññakoṭiyo pahāya pabbajitvā agāmake araññe viharati. Tassa kaniṭṭhabhātu bhariyā ‘‘gacchatha, naṃ jīvitā voropethā’’ti pañcasate core pesesi. Te gantvā theraṃ parivāretvā nisīdiṃsu. Thero āha – ‘‘kasmā āgatattha upāsakā’’ti? Taṃ jīvitā voropessāmāti. Pāṭibhogaṃ me upāsakā, gahetvā ajjekarattiṃ jīvitaṃ dethāti. Ko te, samaṇa, imasmiṃ ṭhāne pāṭibhogo bhavissatīti? Thero mahantaṃ pāsāṇaṃ gahetvā dve ūruṭṭhīni bhinditvā ‘‘vaṭṭati upāsakā pāṭibhogo’’ti āha. Te apakkamitvā caṅkamanasīse aggiṃ katvā nipajjiṃsu. Therassa vedanaṃ vikkhambhetvā sīlaṃ paccavekkhato parisuddhaṃ sīlaṃ nissāya pītipāmojjaṃ uppajji. Tato anukkamena vipassanaṃ vaḍḍhento tiyāmarattiṃ samaṇadhammaṃ katvā aruṇuggamane arahattaṃ patto imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –</p> <hi rend="bold">tisso</hi> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Ubho pādāni bhinditvā, saññapessāmi vo ahaṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Aṭṭiyāmi harāyāmi, sarāgamaraṇaṃ ahaṃ.</p> <p rend="gatha1">Evāhaṃ cintayitvāna, yathābhūtaṃ vipassisaṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Sampatte aruṇuggamhi, arahattamapāpuṇi’’nti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Aparepi <hi rend="bold">tiṃsa bhikkhū</hi> bhagavato santike kammaṭṭhānaṃ gahetvā araññavihāre vassaṃ upagantvā ‘‘āvuso, tiyāmarattiṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0748"/> samaṇadhammova kātabbo, na aññamaññassa santikaṃ āgantabba’’nti vatvā vihariṃsu. Tesaṃ samaṇadhammaṃ katvā paccūsasamaye pacalāyantānaṃ eko byaggho āgantvā ekekaṃ bhikkhuṃ gahetvā gacchati. Na koci ‘‘maṃ byaggho gaṇhī’’ti vācampi nicchāresi. Evaṃ pañcasu dasasu bhikkhūsu khāditesu uposathadivase ‘‘itare, āvuso <pb ed="M" n="2.0340"/>, kuhi’’nti pucchitvā ñatvā ca ‘‘idāni gahitena gahitomhīti vattabba’’nti vatvā vihariṃsu <pb ed="V" n="2.0305"/>. Atha aññataraṃ daharabhikkhuṃ purimanayeneva byaggho gaṇhi. So ‘‘byaggho bhante’’ti āha. Bhikkhū kattaradaṇḍe ca ukkāyo ca gahetvā mocessāmāti anubandhiṃsu. Byaggho bhikkhūnaṃ agatiṃ chinnataṭaṭṭhānamāruyha taṃ bhikkhuṃ pādaṅguṭṭhakato paṭṭhāya khādituṃ ārabhi. Itarepi ‘‘idāni sappurisa, amhehi kattabbaṃ natthi, bhikkhūnaṃ viseso nāma evarūpe ṭhāne paññāyatī’’ti āhaṃsu. So byagghamukhe nipannova taṃ vedanaṃ vikkhambhetvā vipassanaṃ vaḍḍhento yāva gopphakā khāditasamaye sotāpanno hutvā, yāva jaṇṇukā khāditasamaye sakadāgāmī, yāva nābhiyā khāditasamaye anāgāmī hutvā, hadayarūpe akhāditeyeva saha paṭisambhidāhi arahattaṃ patvā imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –</p> <hi rend="bold">tiṃsa bhikkhū</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0748"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0340"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0305"/> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Sīlavā vatasampanno, paññavā susamāhito;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Muhuttaṃ pamādamanvāya, byagghenoruddhamānaso.</p> <p rend="gatha1">Pañjarasmiṃ gahetvāna, silāya uparī kato;</p> <p rend="gatha2">Kāmaṃ khādatu maṃ byaggho, aṭṭhiyā ca nhārussa ca;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Kilese khepayissāmi, phusissāmi vimuttiya’’nti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Aparopi pītamallatthero nāma gihikāle tīsu rajjesu paṭākaṃ gahetvā tambapaṇṇidīpaṃ āgamma rājānaṃ passitvā raññā <pb ed="P" n="3.0749"/> katānuggaho ekadivasaṃ kilañjakāpaṇasāladvārena gacchanto ‘‘rūpaṃ, bhikkhave, na tumhākaṃ, taṃ pajahatha, taṃ vo pahīnaṃ dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya bhavissatī’’ti na tumhākavākyaṃ sutvā cintesi ‘‘neva kira rūpaṃ attano, na vedanā’’ti. So taṃyeva aṅkusaṃ katvā nikkhamitvā mahāvihāraṃ gantvā pabbajjaṃ yācitvā pabbajito upasampanno dvemātikā paguṇā katvā tiṃsa bhikkhū gahetvā gabalavāliyaaṅgaṇaṃ gantvā samaṇadhammaṃ akāsi. Pādesu avahantesu jaṇṇukehi caṅkamati. Tamenaṃ rattiṃ eko migaluddako migoti maññamāno pahari. Satti vinivijjhitvā gatā, so taṃ sattiṃ harāpetvā paharaṇamukhāni tiṇavaṭṭiyā pūrāpetvā pāsāṇapiṭṭhiyaṃ attānaṃ nisīdāpetvā okāsaṃ kāretvā vipassanaṃ vaḍḍhetvā saha <pb ed="M" n="2.0341"/> paṭisambhidāhi arahattaṃ patvā ukkāsitasaddena āgatānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ byākaritvā imaṃ udānaṃ udānesi –</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0749"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0341"/> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Bhāsitaṃ buddhaseṭṭhassa, sabbalokaggavādino;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Na tumhākamidaṃ rūpaṃ, taṃ jaheyyātha bhikkhavo.</p> <p rend="gatha1">Aniccā <pb ed="V" n="2.0306"/> vata saṅkhārā, uppādavayadhammino;</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0306"/> <p rend="gathalast">Uppajjitvā nirujjhanti, tesaṃ vūpasamo sukho’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Evaṃ tāva ayaṃ maggo tissattherādīnaṃ viya dukkhassa atthaṅgamāya saṃvattati.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Sakko pana devānamindo attano pañcavidhapubbanimittaṃ disvā maraṇabhayasantajjito domanassajāto bhagavantaṃ upasaṅkamitvā pañhaṃ pucchi. So upekkhāpañhavissajjanāvasāne <pb ed="P" n="3.0750"/> asītisahassāhi devatāhi saddhiṃ sotāpattiphale patiṭṭhāsi. Sā cassa upapatti puna pākatikāva ahosi.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0750"/> <p rend="bodytext">Subrahmāpi devaputto accharāsahassaparivuto saggasampattiṃ anubhoti. Tattha pañcasatā accharāyo rukkhato pupphāni ocinantiyo cavitvā niraye uppannā. So ‘‘kiṃ imā cirāyantī’’ti upadhārento tāsaṃ niraye nibbattanabhāvaṃ ñatvā ‘‘kittakaṃ nu kho mama āyū’’ti upaparikkhanto attano āyuparikkhayaṃ viditvā cavitvā tattheva niraye nibbattanabhāvaṃ disvā bhīto ativiya domanassajāto hutvā ‘‘imaṃ me domanassaṃ satthā vinayissati, na añño’’ti avasesā pañcasatā accharāyo gahetvā bhagavantaṃ upasaṅkamitvā pañhaṃ pucchi –</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Niccaṃ utrastamidaṃ cittaṃ, niccaṃ ubbiggidaṃ mano;</p> <p rend="gatha2">Anuppannesu kicchesu, atho uppatitesu ca;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Sace atthi anutrastaṃ, taṃ me akkhāhi pucchitoti. (saṃ. ni. 1.98);</p> <p rend="bodytext">Tato naṃ bhagavā āha –</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Nāññatra bojjhā tapasā, nāññatrindriyasaṃvarā;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Nāññatra sabbanissaggā, sotthiṃ passāmi pāṇina’’nti. (saṃ. ni. 1.98);</p> <p rend="bodytext">So <pb ed="M" n="2.0342"/> desanāpariyosāne pañcahi accharāsatehi saddhiṃ sotāpattiphale patiṭṭhāya taṃ sampattiṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0307"/> thāvaraṃ katvā devalokameva agamāsīti. Evaṃ ayaṃ maggo bhāvito sakkādīnaṃ viya domanassassa atthaṅgamāya saṃvattatīti veditabbo.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0342"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0307"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Ñāyassa adhigamāyā</hi>ti ñāyo vuccati ariyo aṭṭhaṅgiko maggo, tassa adhigamāya, pattiyāti vuttaṃ hoti. Ayañhi pubbabhāge lokiyo satipaṭṭhānamaggo bhāvito lokuttaramaggassa <pb ed="P" n="3.0751"/> adhigamāya saṃvattati. Tenāha ‘‘ñāyassa adhigamāyā’’ti. <hi rend="bold">Nibbānassa sacchikiriyāyā</hi>ti taṇhāvānavirahitattā nibbānanti laddhanāmassa amatassa sacchikiriyāya, attapaccakkhatāyāti vuttaṃ hoti. Ayañhi maggo bhāvito anupubbena nibbānasacchikiriyaṃ sādheti. Tenāha ‘‘nibbānassa sacchikiriyāyā’’ti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Ñāyassa adhigamāyā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0751"/> <hi rend="bold">Nibbānassa sacchikiriyāyā</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha kiñcāpi ‘‘sattānaṃ visuddhiyā’’ti vutte sokasamatikkamādīni atthato siddhāneva honti, ṭhapetvā pana sāsanayuttikovide aññesaṃ na pākaṭāni, na ca bhagavā paṭhamaṃ sāsanayuttikovidaṃ janaṃ katvā pacchā dhammaṃ deseti. Tena teneva pana suttena taṃ taṃ atthaṃ ñāpeti. Tasmā idha yaṃ yaṃ atthaṃ ekāyanamaggo sādheti, taṃ taṃ pākaṭaṃ katvā dassento ‘‘sokaparidevānaṃ samatikkamāyā’’tiādimāha. Yasmā vā yā sattānaṃ visuddhi ekāyanamaggena saṃvattati, sā sokaparidevānaṃ samatikkamena hoti. Sokaparidevānaṃ samatikkamo dukkhadomanassānaṃ atthaṅgamena, dukkhadomanassānaṃ atthaṅgamo ñāyassādhigamena, ñāyassādhigamo nibbānassa sacchikiriyāya. Tasmā imampi kamaṃ dassento ‘‘sattānaṃ visuddhiyā’’ti vatvā ‘‘sokaparidevānaṃ samatikkamāyā’’tiādimāha.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Apica vaṇṇabhaṇanametaṃ ekāyanamaggassa. Yatheva hi bhagavā – ‘‘dhammaṃ vo, bhikkhave, desessāmi ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāsessāmi yadidaṃ chachakkānī’’ti (ma. ni. 3.420) chachakkadesanāya aṭṭhahi padehi vaṇṇaṃ abhāsi. Yathā ca ariyavaṃsadesanāya ‘‘cattārome, bhikkhave, ariyavaṃsā aggaññā rattaññā vaṃsaññā porāṇā asaṃkiṇṇā asaṃkiṇṇapubbā na <pb ed="M" n="2.0343"/> saṅkīyanti na saṅkīyissanti, appaṭikuṭṭhā samaṇehi brāhmaṇehi viññūhī’’ti (a. ni. 4.28) navahi padehi vaṇṇaṃ abhāsi; evaṃ imassāpi ekāyanamaggassa sattānaṃ visuddhiyātiādīhi sattahi padehi vaṇṇaṃ abhāsi. Kasmāti ce, tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ ussāhajananatthaṃ. Vaṇṇabhāsanañhi sutvā te bhikkhū ‘‘ayaṃ kira maggo hadayasantāpabhūtaṃ sokaṃ, vācāvippalāpabhūtaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0752"/> paridevaṃ, kāyikaasātabhūtaṃ dukkhaṃ, cetasikaasātabhūtaṃ domanassanti <pb ed="V" n="2.0308"/> cattāro upaddave hanati, visuddhiṃ ñāyaṃ nibbānanti tayo visese āvahatī’’ti ussāhajātā imaṃ dhammadesanaṃ uggahetabbaṃ pariyāpuṇitabbaṃ dhāretabbaṃ, vācetabbaṃ, imañca maggaṃ bhāvetabbaṃ maññissanti. Iti tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ ussāhajananatthaṃ vaṇṇaṃ abhāsi. Kambalavāṇijādayo kambalādīnaṃ vaṇṇaṃ viya.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0343"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0752"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0308"/> <p rend="bodytext">Yathā hi satasahassagghanikapaṇḍukambalavāṇijena ‘kambalaṃ gaṇhathā’ti ugghositepi asukakambaloti na tāva manussā jānanti. Kesakambalavāḷakambalādayopi hi duggandhā kharasamphassā kambalātveva vuccanti. Yadā pana tena gandhārako rattakambalo sukhumo ujjalo sukhasamphassoti ugghositaṃ hoti, tadā ye pahonti, te gaṇhanti. Ye nappahonti, tepi dassanakāmā honti; evameva ‘ekāyano, bhikkhave, ayaṃ maggo’ti vuttepi asukamaggoti na tāva pākaṭo hoti. Nānappakārakā hi aniyyānikamaggāpi maggātveva vuccanti. ‘‘Sattānaṃ visuddhiyā’’tiādimhi pana vutte ‘‘ayaṃ kira maggo cattāro upaddave hanati, tayo visese āvahatī’’ti ussāhajātā imaṃ dhammadesanaṃ uggahetabbaṃ pariyāpuṇitabbaṃ dhāretabbaṃ vācetabbaṃ, imañca maggaṃ bhāvetabbaṃ maññissantīti vaṇṇaṃ bhāsanto ‘‘sattānaṃ visuddhiyā’’tiādimāha. Yathā ca satasahassagghanikapaṇḍukambalavāṇijūpamā; evaṃ rattajambunadasuvaṇṇaudakappasādakamaṇiratanasuvisuddhamuttaratanapavāḷādivāṇijūpamādayopettha āharitabbā.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Yadida</hi>nti nipāto, ye imeti ayamassa attho. <hi rend="bold">Cattāro</hi>ti gaṇanaparicchedo. Tena na tato heṭṭhā, na uddhanti satipaṭṭhānaparicchedaṃ dīpeti. <hi rend="bold">Satipaṭṭhānā</hi>ti tayo satipaṭṭhānā satigocaropi tidhā paṭipannesu sāvakesu satthuno paṭighānunayavītivattatāpi, satipi. ‘‘Catunnaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0344"/>, bhikkhave, satipaṭṭhānānaṃ samudayañca atthaṅgamañca desessāmi, taṃ suṇātha…pe… ko ca, bhikkhave, kāyassa samudayo. Āhārasamudayā kāyassa samudayo’’tiādīsu (saṃ. ni. 5.408) hi <pb ed="P" n="3.0753"/> satigocaro satipaṭṭhānanti vuccati. Tathā ‘‘kāyo upaṭṭhānaṃ no sati, sati pana upaṭṭhānañceva sati cā’’tiādīsupi (paṭi. ma. 3.35). Tassattho – patiṭṭhāti asminti <hi rend="bold">paṭṭhānaṃ.</hi> Kā patiṭṭhāti? Sati. Satiyā paṭṭhānaṃ <hi rend="bold">satipaṭṭhānaṃ,</hi> padhānaṃ ṭhānanti vā paṭṭhānaṃ. Satiyā paṭṭhānaṃ satipaṭṭhānaṃ hatthiṭṭhānaassaṭṭhānādīni viya.</p> <hi rend="bold">Yadida</hi> <hi rend="bold">Cattāro</hi> <hi rend="bold">Satipaṭṭhānā</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0344"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0753"/> <hi rend="bold">paṭṭhānaṃ.</hi> <hi rend="bold">satipaṭṭhānaṃ,</hi> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Tayo satipaṭṭhānā yadariyo sevati, yadariyo sevamāno satthā gaṇamanusāsituṃ arahatī’’ti (ma. ni. 3.311) ettha tidhā paṭipannesu sāvakesu satthuno paṭighānunayavītivattatā ‘‘satipaṭṭhāna’’nti vuttā. Tassattho – paṭṭhapetabbato paṭṭhānaṃ, pavattayitabbatoti <pb ed="V" n="2.0309"/> attho. Kena paṭṭhapetabbatoti? Satiyā. Satiyā paṭṭhānaṃ satipaṭṭhānaṃ. ‘‘Cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvitā bahulīkatā satta sambojjhaṅge paripūrentī’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 3.147) pana satiyeva ‘‘satipaṭṭhānaṃ’’ti vuccati. Tassattho – paṭṭhātīti paṭṭhānaṃ, upaṭṭhāti okkanditvā pakkhanditvā pattharitvā pavattatīti attho. Satiyeva satipaṭṭhānaṃ. Atha vā saraṇaṭṭhena sati, upaṭṭhānaṭṭhena paṭṭhānaṃ. Iti sati ca sā paṭṭhānaṃ cātipi satipaṭṭhānaṃ. Idamidhādhippetaṃ.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0309"/> <p rend="bodytext">Yadi evaṃ kasmā ‘‘satipaṭṭhānā’’ti bahuvacanaṃ? Satibahuttā. Ārammaṇabhedena hi bahukā etā satiyo. Atha maggoti kasmā ekavacanaṃ? Maggaṭṭhena ekattā. Catassopi hi etā satiyo maggaṭṭhena ekattaṃ gacchanti. Vuttañhetaṃ – ‘‘maggoti kenaṭṭhena maggo? Nibbānagamanaṭṭhena. Nibbānatthikehi magganīyaṭṭhena cā’’ti. Catassopi cetā aparabhāge kāyādīsu ārammaṇesu kiccaṃ sādhayamānā nibbānaṃ gacchanti, ādito paṭṭhāya ca nibbānatthikehi maggiyanti, tasmā catassopi eko maggoti vuccanti. Evañca sati vacanānusandhinā sānusandhikāva desanā hoti, ‘‘mārasenappamaddanaṃ, vo bhikkhave, maggaṃ desessāmi, taṃ suṇātha…pe… katamo ca, bhikkhave, mārasenappamaddano maggo? Yadidaṃ satta bojjhaṅgā’’tiādīsu (saṃ. ni. 5.224) viya. Yathā mārasenappamaddanoti ca, satta bojjhaṅgāti ca atthato ekaṃ, byañjanamevettha nānaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0345"/>. Evaṃ ‘‘ekāyanamaggo’’ti <pb ed="P" n="3.0754"/> ca ‘‘cattāro satipaṭṭhānā’’ti ca atthato ekaṃ, byañjanamevettha nānaṃ, tasmā maggaṭṭhena ekattā ekavacanaṃ. Ārammaṇabhedena satibahuttā bahuvacanaṃ veditabbaṃ.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0345"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0754"/> <p rend="bodytext">Kasmā pana bhagavatā cattārova satipaṭṭhānā vuttā anūnā anadhikāti? Veneyyahitattā. Taṇhācaritadiṭṭhicaritasamathayānikavipassanāyānikesu hi mandatikkhavasena dvedhā dvedhā pavattesu veneyyesu mandassa taṇhācaritassa oḷārikaṃ kāyānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ visuddhimaggo, tikkhassa sukhumaṃ vedanānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ. Diṭṭhicaritassapi mandassa nātippabhedagataṃ cittānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ visuddhimaggo, tikkhassa atippabhedagataṃ dhammānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ visuddhimaggo. Samathayānikassa ca mandassa akicchena adhigantabbanimittaṃ paṭhamaṃ satipaṭṭhānaṃ visuddhimaggo, tikkhassa oḷārikārammaṇe asaṇṭhahanato dutiyaṃ. Vipassanāyānikassapi mandassa nātippabhedagatārammaṇaṃ tatiyaṃ, tikkhassa atippabhedagatārammaṇaṃ catutthaṃ. Iti cattārova vuttā anūnā anadhikāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Subhasukhaniccaattabhāvavipallāsappahānatthaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0310"/> vā. Kāyo hi asubho, tattha ca subhavipallāsavipallatthā sattā. Tesaṃ tattha asubhabhāvadassanena tassa vipallāsassa pahānatthaṃ paṭhamaṃ satipaṭṭhānaṃ vuttaṃ. Sukhaṃ niccaṃ attāti gahitesupi ca vedanādīsu vedanā dukkhā, cittaṃ aniccaṃ, dhammā anattā, tesu ca sukhaniccaattavipallāsavipallatthā sattā. Tesaṃ tattha dukkhādibhāvadassanena tesaṃ vipallāsānaṃ pahānatthaṃ sesāni tīṇi vuttānīti evaṃ subhasukhaniccaattabhāvavipallāsappahānatthaṃ vā cattārova vuttā anūnā anadhikāti veditabbā. Na kevalañca vipallāsappahānatthameva, atha kho caturoghayogāsavaganthaupādānaagatipahānatthampi catubbidhāhārapariññatthañca cattārova vuttāti veditabbā. Ayaṃ tāva pakaraṇanayo.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0310"/> <p rend="bodytext">Aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana saraṇavasena ceva ekattasamosaraṇavasena ca ekameva satipaṭṭhānaṃ ārammaṇavasena cattāroti etadeva vuttaṃ. Yathā <pb ed="P" n="3.0755"/> hi catudvāre nagare pācīnato āgacchantā pācīnadisāya uṭṭhānakaṃ bhaṇḍaṃ gahetvā pācīnadvārena nagarameva pavisanti, dakkhiṇato. Pacchimato. Uttarato āgacchantā uttaradisāya uṭṭhānakaṃ bhaṇḍaṃ gahetvā uttaradvārena nagarameva pavisanti; evaṃ – sampadamidaṃ veditabbaṃ. Nagaraṃ viya <pb ed="M" n="2.0346"/> hi nibbānamahānagaraṃ, dvāraṃ viya aṭṭhaṅgiko lokuttaramaggo, pācīnadisādayo viya kāyādayo.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0755"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0346"/> <p rend="bodytext">Yathā pācīnato āgacchantā pācīnadisāya uṭṭhānakaṃ bhaṇḍaṃ gahetvā pācīnadvārena nagarameva pavisanti, evaṃ kāyānupassanāmukhena āgacchantā cuddasavidhena kāyānupassanaṃ bhāvetvā kāyānupassanābhāvanānubhāvanibbattena ariyamaggena ekaṃ nibbānameva osaranti. Yathā dakkhiṇato āgacchantā dakkhiṇāya disāya uṭṭhānakaṃ bhaṇḍaṃ gahetvā dakkhiṇadvārena nagarameva pavisanti, evaṃ vedanānupassanāmukhena āgacchantā navavidhena vedanānupassanaṃ bhāvetvā vedanānupassanābhāvanānubhāvanibbattena ariyamaggena ekaṃ nibbānameva osaranti. Yathā pacchimato āgacchantā pacchimadisāya uṭṭhānakaṃ bhaṇḍaṃ gahetvā pacchimadvārena nagarameva pavisanti, evaṃ cittānupassanāmukhena āgacchantā soḷasavidhena cittānupassanaṃ bhāvetvā cittānupassanābhāvanānubhāvanibbattena ariyamaggena ekaṃ nibbānameva osaranti. Yathā uttarato āgacchantā uttaradisāya uṭṭhānakaṃ bhaṇḍaṃ gahetvā uttaradvārena nagarameva pavisanti, evaṃ dhammānupassanāmukhena āgacchantā pañcavidhena dhammānupassanaṃ bhāvetvā dhammānupassanābhāvanānubhāvanibbattena ariyamaggena ekaṃ nibbānameva osaranti. Evaṃ saraṇavasena ceva ekattasamosaraṇavasena ca ekameva satipaṭṭhānaṃ ārammaṇavasena cattārova vuttāti veditabbā.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Katame</hi><pb ed="V" n="2.0311"/><hi rend="bold">cattāro</hi>ti kathetukamyatā pucchā. <hi rend="bold">Idhā</hi>ti imasmiṃ sāsane. <hi rend="bold">Bhikkhave</hi>ti dhammapaṭiggāhakapuggalālapanametaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Bhikkhū</hi>ti paṭipattisampādakapuggalanidassanametaṃ. Aññepi ca devamanussā paṭipattiṃ sampādentiyeva, seṭṭhattā pana paṭipattiyā bhikkhubhāvadassanato ca ‘‘bhikkhū’’ti āha. Bhagavato hi anusāsaniṃ sampaṭicchantesu bhikkhu seṭṭho, sabbappakārāya anusāsaniyā bhājanabhāvato. Tasmā seṭṭhattā ‘‘bhikkhū’’ti <pb ed="P" n="3.0756"/> āha. Tasmiṃ gahite pana sesā gahitāva honti, rājagamanādīsu rājaggahaṇena sesaparisā viya. Yo ca imaṃ paṭipattiṃ paṭipajjati, so bhikkhu nāma hotīti paṭipattiyā bhikkhubhāvadassanatopi ‘‘bhikkhū’’ti āha. Paṭipannako hi devo vā hotu manusso vā, bhikkhūti saṅkhyaṃ gacchatiyeva yathāha –</p> <hi rend="bold">Katame</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0311"/> <hi rend="bold">cattāro</hi> <hi rend="bold">Idhā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Bhikkhave</hi> <hi rend="bold">Bhikkhū</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0756"/> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Alaṅkato <pb ed="M" n="2.0347"/> cepi samaṃ careyya,</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0347"/> <p rend="gatha2">Santo danto niyato brahmacārī;</p> <p rend="gatha3">Sabbesu bhūtesu nidhāya daṇḍaṃ,</p> <p rend="gathalast">So brāhmaṇo so samaṇo sa bhikkhū’’ti. (dha. pa. 142);</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Kāye</hi>ti rūpakāye. Rūpakāyo hi idha aṅgapaccaṅgānaṃ kesādīnañca dhammānaṃ samūhaṭṭhena hatthikāyarathakāyādayo viya kāyoti adhippeto. Yathā ca samūhaṭṭhena, evaṃ kucchitānaṃ āyaṭṭhena. Kucchitānañhi paramajegucchānaṃ so āyotipi kāyo. Āyoti uppattideso. Tatthāyaṃ vacanattho. Āyanti tatoti āyo. Ke āyanti? Kucchitā kesādayo. Iti kucchitānaṃ āyoti kāyo.</p> <hi rend="bold">Kāye</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Kāyānupassī</hi>ti kāye anupassanasīlo kāyaṃ vā anupassamāno. Kāyeti ca vatvāpi puna kāyānupassīti dutiyakāyaggahaṇaṃ asammissato vavatthānaghanavinibbhogādidassanatthaṃ katanti veditabbaṃ. Tena na kāye vedanānupassī vā, cittadhammānupassī vā, atha kho kāyānupassīyevāti kāyasaṅkhāte vatthusmiṃ kāyānupassanākārasseva dassanena asammissato vavatthānaṃ dassitaṃ hoti. Tathā na kāye aṅgapaccaṅgavinimuttaekadhammānupassī, nāpi kesalomādivinimuttaitthipurisānupassī, yopi cettha kesalomādiko bhūtupādāyasamūhasaṅkhāto kāyo, tatthapi na bhūtupādāyavinimuttaekadhammānupassī, atha kho rathasambhārānupassako viya aṅgapaccaṅgasamūhānupassī, nagarāvayavānupassako viya kesalomādisamūhānupassī, kadalikkhandhapattavaṭṭivinibbhujako viya rittamuṭṭhiviniveṭhako viya ca bhūtupādāyasamūhānupassīyevāti nānappakārato samūhavaseneva kāyasaṅkhātassa vatthuno dassanena ghanavinibbhogo <pb ed="V" n="2.0312"/> dassito hoti. Na hettha <pb ed="P" n="3.0757"/> yathāvuttasamūhavinimutto kāyo vā itthī vā puriso vā añño vā koci dhammo dissati, yathāvuttadhammasamūhamatteyeva pana tathā tathā sattā micchābhinivesaṃ karonti. Tenāhu porāṇā –</p> <hi rend="bold">Kāyānupassī</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0312"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0757"/> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Yaṃ passati na taṃ diṭṭhaṃ, yaṃ diṭṭhaṃ taṃ na passati;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Apassaṃ bajjhate mūḷho, bajjhamāno na muccatī’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Ghanavinibbhogādidassanatthanti <pb ed="M" n="2.0348"/> vuttaṃ, ādisaddena cettha ayampi attho veditabbo. Ayañhi etasmiṃ kāye kāyānupassīyeva, na añña dhammānupassīti vuttaṃ hoti. Yathā anudakabhūtāyapi marīciyā udakānupassino honti, na evaṃ aniccadukkhānattaasubhabhūteyeva imasmiṃ kāye niccasukhaattasubhabhāvānupassī, atha kho kāyānupassī aniccadukkhānattaasubhākārasamūhānupassīyevāti vuttaṃ hoti. Atha vā yvāyaṃ parato ‘‘idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu araññagato vā…pe… so satova assasatī’’tiādinā nayena assāsapassāsādicuṇṇikajātaaṭṭhikapariyosāno kāyo vutto, yo ca ‘‘idhekacco pathavīkāyaṃ aniccato anupassati, āpokāyaṃ tejokāyaṃ vāyokāyaṃ kesakāyaṃ lomakāyaṃ chavikāyaṃ cammakāyaṃ maṃsakāyaṃ rudhirakāyaṃ nhārukāyaṃ aṭṭhikāyaṃ aṭṭhimiñjakāya’’nti (paṭi. ma. 3.35) paṭisambhidāyaṃ kāyo vutto, tassa sabbassa imasmiññeva kāye anupassanato kāye kāyānupassīti evampi attho veditabbo.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0348"/> <p rend="bodytext">Atha vā kāye ahanti vā mamanti vā evaṃ gahetabbassa yassa kassaci ananupassanato, tassa tasseva pana kesalomādikassa nānādhammasamūhassa anupassanato kāye kesādidhammasamūhasaṅkhātakāyānupassīti evamattho daṭṭhabbo.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Apica ‘‘imasmiṃ kāye aniccato anupassati, no niccato’’tiādinā anukkamena paṭisambhidāyaṃ āgatanayassa sabbasseva aniccalakkhaṇādino ākārasamūhasaṅkhātassa kāyassa anupassanatopi kāye kāyānupassīti evampi attho daṭṭhabbo. Tathā <pb ed="P" n="3.0758"/> hi ayaṃ kāye kāyānupassanāpaṭipadaṃ paṭipanno bhikkhu imaṃ kāyaṃ aniccānupassanādīnaṃ sattannaṃ anupassanānaṃ vasena aniccato anupassati, no niccato. Dukkhato anupassati, no sukhato. Anattato anupassati, no attato. Nibbindati, no nandati, virajjati, no rajjati, nirodheti. No samudeti, paṭinissajjati, no ādiyati. So taṃ aniccato anupassanto niccasaññaṃ pajahati <pb ed="V" n="2.0313"/>, dukkhato anupassanto sukhasaññaṃ pajahati, anattato anupassanto attasaññaṃ pajahati, nibbindanto nandiṃ pajahati <pb ed="M" n="2.0349"/>, virajjanto rāgaṃ pajahati, nirodhento samudayaṃ pajahati, paṭinissajjanto ādānaṃ pajahatīti veditabbo.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0758"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0313"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0349"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Viharatī</hi>ti iriyati. <hi rend="bold">Ātāpī</hi>ti tīsu bhavesu kilese ātāpetīti ātāpo, vīriyassetaṃ nāmaṃ. Ātāpo assa atthīti ātāpī. <hi rend="bold">Sampajāno</hi>ti sampajaññasaṅkhātena ñāṇena samannāgato. <hi rend="bold">Satimā</hi>ti kāyapariggāhikāya satiyā samannāgato. Ayaṃ pana yasmā satiyā ārammaṇaṃ pariggahetvā paññāya anupassati, na hi sativirahitassa anupassanā nāma atthi, tenevāha – ‘‘satiñca khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, sabbatthikaṃ vadāmī’’ti (saṃ. ni. 5.234). Tasmā ettha ‘‘kāye kāyānupassī viharatī’’ti ettāvatā kāyānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ vuttaṃ hoti. Atha vā yasmā anātāpino antosaṅkhepo antarāyakaro hoti, asampajāno upāyapariggahe anupāyaparivajjane ca sammuyhati, muṭṭhassati upāyāpariccāge anupāyāpariggahe ca asamattho hoti, tenassa taṃ kammaṭṭhānaṃ na sampajjati. Tasmā yesaṃ dhammānaṃ ānubhāvena taṃ sampajjati, tesaṃ dassanatthaṃ ‘‘ātāpī sampajāno satimā’’ti idaṃ vuttanti veditabbaṃ.</p> <hi rend="bold">Viharatī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Ātāpī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Sampajāno</hi> <hi rend="bold">Satimā</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Iti kāyānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ sampayogaṅgañcassa dassetvā idāni pahānaṅgaṃ dassetuṃ <hi rend="bold">vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassa</hi>nti vuttaṃ. Tattha <hi rend="bold">vineyyā</hi>ti tadaṅgavinayena vā vikkhambhanavinayena vā vinayitvā. <hi rend="bold">Loke</hi>ti tasmiññeva kāye. Kāyo hi idha lujjanapalujjanaṭṭhena lokoti adhippeto. Yasmā panassa na kāyamatteyeva abhijjhādomanassaṃ pahīyati, vedanādīsupi pahīyatiyeva. Tasmā pañcapi upādānakkhandhā lokoti vibhaṅge vuttaṃ. Lokasaṅkhātattā <pb ed="P" n="3.0759"/> vā tesaṃ dhammānaṃ atthuddhāranayenetaṃ vuttaṃ. Yaṃ panāha – ‘‘tattha katamo loko? Sveva kāyo loko’’ti, ayamevettha attho. Tasmiṃ loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ vineyyāti evaṃ sambandho daṭṭhabbo. Yasmā panettha abhijjhāggahaṇena kāmacchando, domanassaggahaṇena byāpādo saṅgahaṃ gacchati, tasmā nīvaraṇapariyāpannabalavadhammadvayadassanena nīvaraṇappahānaṃ vuttaṃ hotīti veditabbaṃ.</p> <hi rend="bold">vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassa</hi> <hi rend="bold">vineyyā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Loke</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0759"/> <p rend="bodytext">Visesena <pb ed="M" n="2.0350"/> cettha abhijjhāvinayena kāyasampattimūlakassa anurodhassa, domanassavinayena kāyavipattimūlakassa virodhassa, abhijjhāvinayena ca kāye abhiratiyā, domanassavinayena kāyabhāvanāya anabhiratiyā, abhijjhāvinayena kāye abhūtānaṃ subhasukhabhāvādīnaṃ pakkhepassa, domanassavinayena <pb ed="V" n="2.0314"/> kāye bhūtānaṃ asubhāsukhabhāvādīnaṃ apanayanassa ca pahānaṃ vuttaṃ. Tena yogāvacarassa yogānubhāvo yogasamatthatā ca dīpitā hoti. Yogānubhāvo hi esa, yadidaṃ anurodhavirodhavippamutto aratiratisaho abhūtapakkhepabhūtāpanayanavirahito ca hoti. Anurodhavirodhavippamutto cesa aratiratisaho abhūtaṃ apakkhipanto bhūtañca anapanayanto yogasamattho hotīti.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0350"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0314"/> <p rend="bodytext">Aparo nayo ‘‘kāye kāyānupassī’’ti ettha anupassanāya kammaṭṭhānaṃ vuttaṃ. ‘‘Viharatī’’ti ettha vuttavihārena kammaṭṭhānikassa kāyapariharaṇaṃ, ‘‘ātāpī’’tiādīsu pana ātāpena sammappadhānaṃ, satisampajaññena sabbatthakakammaṭṭhānaṃ, kammaṭṭhānapariharaṇūpāyo vā. Satiyā vā kāyānupassanāvasena paṭiladdhasamatho, sampajaññena vipassanā abhijjhādomanassavinayena bhāvanābalaṃ vuttanti veditabbaṃ.</p> <p rend="indent">Vibhaṅge pana <hi rend="bold">anupassī</hi>ti tattha ‘‘katamā anupassanā? Yā paññā pajānanā vicayo pavicayo dhammavicayo sallakkhaṇā upalakkhaṇā paccupalakkhaṇā paṇḍiccaṃ kosallaṃ nepuññaṃ vebhabyā cintā upaparikkhā bhūrīmedhā pariṇāyikā vipassanā sampajaññaṃ patodo paññā paññindriyaṃ paññābalaṃ paññāsatthaṃ paññāpāsādo paññāāloko paññāobhāso paññāpajjoto paññāratanaṃ amoho dhammavicayo sammādiṭṭhi, ayaṃ vuccati anupassanā. Imāya anupassanāya upeto hoti samupeto upagato samupagato upapanno samannāgato, tena vuccati anupassīti. <hi rend="bold">Viharatī</hi>ti iriyati pavattati pāleti yapeti yāpeti carati viharati, tena vuccati viharatīti. <hi rend="bold">Ātāpī</hi>ti tattha katamaṃ ātāpaṃ? Yo cetasiko vīriyārambho nikammo parakkamo uyyāmo vāyāmo ussāho ussoḷhī thāmo dhiti asithilaparakkamatā anikkhittaddandatā anikkhittadhuratā dhurasampaggāhī vīriyaṃ vīriyindriyaṃ vīriyabalaṃ sammāvāyāmo, idaṃ vuccati ātāpaṃ. Iminā ātāpena upeto hoti…pe… samannāgato <pb ed="P" n="3.0760"/>, tena vuccati ātāpīti. <hi rend="bold">Sampajāno</hi>ti tattha katamaṃ sampajaññaṃ? Yā paññā pajānanā vicayo pavicayo dhammavicayo sallakkhaṇā upalakkhaṇā paccupalakkhaṇā paṇḍiccaṃ kosallaṃ nepuññaṃ vebhabyā cintā upaparikkhā bhūrīmedhā pariṇāyikā vipassanā sampajaññaṃ patodo paññā paññindriyaṃ paññābalaṃ paññāsatthaṃ paññāpāsādo paññāāloko paññāobhāso paññāpajjoto paññāratanaṃ amoho dhammavicayo sammādiṭṭhi, idaṃ vuccati sampajaññaṃ. Iminā sampajaññena upeto hoti <pb ed="V" n="2.0315"/> …pe… samannāgato, tena vuccati sampajānoti. <hi rend="bold">Satimā</hi>ti tattha katamā sati? Yā sati anussati paṭissati sati saraṇatā dhāraṇatā apilāpanatā asammusanatā sati satindriyaṃ satibalaṃ sammāsati <pb ed="M" n="2.0351"/>, ayaṃ vuccati sati. Imāya satiyā upeto hoti…pe… samannāgato, tena vuccati satimāti.</p> <hi rend="bold">anupassī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Viharatī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Ātāpī</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0760"/> <hi rend="bold">Sampajāno</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0315"/> <hi rend="bold">Satimā</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0351"/> <p rend="indent"><hi rend="bold">Vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassa</hi>nti tattha katamo loko? Sveva kāyo loko. Pañcapi upādānakkhandhā loko, ayaṃ vuccati loko. Tattha katamā abhijjhā? Yo rāgo sārāgo anunayo anurodho nandī nandirāgo cittassa sārāgo, ayaṃ vuccati abhijjhā. Tattha katamaṃ domanassaṃ? Yaṃ cetasikaṃ asātaṃ cetasikaṃ dukkhaṃ cetosamphassajā asātā dukkhā vedanā, idaṃ vuccati domanassaṃ. Iti ayañca abhijjhā, idañca domanassaṃ imamhi loke vinītā honti paṭivinītā santā samitā vūpasamitā atthaṅgatā abbhatthaṅgatā appitā byappitā sositā visositā byantīkatā, tena vuccati vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassa’’nti (vibha. 357-362).</p> <hi rend="bold">Vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassa</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Evametesaṃ padānaṃ attho vutto. Tena saha ayaṃ aṭṭhakathānayo yathā saṃsandati, evaṃ veditabbo. Ayaṃ tāva kāyānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānuddesassa atthavaṇṇanā.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Idāni vedanāsu. Citte. Dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati…pe… vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassanti ettha <hi rend="bold">vedanāsu vedanānupassī</hi>ti evamādīsu vedanādīnaṃ puna vacane payojanaṃ kāyānupassanāyaṃ vuttanayeneva veditabbaṃ. Vedanāsu vedanānupassī. Citte cittānupassī. Dhammesu dhammānupassīti ettha pana <hi rend="bold">vedanā</hi>ti tisso vedanā, tā ca lokiyā eva. Cittampi lokiyaṃ, tathā dhammā. Tesaṃ vibhāgo niddesavāre pākaṭo bhavissati. Kevalaṃ panidha yathā vedanā anupassitabbā, tathā tā anupassanto ‘‘vedanāsu vedanānupassī’’ti veditabbo. Esa nayo cittadhammesupi. Kathañca vedanā anupassitabbāti? Sukhā tāva vedanā dukkhato, dukkhā sallato, adukkhamasukhā aniccato. Yathāha –</p> <hi rend="bold">vedanāsu vedanānupassī</hi> <hi rend="bold">vedanā</hi> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Yo <pb ed="M" n="2.0352"/><pb ed="P" n="3.0761"/> sukhaṃ dukkhato adda, dukkhamaddakkhi sallato;</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0352"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0761"/> <p rend="gatha2">Adukkhamasukhaṃ santaṃ, addakkhi naṃ aniccato;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Sa ve sammaddaso bhikkhu, upasanto carissatī’’ti. (saṃ. ni. 4.253);</p> <p rend="bodytext">Sabbā <pb ed="V" n="2.0316"/> eva cetā ‘‘dukkhā’’tipi anupassitabbā. Vuttañhetaṃ – ‘‘yaṃ kiñci vedayitaṃ, taṃ dukkhasminti vadāmī’’ti (saṃ. ni. 4.259). Sukhadukkhatopi ca anupassitabbā. Yathāha ‘‘sukhā vedanā ṭhitisukhā vipariṇāmadukkhā’’ti (ma. ni. 1.465) sabbaṃ vitthāretabbaṃ. Apica aniccādisattaanupassanāvasenapi anupassitabbā. Sesaṃ niddesavāreyeva pākaṭaṃ bhavissati.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0316"/> <p rend="bodytext">Cittadhammesupi cittaṃ tāva ārammaṇādhipatisahajātabhūmikammavipākakiriyādinānattabhedānaṃ aniccādianupassanānaṃ niddesavāre āgatasarāgādibhedānañca vasena anupassitabbaṃ. Dhammā salakkhaṇasāmaññalakkhaṇānaṃ suññatadhammassa aniccādisattānupassanānaṃ niddesavāre āgatasantādibhedānañca vasena anupassitabbā. Sesaṃ vuttanayameva. Kāmañcettha yassa kāyasaṅkhāte loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ pahīnaṃ, tassa vedanādīsupi taṃ pahīnameva. Nānāpuggalavasena pana nānācittakkhaṇikasatipaṭṭhānabhāvanāvasena ca sabbattha vuttaṃ. Yato vā ekattha pahīnaṃ sesesupi pahīnaṃ hoti, tenevassa tattha pahānadassanatthampi etaṃ vuttanti veditabbanti.</p> <p rend="centre">Uddesavārakathā niṭṭhitā.</p> <p rend="subhead">Kāyānupassanā ānāpānapabbavaṇṇanā</p>
373. Thus have I heard: the *Mahāsatipaṭṭhāna Sutta*.

Here follows an explanation of the preliminary words: “*Ekāyano ayaṃ, bhikkhave, maggo*” – why did the Blessed One speak this sutta? Because the inhabitants of the Kuru country were capable of receiving profound teachings.

It is said that in the Kuru country, *bhikkhus*, *bhikkhunis*, *upāsakas*, and *upāsikas* were always healthy in body and mind due to the perfection of the climate and other conditions, and through their enjoyment of suitable seasonal conditions.

They were capable of receiving profound teachings, their minds and bodies being healthy, and having the strength of wisdom supported by [these conditions].

Therefore, the Blessed One, perceiving their ability to appreciate this profound instruction, proclaimed to them this *Mahāsatipaṭṭhāna Sutta*, which is deep in meaning, having set up the subject of meditation, in arahantship, in twenty-one places.

For even as a man, having got a golden basket should fill it with divers flowers, or indeed having got a golden casket should fill it with precious jewels of the seven kinds, the Blessed One, having got a following of the Kuru-land people, dispensed deep doctrine.

Thus, there are other profound meanings in this Dīgha Nikāya: the *Mahānidāna*; in the Majjhima Nikāya: the *Satipaṭṭhāna, Sāropama, Rukkhopama, Raṭṭhapāla, Māgaṇḍiya, Āneñjasappāya* and other suttas he taught.

Furthermore, in that territory, the four assemblies (monks, nuns, laymen and laywomen) naturally lived devoted to the practice of mindfulness, so that even servants and laborers spoke only in connection with mindfulness.

At wells or in spinning halls, useless talk was not heard.

If any woman, being asked, “My dear, which mindfulness practice do you cultivate?” were to say, “None,” they would rebuke her, saying, “Alas for your life! Even living, you are like the dead!”

Then, having admonished her, “Do not do this again,” they would teach her some mindfulness practice.

But if one were to say, “I cultivate such-and-such mindfulness practice,” having applauded her with “Good! Good!”, they would praise her thus: “Your life is well lived! You are one who has attained to the human state! For your sake the *Sammāsambuddha* has arisen!”

Not only humans, but also animals were skilled in the practice of mindfulness, living dependent on them.

Here is a story: A certain actor, having taken a young parrot, went about training it.

Having stayed near a convent for nuns, he left the young parrot behind when he departed.

The novices took it and cared for it.

They named it Buddharakkhita Tissa.

One day, seeing it sitting in front of her, the elder nun said, “Buddharakkhita!”

“What is it, venerable one?” “Do you have any mindfulness practice?” “No, venerable one.”

“Friend, it is not proper for one living near those who have gone forth to be without any particular attainment. One must desire some mindfulness practice. Since you are not able to do anything else, recite, ‘Bone, bone!’”

Having stood by the nun's instruction, he went about reciting, “Bone, bone!”

One day, as he was sitting at the gate early in the morning, warming himself in the early sunlight, a certain bird seized him with its claws.

He cried out, “Kiri, kiri!”

Having heard this, the novices said, “Venerable one, Buddharakkhita has been seized by a bird. Shall we release him?” Taking clods of earth and other things, they chased after [the bird] and released him.

Bringing him back and placing him in front of her, the elder nun said, “Buddharakkhita, what did you think when you were seized by the bird?” “Venerable one, I did not think anything else, but only that a mass of bones was taking a mass of bones, wondering in what place it would scatter them.”

“Good, good, Buddharakkhita! In the future, this will be a condition for the destruction of becoming for you!”

Thus, even animals there were skilled in mindfulness.

Therefore, the Blessed One spoke this sutta, only generating mindfulness in them.

There, *ekāyano* means the one way.
Indeed, concerning the path ( *maggassa hi* ) – the *ekāyana*.
The verse states: "The path ( *maggo* ), the way ( *pantho* ), the road ( *patho* ), the course ( *pajjo* ), straight ( *añjasaṃ* ), the cart path ( *vaṭumāyanaṃ* ); a boat ( *nāvā* ), a bridge for crossing over ( *uttarasetū* ), a raft ( *kullo* ), and a bridge made of *bhiṣa* grass ( *bhisisaṅkamo* )."

There are many words ( *bahūni nāmāni* ).

Here, it is spoken of by the name *ayana*. Therefore, "This is the *ekāyana*, O *bhikkhus*, the path ( *maggo* )" means here, "This is the single path ( *ekamaggo* ), O *bhikkhus*, the path ( *maggo* ), it is not of a twofold nature ( *na dvidhā pathabhūto* )"; thus, the meaning ( *attho* ) should be understood ( *daṭṭhabbo* ).

Or rather, because it is to be trodden ( *ayitabbo* ) by one person ( *ekena* ) alone, it is the *ekāyana*.

"By one person" means having abandoned the association with a crowd ( *gaṇasaṅgaṇikaṃ pahāya* ), with a mind that is withdrawn and solitary ( *vūpakaṭṭhena pavivittacittena* ).

It is to be trodden ( *ayitabbo* ) and practiced ( *paṭipajjitabbo* ). *Ayana* means that by which one goes ( *ayanti vā etenāti ayano* ), the meaning is that one goes to nirvāṇa from *saṃsāra* ( *saṃsārato nibbānaṃ gacchantīti attho* ).

*Ekassa ayano ekāyano*.

"*Ekassā* " means of the best ( *seṭṭhassa* ).

The Blessed One ( *bhagavā* ) is the best of all beings ( *sabbasattaseṭṭho ca bhagavā* ), therefore it is said "of the Blessed One" ( *tasmā bhagavatoti vuttaṃ hoti* ).

Even though others ( *aññepi* ) may go ( *ayanti* ) by it ( *tena* ), even so ( *evaṃ santepi* ) that *ayana* is of the Blessed One ( *bhagavatova so ayano* ) because it was produced by him ( *tena uppāditattā* ).

As it is said: "He, O *brāhmaṇa*, the Blessed One ( *bhagavā* ), is the producer ( *uppādetā* ) of the unarisen path ( *anuppannassa maggassa* )" etc. (Majjhima Nikāya 3.79).

*Ayatīti vā ayano*: *Ayana* means that by which one goes ( *gacchati pavattatīti attho* ).

*Ekasmiṃ ayanoti ekāyano*: *Ekāyana* means that it proceeds ( *pavattati* ) in this Doctrine and Discipline ( *imasmiññeva dhammavinaye* ), and not elsewhere ( *na aññatthāti vuttaṃ hoti* ).

As it is said: "In this Doctrine and Discipline ( *imasmiṃ kho, subhadda, dhammavinaye* ), O *Subhadda*, the Noble Eightfold Path ( *ariyo aṭṭhaṅgiko maggo* ) is found ( *upalabbhatī* )" (Dīgha Nikāya 2.214).

The distinction ( *bhedoyeva* ) is in the teaching ( *desanā* ), but in meaning ( *atthato* ) it is only one ( *ekova* ).

And further, *ekaṃ ayatīti ekāyano*: *Ekāyana* means that one goes to the one ( *ekaṃ ayatīti ekāyano* ).

Although in the earlier part ( *pubbabhāge* ) there is a progression ( *pavattopi* ) of various approaches ( *nānāmukhabhāvanānaya* ), in the later part ( *aparabhāge* ) it goes ( *gacchatīti* ) to the one nirvāṇa ( *ekaṃ nibbānameva* ).

As Brahmā Sahampati said:

"*Ekāyanaṃ* , seeing the end of birth ( *jātikhayantadassī* ), he knows the path ( *maggaṃ pajānāti* ), having compassion for welfare ( *hitānukampī* ); by this path ( *etena maggena* ) the former ones crossed over ( *tariṃsu pubbe* ), and those who will cross over ( *tarissanti* ), and those who are crossing over, the flood ( *oghanti* )" (Saṃyutta Nikāya 5.409).

But some say ( *keci pana* ), "Because one goes to nirvāṇa ( *nibbānaṃ gacchati* ) once ( *ekavāraṃ* ) by the method of the verse ( *gāthānayena* ) 'they do not go twice to the far shore' ( *na pāraṃ diguṇaṃ yantī* ), therefore they say it is *ekāyana*," but that is not fitting ( *taṃ na yujjati* ).

Indeed, it must be that "one goes once" ( *sakiṃ ayanoti* ) to the meaning ( *atthassa* ) of this ( *imassa* ) by this expression ( *iminā byañjanena bhavitabbaṃ* ).

But if one were to connect ( *yojetvā* ) the meaning ( *atthaṃ* ) thus ( *evaṃ* ), "for him there is one *ayana*, one course, one progression ( *ekaṃ ayanamassa ekā gati pavattīti* )," the expression ( *byañjanaṃ* ) would be fitting ( *yujjeyya* ), but the meaning ( *attho* ) is not fitting ( *na yujjati* ) in either way ( *ubhayathāpi* ).

Why ( *kasmā* )? Because here ( *idha* ) the earlier part of the path ( *pubbabhāgamaggassa* ) is intended ( *adhippetattā* ).

Indeed, the path of mindfulness, whose object is the body, etc., (*kāyādicatuārammaṇappavatto hi pubbabhāgasatipaṭṭhānamaggo*) is intended here (*idhādhippeto*), not the supramundane (*na lokuttaro*), and he goes by that (path) many times (*so ca anekavārampi ayati*), and he has many *ayanas* (*anekañcassa ayanaṃ hoti*).

And previously ( *pubbepi ca* ) in this passage ( *imasmiṃ pade* ) there was indeed ( *ahosiyeva* ) a discussion ( *sākacchā* ) of the great elders ( *mahātherānaṃ* ).

The Elder Tipiṭaka Cūḷanāga said ( *āha* ), "*pubbabhāgasatipaṭṭhānamaggo* " is the path of the earlier part (of practice) (*pubbabhāgasatipaṭṭhānamaggoti*).
His teacher, the Elder Tipiṭaka Cūḷasumma, said: “The Way is a mixed one.”

“Is it the preliminary part, venerable sir?”
“It is a mixed one, friend.”

When the teacher spoke again and again, without objecting, he remained silent.

They went away without coming to a decision.

Then, as the Venerable Teacher was going to the bathing-house, he reflected thus: “I have spoken of the mixed way, but Cūḷanāga uses the term ‘preliminary part of the way’. What, now, is the correct decision in this matter?” So, turning over in his mind the Suttanta from the beginning, he took note of the passage, “Indeed, monks, should anyone cultivate these four foundations of mindfulness in this way for seven years…”

There is no such thing as a supramundane path arising and lasting for seven years; the mixed way as spoken by me is not to be found.

Realizing that the preliminary part of the way as seen by Cūḷanāga is indeed to be found, on the eighth day, when the Dhamma was being proclaimed, he went to the assembly.

It seems that the Elders of old were fond of hearing the Dhamma, and as soon as they heard the sound, they would rush forward with one blow, saying, “I [will go] first, I [will go] first!”

And on that day, it was the turn of the Elder Cūḷanāga, so having sat down on the Dhamma seat, taking the fan, when the opening verses had been uttered, it occurred to the Elder, standing behind him near the seat: “I will not speak sitting in private.”

For the Elders of old were not envious.

They did not go about holding up only what they liked as though it were a bundle of sugar-cane. They took up what was rational; they gave up what was not.

Therefore, the Elder said, “Friend, Cūḷanāga.”

He heard the voice of his teacher and, pausing in his exposition of the Dhamma, replied: “What is it, Venerable Sir?”

"Friend, Cūḷanāga, the mixed way as spoken by me is not to be found; only the preliminary part of the Way of Mindfulness-arousing as spoken by you is to be found."

The Elder thought: “Our teacher is versed in all the texts, a master of the Tipiṭaka, a Buddha in learning; even such a bhikkhu as he is perplexed by this question. In the future, my brethren will be perplexed by this question, so taking a sutta, I will settle this question.” From the Paṭisambhidāmagga, [he quoted]: "The only way' is said to be the preliminary part of the Way of Mindfulness-arousing.”

"The Noble Eightfold Path is the best of paths; the four lines are the best of truths;

dispassion is the best of states; and the Seeing One is the best of bipeds.

This is the only path; there is no other for the purity of vision.

Enter upon this path, for it will confound the army of Māra.

Entering upon this path, you will make an end of suffering."

Having brought the sutta, he established it.

*Magga*: In what sense is it *magga* (path)? It is *magga* in the sense of going to *Nibbāna*, and in the sense that it should be sought by those who desire *Nibbāna*.

*Sattānaṃ visuddhiyā*: For the purification of beings whose minds are defiled by taints such as lust, and by corruptions such as covetousness and unrighteous greed.

For in this very *kappa*, beginning with the Buddhas named Taṇhaṅkara, Medhaṅkara, Saraṇaṅkara and Dīpaṅkara, who arose a hundred thousand *kappas* and more ago—above four *asaṅkhyeyyas*—and ending with Sākyamuni, many Perfect Sambuddhas, many hundreds of Paccekabuddhas, and countless Noble Disciples, all washed away the taint of mind and attained to supreme purity by means of this very path.

However, with regard to physical taints, there is no teaching of the cleansing of impurities.

For thus [it is said]:

“By a tainted form beings are tainted;

by a pure form they are purified’—it was not said by the Great Seer.

‘By a tainted mind beings are tainted;

by a pure mind they are purified’—so it was said by the Great Seer.”

As he said: “Beings are tainted, monks, by the taint of mind, and they are purified by the cleansing of mind.”

And that cleansing of mind comes about by means of this Way of Mindfulness-arousing.

Therefore, he said, "*sattānaṃ visuddhiyā*." (For the purification of beings.)

*Sokaparidevānaṃ samatikkamāya*: *sokassa ca paridevassa ca samatikkamāya pahānāya*—meaning, for the overcoming, for the abandoning of sorrow and lamentation. For this path, if cultivated, conduces to the overcoming of sorrow like that of Santati the Great Minister, and to the overcoming of lamentation like that of Paṭācārā and others.
Therefore, it is said, "for the overcoming of sorrow and lamentation."
Even though the *saṃtati-mahāmatta*—</p> <hi rend="bold">for the overcoming of sorrow and lamentation</hi> the *saṃtati-mahāmatta* Paṭācārā—</p> <p rend="gatha1">"What was before, purify that; afterwards, let there be nothing for your mother; </p> <p rend="gathalast">if you do not grasp in the middle, you will live calmly,"</p> (Su. Ni. 945);</p> <p rend="bodytext">having heard this verse, attained *arahatship* together with the discriminations.
Paṭācārā—</p> <p rend="gatha1">"There are no sons for protection, nor a father, nor even relatives;</p> <p rend="gathalast">for one overcome by the End-maker, there is no protection among relatives,"</p> (Dha. Pa. 288);</p> <p rend="bodytext">having heard this verse, she was established in the fruition of stream-entry ( *sotāpattiphala*).
Since, however, without laying hold on something whatsoever in material form, feeling, consciousness and mental objects (*kāyavedanācittadhammesu*), there is nothing called spiritual development (*bhāvanā*), therefore they too overcame sorrow and lamentation just by this Way of Mindfulness.
</p>
<p rend="bodytext">"*Dukkha-domanassānaṃ atthaṅgamāyā*": that is, for the cessation of bodily suffering (*kāyikadukkha*) and mental grief (*cetasikadomanassa*); the meaning is "for the cessation, for the stopping of these two."
For this Way, maintained by contemplation, is conducive to the destruction of suffering (*dukkha*) similar to that of the Elder Tissa, and of grief (*domanassa*) similar to that of Sakka.
</p> "*Dukkha-domanassānaṃ atthaṅgamāyā*"</p> <p rend="bodytext">Here, this is the explanation of the meaning: It is said that in Śrāvastī (*sāvatthī*) there was a householder's son named Tissa (*tisso*), who, having abandoned forty *hirañña-koṭis*, went forth and lived in a village-free forest.
His younger brother's wife sent five hundred robbers, saying, "Go, deprive him of his life!"
Having gone, they surrounded the elder and sat down.
The elder said, "Why have you come here, lay followers (*upāsakā*)?" "To deprive you of your life!"
"Lay followers, on a security, give me my life for just this one night!"
"What security will there be for you, *samaṇa*, in this place?" The elder, having taken a large stone, broke both his thigh bones, and said, "Lay followers, the security is valid!"
Having departed, they lit a fire at the head of the cloister and lay down.
The elder, having suppressed his pain, and contemplating on the purity of his virtue, joy and gladness arose.
Then, gradually developing insight (*vipassanā*), having performed the ascetic's duty (*samaṇadhamma*) during the three watches of the night, at the rising of the dawn he attained *arahatship*, and uttered this utterance (*udāna*):—</p> Tissa (*tisso*)
<p rend="gatha1">"Having broken both feet, I will make you understand;</p>
<p rend="gathalast">I am distressed, I am disgusted, by death with passion."</p>
<p rend="gatha1">"Thus I, having thought, rightly discerned;</p>
<p rend="gathalast">at the coming of the dawn, I attained *arahatship*,"</p>
<p rend="bodytext">Moreover, thirty monks, having taken a meditation subject (*kammaṭṭhāna*) in the presence of the Blessed One (*bhagavato*), having gone to a forest monastery for the rainy season, saying, "Friends (*āvuso*), the ascetic's duty (*samaṇadhamma*) alone must be done during the three watches of the night; one must not come into each other's presence," lived there.
While they, having performed the ascetic's duty (*samaṇadhamma*), were dozing at dawn, a tiger came, seized each monk, and went away.
Not one even uttered the words, "A tiger is seizing me!"
Thus, when five or ten monks had been eaten, on the *uposatha* day, having asked, "Friends (*āvuso*), where are the others?", and having learned, "Now, it should be said, 'I am seized by what has seized (others),'" they lived there.
Then a tiger seized a certain young monk in the same manner as before.
He said, "Tiger, Reverend Sir (*bhante*)!"
The monks, taking knives and torches, pursued it, thinking, "We will free him!"
The tiger, having ascended a broken bank inaccessible to the monks, began to eat that monk from the toe of his foot upwards.
The others said, "Now, good man (*sappurisa*), there is nothing to be done by us; the special spiritual attainment of monks is to be seen in such a place."
Just as he lay in the tiger's mouth, he suppressed that feeling, developed insight, and when his ankles were being eaten, he became a stream-enterer (sotāpanna). When his knees were being eaten, he became a once-returner (sakadāgāmī), when his navel was being eaten, he became a non-returner (anāgāmī), and before his heart was eaten, together with the discriminations, he attained arahantship and uttered this exalted utterance:

Thirty monks

“Virtuous, endowed with good conduct, wise, well-composed;
For a moment following heedlessness, his mind was obstructed by a tiger.

Having taken [me] in a cage, made [me] above a stone;
Let the tiger eat me at will, both bones and sinews;

I will destroy defilements, I will touch liberation.”

Another, named Pītamalla, as an elder, in his householder days, took a pennon in three kingdoms, came to Tambapaṇṇidīpa (Sri Lanka), saw the king, and having received royal favor, one day, while going through the gateway of the Kilañjaka market hall, heard the “Not yours” words, "Form, monks, is not yours; abandon it! That which you have abandoned will be for your welfare and happiness for a long time," and he thought, "Indeed, form is not mine, nor is feeling."

So, making just that thought a goad, he went forth, went to the Great Monastery (Mahāvihāra), asked for ordination, was ordained, received the higher ordination (upasampadā), mastered the Two Codes (dvemātikā), took thirty monks, went to the Gabalavāliya courtyard, and performed the ascetic's duty (samaṇadhammaṃ).

When his feet were moved, he walked with his knees.

One night a deer hunter, thinking he was a deer, struck him.

The spear pierced and went through; so he had that spear removed, filled the spear wound with grass, sat down on a stone slab, made space, developed insight (vipassanā), and together with the discriminations (paṭisambhidā), attained arahantship, and having explained [the situation] to the monks who came with a coughing sound, he uttered this exalted utterance:

"Spoken by the best of Buddhas, the proclaimer of the highest in all the world;

This form is not yours, monks, abandon it!

Impermanent, indeed, are conditioned things, subject to arising and passing away;

Having arisen, they cease; their stilling is bliss."

Thus, indeed, this path, like that of Tissa Thera and others, leads to the cessation of suffering.

Sakka, the king of the gods, having seen his fivefold former omens, terrified by the fear of death, with a mind born of sorrow, approached the Blessed One and asked a question.

At the end of the question and answer of equanimity (upekkhā), together with eighty thousand deities, he was established in the fruit of stream-entry (sotāpattiphala).

And that attainment of his was again natural.

Subrahma, too, a son of the gods, surrounded by a thousand nymphs, experienced heavenly prosperity.

There, five hundred nymphs, picking flowers from a tree, fell, having passed away, and were born in hell.

He, thinking, "Why are these delaying?", noticed their state of being reborn in hell, and knowing, "How much, now, is my life-span?", examining his own life-span's end, having known it, having passed away, having seen their state of rebirth in that very hell, frightened, with a mind born of excessive sorrow, thinking, "The Teacher will remove this sorrow of mine, not another," taking the remaining five hundred nymphs, approached the Blessed One and asked a question:

"Constantly fearful is this mind, constantly agitated is this heart;

In troubles not yet arisen, and also in those that have arisen;

If there is something un-fearful, tell me that when asked."

(Saṃ.
Ni.
1.
98);

Then the Blessed One said to him:

"Not elsewhere than through wisdom, not elsewhere than through restraint of the senses;

Not elsewhere than through the abandonment of all, do I see safety for living beings."

(Saṃ.
Ni.
1.
98);

He, at the end of the teaching, together with five hundred nymphs, having been established in the fruit of stream-entry (sotāpattiphala), making that prosperity permanent, went to the deva-world itself.

Thus, this path, cultivated, leads to the cessation of sorrow for those like Sakka and others, so it should be understood.

<hi rend="bold">For the attainment of rightness (ñāyassa adhigamāyā)</hi>: rightness (ñāyo) is said to be the Noble Eightfold Path (ariyo aṭṭhaṅgiko maggo); for its attainment, it is said to be "for its attainment, for its gaining".
Indeed, this mundane mindfulness path ( *smṛtyupasthāna-mārga* ), practiced in the earlier stage, leads to the supramundane path.

Therefore, he said, "For the attainment of rightness ( *nyāyādhigama* )."

"*Nirvāṇassa sacchikiriyāyā*": To the realization of *nirvāṇa*, which is called deathlessness (*amṛta*) because it is devoid of the flood of craving (*tṛṣṇā-vāna*), to the realization of deathlessness, it is said, is to experience it for oneself (*āttapratyakṣatā*).

Indeed, this path, when cultivated gradually, effects the realization of *nirvāṇa* ( *nirvāṇasākṣātkriyā*).

Therefore, he said, "To the realization of *nirvāṇa*."

*Nyāyassa adhigamāyā*

*Nirvāṇassa sacchikiriyāyā*

There, although when "for the purification of beings (*sattānām viśuddhiyā*)" is said, the overcoming of sorrow and lamentation etc. are indeed accomplished in meaning, setting aside those skilled in the teaching, they are not obvious to others, and the Blessed One does not first make people skilled in the teaching and afterwards teach the Dharma.

But he makes that meaning known by that very discourse.

Therefore, showing what thing the one way (*ekāyanamārga*) accomplishes, making that thing manifest, he said, "For the overcoming of sorrow and lamentation (*śokaparidevānām samatikkamāya*)," and so forth.

Or, because whatever purification of beings comes about through the one way, that comes about through the overcoming of sorrow and lamentation.

The overcoming of sorrow and lamentation comes about through the cessation of suffering and grief (*duḥkhadaurmanasyānām asthaṅgamena*), the cessation of suffering and grief comes about through the attainment of rightness (*nyāyādhigamena*), the attainment of rightness is through the realization of *nirvāṇa*.

Therefore, showing even this order, having said "for the purification of beings (*sattānām viśuddhiyā*)," he said "for the overcoming of sorrow and lamentation (*śokaparidevānām samatikkamāya*)," and so forth.

Moreover, this is an expression of praise for the one way.

Just as the Blessed One – "Monks, I will teach you the Dhamma admirable in the beginning, admirable in the middle, admirable in the end; I will expound the holy life both in its particulars & in its essence, entirely complete, surpassingly pure — in other words, the six sextets" (Ma.

Ni.

3.
420) – by way of eight characteristics expressed praise in the Cha Chakka Sutta.

Just as in the Ariyavamsa Sutta, "These four, monks, are ancestral lines of the noble ones: they are primeval, long-standing, traditional, unadulterated, unadulterated from the past, uncriticized, and unrejected by wise contemplatives & brahmans" (A.

Ni.

4.
28) – by way of nine characteristics expressed praise; just so, of this one way, by way of seven characteristics beginning with "for the purification of beings (*sattānām viśuddhiyā*)" he expressed praise.

And why is that? For the purpose of generating enthusiasm in those monks.

Having heard the utterance of praise, those monks will think, "Indeed, this path casts out the four onrushings – namely sorrow produced by distress of heart (*hṛdayasantāpabhūtaṃ śokaṃ*), lamentation characterized by confused talk (*vācāvippalāpabhūtaṃ paridevaṃ*), suffering produced by disagreeable bodily feeling (*kāyikaasātabhūtaṃ duḥkhaṃ*), and grief produced by disagreeable thought (*cetasikaasātabhūtaṃ daurmanasya*) – and it brings the three special qualities – namely purification (*viśuddhi*), rightness (*nyāya*), and *nirvāṇa*," they will think, “This teaching should be learned, mastered, memorized, recited, and this path should be cultivated.”

Thus, for the purpose of generating enthusiasm in those monks, he expressed praise.

Like the praise of cloths by cloth merchants, and so forth.

Just as, even when a cloth merchant who deals in cloths worth hundreds of thousands says, "Take the cloth!", people do not yet know "which cloth" it is.

Even hair-cloths, rough to the touch and foul-smelling, are called "cloths."

But when he says, "This is a Gandhāra cloth, a red cloth, fine, bright, and pleasant to the touch," then those who can afford it, take it.

Those who cannot afford it, even they want to see it; just so, even when it is said, "Monks, this is the one way," it is not yet obvious "which path" it is.

Indeed, the various non-leading paths are also called paths.

But when "for the purification of beings (*sattānām viśuddhiyā*)," and so forth is said, thinking, "Indeed, this path casts out the four onrushings, and it brings the three special qualities," generating enthusiasm, praising this teaching, saying, "This teaching should be learned, mastered, memorized, recited, and this path should be cultivated," he said "for the purification of beings (*sattānām viśuddhiyā*)," and so forth.

Just as the simile of a cloth merchant who deals in cloths worth hundreds of thousands; so too, similes of merchants who deal in red *jambunada* gold, water-cleansing jewels, gems, very pure pearls, jewels, coral, and so forth, should be brought here.

The word "*yadida*" is a particle; its meaning is: "which is this ( *ye ime*)."
*Cattāro* means a determination of number.

Therefore, it does not reveal a *satipaṭṭhāna*-determination less than that or exceeding that.

*Satipaṭṭhānā* means three *satipaṭṭhānas*: the range of mindfulness, the absence of aversion and compliance on the part of the teacher towards the disciples who have practiced in three ways, and mindfulness itself.

"I will teach the arising and passing away of the four *satipaṭṭhānas*, listen to that...pe... and what, monks, is the arising of the body?

The arising of nutriment is the arising of the body," etc. (Saṃ.

Ni.

5.

408) For hi *satigocaro satipaṭṭhānaṃ* is said.

Similarly, in "the body is the establishment, but it is not mindfulness; mindfulness, however, is both the establishment and mindfulness," etc. (Paṭi.

Ma.

3.

35).

Its meaning is - *paṭṭhānaṃ* is so called because it is established in this.

What is established? Mindfulness.

*Satipaṭṭhānaṃ* is mindfulness's establishment, or *paṭṭhānaṃ* is the state of exertion.

Mindfulness's establishment is *satipaṭṭhānaṃ*, like *hatthiṭṭhāna*, *assaṭṭhāna*, etc.

</p> *yadida cattāro satipaṭṭhānā*

</p> *satipaṭṭhānaṃ*,

"The three *satipaṭṭhānas* that the noble one cultivates, the teacher who cultivates the three *satipaṭṭhānas* is worthy to instruct the assembly" (Ma.

Ni.

3.

311) Here, the absence of aversion and compliance on the part of the teacher towards the disciples who have practiced in three ways is said to be "*satipaṭṭhāna*".

Its meaning is - *paṭṭhānaṃ* is so called because it is to be established, the meaning is that it is to be set in motion.

By what is it to be established? By mindfulness (*satiyā*).

Mindfulness’s establishment is *satipaṭṭhānaṃ*.

In “the four *satipaṭṭhāna*s, when developed and cultivated, bring the seven awakening-factors to fulfillment,” etc. (Ma.

Ni.

3.

147) however, mindfulness itself is called “*satipaṭṭhānaṃ*.”

Its meaning is—*paṭṭhānaṃ* is so called because it is established; *upaṭṭhāti* means it is produced by surging forward, leaping forward, spreading out and proceeding.

Mindfulness itself is *satipaṭṭhānaṃ*.

Or alternatively, mindfulness is *sati* in the sense of refuge, and *paṭṭhānaṃ* in the sense of establishment.

Thus, mindfulness and that establishment together are *satipaṭṭhānaṃ*.

This is what is intended here.

</p> If that is so, why is it " *satipaṭṭhānā* " in the plural? Because of the abundance of mindfulness.
Indeed, these mindfulnesses are numerous due to the variety of objects (*ārammaṇa*).
Then, why is *magga* (path) in the singular? Because of the oneness of the meaning of path (*maggaṭṭhena ekattā*).
Indeed, these four mindfulnesses also go to oneness by way of the meaning of path.

It was said, "By what meaning is it the path (*maggo*)? By the meaning of going to nirvāṇa (*nibbānagamanaṭṭhena*).
And by the meaning of being sought by those who desire nirvāṇa (*nibbānatthikehi magganīyaṭṭhena*)."
These four, accomplishing their function in the respective objects beginning with the body, go to nirvāṇa in the final part, and from the beginning they are sought by those desiring nirvāṇa; therefore, the four are called one path (*eko maggo*).
And when this is so, the teaching is coherent with the connection of words (*vacanānusandhinā sānusandhikāva desanā hoti*), as in "I will teach you, monks, the destruction of Māra's army (*mārasenappamaddanaṃ*), listen to that...pe...and what, monks, is the destruction of Māra's army? That is, the seven factors of enlightenment (*satta bojjhaṅgā*)” etc. (Saṃ.
Ni.
5.
224) as it were.
Just as "destruction of Māra's army" and "seven factors of enlightenment" are one in meaning, but the expression (*byañjana*) is different here.

Likewise, "the one path (*ekāyanamaggo*)" and "the four foundations of mindfulness (*cattāro satipaṭṭhānā*)" are one in meaning, but the expression is different here, therefore, due to the oneness of the meaning of path, it is singular.
Due to the multiplicity of mindfulnesses by way of the variety of objects, the plural number should be understood.

Why, however, were only four foundations of mindfulness spoken of by the Blessed One, neither deficient nor excessive? Because of [their] benefit to those who are to be trained (*veneyyahitattā*).
Indeed, among those to be trained who are proceeding in two ways, twofoldly, by way of dullness and sharpness, those who are of craving-character, those who are of view-character, those who have tranquility as their vehicle, and those who have insight as their vehicle, for the dull one of craving-character, the gross mindfulness of contemplation of the body (*kāyānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ*) is the path of purification (*visuddhimaggo*), for the sharp one, the subtle mindfulness of contemplation of feeling (*vedanānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ*).
For the dull one of view-character also, the mindfulness of contemplation of consciousness (*cittānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ*) not connected to excessive diversity is the path of purification, for the sharp one, the mindfulness of contemplation of phenomena (*dhammānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ*) connected to excessive diversity is the path of purification.
For the dull one of tranquility-vehicle, the first mindfulness of contemplation of body (*paṭhamaṃ satipaṭṭhānaṃ*) with easily attainable signs is the path of purification, for the sharp one, the second [mindfulness of contemplation of feeling] due to not persisting in a gross object (*oḷārikārammaṇe asaṇṭhahanato dutiyaṃ*).
For the dull one of insight-vehicle also, the third [mindfulness of contemplation of consciousness] with objects not connected to excessive diversity (*nātippabhedagatārammaṇaṃ tatiyaṃ*), for the sharp one, the fourth [mindfulness of contemplation of phenomena] with objects connected to excessive diversity (*atippabhedagatārammaṇaṃ catutthaṃ*).
Thus, only four were spoken of, neither deficient nor excessive.

Or for the purpose of abandoning the perversions regarding the aspects of beauty, pleasure, permanence, and self (*subhasukhaniccaattabhāvavipallāsappahānatthaṃ*).
Indeed, the body (*kāya*) is impure (*asubho*), and there beings are with perverted perceptions of beauty (*subhavipallāsavipallatthā sattā*).
For them, by showing the impurity of that there, the first mindfulness of contemplation of the body (*paṭhamaṃ satipaṭṭhānaṃ*) was spoken of for the purpose of abandoning that perversion (*vipallāsassa pahānatthaṃ*).
And among feelings etc. which are grasped as pleasure, permanence, and self, feeling (*vedanā*) is suffering (*dukkha*), consciousness (*citta*) is impermanent (*anicca*), phenomena (*dhammā*) are without self (*anattā*), and there beings are with perverted perceptions of pleasure, permanence, and self.
For them, by showing the nature of suffering etc. there, the remaining three were spoken of for the purpose of abandoning those perversions; thus, or for the purpose of abandoning the perversions regarding the aspects of beauty, pleasure, permanence, and self, only four were spoken of, neither deficient nor excessive, it should be understood.
And not only for the purpose of abandoning the perversions, but also for the purpose of abandoning the floods (*ogha*), yokes (*yoga*), corruptions (*āsava*), bonds (*gantha*), grasping (*upādāna*), and bad destinies (*agati*), and for the purpose of fully knowing the four kinds of nutriment (*catubbidhāhāra*), only four were spoken of, it should be understood.
This is the method of exposition.

In the commentary (*aṭṭhakathāyaṃ*) however, it was said that only one mindfulness (*ekameva satipaṭṭhānaṃ*) by way of remembering (*saraṇavasena*) and by way of meeting in one thing (*ekattasamosaraṇavasena*), and four by way of the object (*ārammaṇavasena*).
Just as in a city with four gates, those coming from the east, taking goods originating in the eastern direction enter the city through the eastern gate, and those from the south,
from the west,
those coming from the north, taking goods originating in the northern direction enter the city through the northern gate; thus - this accomplishment should be understood.
Like a city, indeed, is the great city of nirvāṇa (*nibbānamahānagaraṃ*), like a gate is the eightfold supramundane path (*aṭṭhaṅgiko lokuttaramaggo*), like the eastern direction etc. are the body etc..
Like those coming from the East, taking goods produced in the east and entering the city through the east gate, so too those coming by way of mindfulness of the body, cultivating mindfulness of the body in fourteen ways, descend to a single *nirvāṇa* by means of the Noble Path produced by the power of cultivating mindfulness of the body.
