Kammaṭṭhānakathā pana visuddhimagge vitthāritā.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0361"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0771"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi>ti evaṃ catudhātupariggaṇhanena attano vā kāye, parassa vā kāye, kālena vā attano, kālena vā parassa kāye kāyānupassī viharati. Ito paraṃ vuttanayameva. Kevalañhi idha catudhātupariggāhikā sati dukkhasaccanti evaṃ yojanaṃ katvā niyyānamukhaṃ veditabbaṃ, sesaṃ purimasadisamevāti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi> <p rend="centre">Dhātumanasikārapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.</p> <p rend="subhead">Navasivathikapabbavaṇṇanā</p>
378. Thus, having divided the contemplation of the body as unattractive by way of unpleasantness, now, in order to divide by way of contemplation on the elements, he says, "Again, further" and so on.

In this connection, the meaning is elaborated together with the application of a simile: Just as if some cow-butcher, or an apprentice of that very butcher who works for his keep, having killed a cow, and having thoroughly cut it up, were sitting having made portions, portion by portion, at a four-way crossroads, known as the intersection of the main roads going in the four directions; just so, a *bhikṣu* reviews the body, however it stands because of some posture among the four postures, just as it is, and just as it is disposed, thinking thus: "There are, in this body, the earth element (*pṛthivīdhātu*)... and so on... the air element (*vāyodhātu*)."

What is said is this: just as the perception "cow" does not disappear from a cow-butcher while he is feeding the cow, while he is bringing it to the slaughtering place, while he is tying it there, while he is killing it, while he is seeing the killed, dead cow, just so, until he cuts it open and divides it into portions.

But for him sitting having divided it up, the perception "cow" disappears, and the perception "meat" comes into being.

He does not think, "I am selling the cow, these people are carrying away the cow."

But rather, it occurs to him, "I am selling meat; these people are carrying away meat." Just so, for this *bhikṣu* also, formerly in his time as a foolish ordinary person, even as a householder, even as one gone forth [into homelessness], just so long the perception of a being (*sattva*) or a person (*pudgala*) does not disappear, until, having made this very body, just as it stands, just as it is disposed, into a non-compact aggregate, he reviews it by way of the elements (*dhātu*).

But for him who reviews by way of the elements (*dhātu*), the perception of a being (*sattva-saṃjñā*) disappears, and the mind gets established by way of the elements (*dhātu*).

Therefore, the Blessed One (*bhagavān*) said: “'He reviews this very body, just as it stands, just as it is disposed, by way of the elements (*dhātu*), [thinking,] “There are in this body the earth element (*pṛthivīdhātu*), the water element (*āpodhātu*), the fire element (*tejodhātu*), and the air element (*vāyodhātu*)’”.

Just as, *bhikṣus*, a skilled cow-butcher... and so on... the air element (*vāyodhātu*)’."

Indeed, the *yogī* is like a cow-butcher, the perception "cow" is like the perception "being" (*sattva-saṃjñā*), the four-way crossroads is like the four postures, the state of sitting having divided it into portions is like reviewing by way of the elements (*dhātu*). This is the explanation of the passage here.

The talk about the meditation subject (*karmaṭṭhāna-kathā*), however, is extensively explained in the *Visuddhimagga*.

Thus, internally. Thus, by means of the fourfold laying hold of the elements (*dhātu*), the *kāyānupassī* abides contemplating the body either in his own body (*kāya*) or in another’s body (*kāya*), or at one time in his own, and at another time in another’s body.

From here on, the exposition is just according to the method already stated.

Indeed, here, mindfulness (*smṛti*) which lays hold of the four elements (*dhātu*) is the Truth of Suffering (*duḥkhasatya*). Thus, having made the connection, the portal to deliverance (*niryyāṇamukha*) should be known. The rest is just similar to what has already been said.

Thus, internally.

The section on contemplation on the elements (*dhātumanasikārapabba*) is finished.

Explanation of the section on the nine charnel grounds (*navasivathikapabbavaṇṇanā*).
379
<p n="379" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">379</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Puna <pb ed="M" n="2.0235"/> caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu seyyathāpi passeyya sarīraṃ sivathikāya chaḍḍitaṃ ekāhamataṃ vā dvīhamataṃ vā tīhamataṃ vā uddhumātakaṃ vinīlakaṃ vipubbakajātaṃ. So imameva kāyaṃ upasaṃharati – ‘ayampi kho kāyo evaṃdhammo evaṃbhāvī evaṃanatīto’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Iti ajjhattaṃ vā <pb ed="T" n="2.0331"/> …pe… evampi kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāye kāyānupassī viharati.</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0331"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu seyyathāpi passeyya sarīraṃ sivathikāya chaḍḍitaṃ kākehi vā <pb ed="V" n="2.0219"/> khajjamānaṃ kulalehi vā khajjamānaṃ gijjhehi vā khajjamānaṃ kaṅkehi vā khajjamānaṃ sunakhehi vā khajjamānaṃ byagghehi vā khajjamānaṃ dīpīhi vā khajjamānaṃ siṅgālehi vā <note>gijjhehi vā khajjamānaṃ, suvānehi vā khajjamānaṃ, sigālehi vā khajjamānaṃ, (syā. pī.)</note> khajjamānaṃ vividhehi vā pāṇakajātehi khajjamānaṃ. So imameva kāyaṃ upasaṃharati – ‘ayampi kho kāyo evaṃdhammo evaṃbhāvī evaṃanatīto’ti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0219"/> <note>gijjhehi vā khajjamānaṃ, suvānehi vā khajjamānaṃ, sigālehi vā khajjamānaṃ, (syā. pī.)</note> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Iti <pb ed="P" n="2.0296"/> ajjhattaṃ vā…pe… evampi kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāye kāyānupassī viharati.</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0296"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu seyyathāpi passeyya sarīraṃ sivathikāya chaḍḍitaṃ aṭṭhikasaṅkhalikaṃ samaṃsalohitaṃ nhārusambandhaṃ…pe… aṭṭhikasaṅkhalikaṃ nimaṃsalohitamakkhitaṃ nhārusambandhaṃ…pe… aṭṭhikasaṅkhalikaṃ apagatamaṃsalohitaṃ nhārusambandhaṃ…pe… aṭṭhikāni apagatasambandhāni <note>apagatanhārusambandhāni (syā.)</note> disā vidisā vikkhittāni, aññena hatthaṭṭhikaṃ aññena pādaṭṭhikaṃ aññena gopphakaṭṭhikaṃ <note>‘‘aññena gopphakaṭṭhika’’nti idaṃ sī. syā. pī. potthakesu natthi</note> aññena jaṅghaṭṭhikaṃ aññena ūruṭṭhikaṃ aññena kaṭiṭṭhikaṃ <note>aññena kaṭaṭṭhikaṃ aññena piṭṭhaṭṭhikaṃ aññena kaṇḍakaṭṭhikaṃ aññena phāsukaṭṭhikaṃ aññena uraṭṭhikaṃ aññena aṃsaṭṭhikaṃ aññena bāhuṭṭhikaṃ (syā.)</note> aññena phāsukaṭṭhikaṃ aññena <pb ed="P" n="2.0297"/> piṭṭhiṭṭhikaṃ aññena khandhaṭṭhikaṃ <note>aññena kaṭaṭṭhikaṃ aññena piṭṭhaṭṭhikaṃ aññena kaṇḍakaṭṭhikaṃ aññena phāsukaṭṭhikaṃ aññena uraṭṭhikaṃ aññena aṃsaṭṭhikaṃ aññena bāhuṭṭhikaṃ (syā.)</note> aññena gīvaṭṭhikaṃ aññena hanukaṭṭhikaṃ aññena dantaṭṭhikaṃ aññena sīsakaṭāhaṃ. So imameva kāyaṃ upasaṃharati – ‘ayampi kho kāyo evaṃdhammo evaṃbhāvī evaṃanatīto’ti.</p> <note>apagatanhārusambandhāni (syā.)</note> <note>‘‘aññena gopphakaṭṭhika’’nti idaṃ sī. syā. pī. potthakesu natthi</note> <note>aññena kaṭaṭṭhikaṃ aññena piṭṭhaṭṭhikaṃ aññena kaṇḍakaṭṭhikaṃ aññena phāsukaṭṭhikaṃ aññena uraṭṭhikaṃ aññena aṃsaṭṭhikaṃ aññena bāhuṭṭhikaṃ (syā.)</note> <pb ed="P" n="2.0297"/> <note>aññena kaṭaṭṭhikaṃ aññena piṭṭhaṭṭhikaṃ aññena kaṇḍakaṭṭhikaṃ aññena phāsukaṭṭhikaṃ aññena uraṭṭhikaṃ aññena aṃsaṭṭhikaṃ aññena bāhuṭṭhikaṃ (syā.)</note> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Iti ajjhattaṃ vā <pb ed="T" n="2.0332"/> …pe… viharati.</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0332"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Puna <pb ed="M" n="2.0236"/> caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu seyyathāpi passeyya sarīraṃ sivathikāya chaḍḍitaṃ aṭṭhikāni setāni saṅkhavaṇṇapaṭibhāgāni…pe… aṭṭhikāni puñjakitāni terovassikāni <pb ed="V" n="2.0220"/> …pe… aṭṭhikāni pūtīni cuṇṇakajātāni. So imameva kāyaṃ upasaṃharati – ‘ayampi kho kāyo evaṃdhammo evaṃbhāvī evaṃanatīto’ti. Iti ajjhattaṃ vā kāye kāyānupassī viharati, bahiddhā vā kāye kāyānupassī viharati, ajjhattabahiddhā vā kāye kāyānupassī <pb ed="P" n="2.0298"/> viharati. Samudayadhammānupassī vā kāyasmiṃ viharati, vayadhammānupassī vā kāyasmiṃ viharati, samudayavayadhammānupassī vā kāyasmiṃ viharati. ‘Atthi kāyo’ti vā panassa sati paccupaṭṭhitā hoti yāvadeva ñāṇamattāya paṭissatimattāya anissito ca viharati, na ca kiñci loke upādiyati. Evampi kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāye kāyānupassī viharati.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0236"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0220"/> <pb ed="P" n="2.0298"/> <p rend="centre">Navasivathikapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.</p> <p rend="centre">Cuddasa kāyānupassanā niṭṭhitā.</p> <p rend="subhead">Vedanānupassanā</p>
379. “Again, monks, as if a monk were to see a corpse cast away in a charnel ground—one day dead, two days dead, three days dead—bloated, livid, and festering. He compares this body with that, thinking: ‘This body is of the same nature, it will become like that, it is not exempt from that fate.’

“Thus, either internally… and so forth… That too, monks, is how a monk abides contemplating the body as a body.

“Again, monks, as if a monk were to see a corpse cast away in a charnel ground, being devoured by crows, being devoured by hawks, being devoured by vultures, being devoured by dogs, being devoured by hyenas, being devoured by jackals, or being devoured by various kinds of creatures. He compares this body with that, thinking: ‘This body is of the same nature, it will become like that, it is not exempt from that fate.’

“Thus, either internally… and so forth… That too, monks, is how a monk abides contemplating the body as a body.

“Again, monks, as if a monk were to see a corpse cast away in a charnel ground, a skeleton with flesh and blood, connected by tendons… and so forth… a skeleton smeared with blood, devoid of flesh, connected by tendons… and so forth… a skeleton without flesh or blood, connected by tendons… and so forth… bones disconnected, scattered in all directions—here a hand-bone, there a foot-bone, here an ankle-bone, here a shin-bone, there a thigh-bone, here a hip-bone, here a rib, here a back-bone, here a shoulder-bone, here a neck-bone, here a jaw-bone, here a tooth, here a skull. He compares this body with that, thinking: ‘This body is of the same nature, it will become like that, it is not exempt from that fate.’

“Thus, either internally… and so forth… he abides.

“Again, monks, as if a monk were to see a corpse cast away in a charnel ground, bones bleached white, the color of shells… and so forth… bones heaped up, more than a year old… and so forth… bones rotten and crumbled to dust.
He compares this very body with that, thinking: “This body too is of such a nature (evaṃdhammo), it will become like that (evaṃbhāvī), it is not exempt from that fate (evaṃanatīto)."

In this way, he abides contemplating the body as a body internally, or he abides contemplating the body as a body externally, or he abides contemplating the body as a body both internally and externally.

Or he abides contemplating the arising nature (samudayadhammānupassī) in the body, or he abides contemplating the dissolving nature (vayadhammānupassī) in the body, or he abides contemplating the arising and dissolving nature (samudayavayadhammānupassī) in the body.

Or else, mindfulness that ‘there is a body (atthi kāyo)’ is present to him just to the extent necessary for knowledge (ñāṇamattāya) and remembrance (paṭissatimattāya). And he abides independent (anissito), not clinging to anything in the world (na ca kiñci loke upādiyati).

That is how, monks (bhikkhave), a monk (bhikkhu) abides contemplating the body as a body (kāye kāyānupassī viharati).

The chapter on the nine charnel grounds is finished.

The fourteen contemplations of the body are finished.

Contemplation of feeling.
<p n="379" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">379</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Evaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0325"/> dhātumanasikāravasena kāyānupassanaṃ vibhajitvā idāni navahi sivathikapabbehi vibhajituṃ <hi rend="bold">puna capara</hi>ntiādimāha. Tattha <hi rend="bold">seyyathāpi passeyyā</hi>ti yathā passeyya. <hi rend="bold">Sarīra</hi>nti matasarīraṃ. <hi rend="bold">Sivathikāya chaḍḍita</hi>nti susāne apaviddhaṃ. Ekāhaṃ matassa assāti <hi rend="bold">ekāhamataṃ.</hi> Dvīhaṃ matassa assāti <hi rend="bold">dvīhamataṃ.</hi> Tīhaṃ matassa assāti <hi rend="bold">tīhamataṃ.</hi> Kammārabhastā viya vāyunā uddhaṃ jīvitapariyādānā yathānukkamaṃ samuggatena sūnabhāvena uddhumātattā uddhumātaṃ, uddhumātameva <hi rend="bold">uddhumātakaṃ.</hi> Paṭikūlattā vā kucchitaṃ uddhumātanti uddhumātakaṃ. Vinīlaṃ vuccati <pb ed="M" n="2.0362"/> viparibhinnavaṇṇaṃ, vinīlameva <hi rend="bold">vinīlakaṃ.</hi> Paṭikūlattā vā kucchitaṃ vinīlanti vinīlakaṃ. Maṃsussadaṭṭhānesu rattavaṇṇassa pubbasannicayaṭṭhānesu setavaṇṇassa yebhuyyena ca nīlavaṇṇassa nīlaṭṭhānesu nīlasāṭakapārutasseva chavasarīrassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Paribhinnaṭṭhānehi navahi vā vaṇamukhehi vissandamānapubbaṃ vipubbaṃ, vipubbameva vipubbakaṃ. Paṭikūlattā vā kucchitaṃ vipubbanti vipubbakaṃ. Vipubbakaṃ jātaṃ tathābhāvaṃ gatanti <hi rend="bold">vipubbakajātaṃ.</hi></p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">So imameva kāya</hi>nti so bhikkhu imaṃ attano kāyaṃ tena kāyena saddhiṃ ñāṇena upasaṃharati upaneti. Kathaṃ? <hi rend="bold">Ayampi kho kāyo evaṃdhammo evaṃbhāvī evaṃanatīto</hi>ti. Idaṃ vuttaṃ hoti – āyu, usmā, viññāṇanti imesaṃ tiṇṇaṃ dhammānaṃ atthitāya ayaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0772"/> kāyo ṭhānagamanādikhamo hoti, imesaṃ pana vigamā ayampi kho kāyo evaṃdhammo evaṃ pūtikasabhāvoyeva, <hi rend="bold">evaṃbhāvī</hi> evaṃ uddhumātādibhedo bhavissati, <hi rend="bold">evaṃanatīto</hi> evaṃ uddhumātādibhāvaṃ anatikkantoti. <hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi>ti evaṃ uddhumātādipariggaṇhanena attano vā kāye, parassa vā kāye, kālena vā attano, kālena vā parassa kāye kāyānupassī viharati.</p> <hi rend="bold">So imameva kāya</hi> <hi rend="bold">Ayampi kho kāyo evaṃdhammo evaṃbhāvī evaṃanatīto</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0772"/> <hi rend="bold">evaṃbhāvī</hi> <hi rend="bold">evaṃanatīto</hi> <hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Khajjamāna</hi>nti udarādīsu nisīditvā udaramaṃsaoṭṭhamaṃsaakkhikūṭādīni luñcitvā luñcitvā khādiyamānaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Samaṃsalohita</hi>nti sāvasesamaṃsalohitayuttaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Nimaṃsalohitamakkhita</hi>nti maṃse khīṇepi lohitaṃ na sussati, taṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ ‘‘nimaṃsalohitamakkhita’’nti. <hi rend="bold">Aññenā</hi>ti aññena disābhāgena. <hi rend="bold">Hatthaṭṭhika</hi>nti catusaṭṭhibhedampi hatthaṭṭhikaṃ pāṭiyekkaṃ pāṭiyekkaṃ vippakiṇṇaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Pādaṭṭhikā</hi>dīsupi eseva nayo.</p> <hi rend="bold">Khajjamāna</hi> <hi rend="bold">Samaṃsalohita</hi> <hi rend="bold">Nimaṃsalohitamakkhita</hi> <hi rend="bold">Aññenā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Hatthaṭṭhika</hi> <hi rend="bold">Pādaṭṭhikā</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Terovassikānī</hi>ti atikkantasaṃvaccharāni. <hi rend="bold">Pūtīnī</hi>ti abbhokāse ṭhitāni vātātapavuṭṭhisamphassena <pb ed="V" n="2.0326"/> terovassikāneva pūtīni honti, antobhūmigatāni pana cirataraṃ tiṭṭhanti. <hi rend="bold">Cuṇṇakajātānī</hi>ti cuṇṇaṃ cuṇṇaṃ hutvā vippakiṇṇāni. Sabbattha so imamevāti vuttanayena khajjamānādīnaṃ vasena yojanā kātabbā. <hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi>ti evaṃ khajjamānādipariggaṇhanena yāva cuṇṇakabhāvā attano vā kāye, parassa vā kāye kālena vā attano, kālena vā parassa kāye kāyānupassī viharati.</p> <hi rend="bold">Terovassikānī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Pūtīnī</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0326"/> <hi rend="bold">Cuṇṇakajātānī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Idha <pb ed="M" n="2.0363"/> pana ṭhatvā navasivathikā samodhānetabbā. Ekāhamataṃ vāti hi ādinā nayena vuttā sabbāpi ekā, kākehi vā khajjamānantiādikā ekā, aṭṭhikasaṅkhalikaṃ samaṃsalohitaṃ nhārusambandhanti ekā, nimaṃsalohitamakkhitaṃ nhārusambandhanti ekā, apagatamaṃsalohitaṃ nhārusambandhanti ekā, aṭṭhikāni apagatasambandhānītiādikā ekā aṭṭhikāni setāni saṅkhavaṇṇapaṭibhāgānīti ekā, puñjakitāni terovassikānīti ekā, pūtīni cuṇṇakajātānīti ekāti.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0363"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Evaṃ kho, bhikkhave</hi>ti idaṃ navasivathikā dassetvā kāyānupassanaṃ niṭṭhapento āha. Tattha navasivathikapariggāhikā <pb ed="P" n="3.0773"/> sati dukkhasaccaṃ, tassā samuṭṭhāpikā purimataṇhā samudayasaccaṃ, ubhinnaṃ appavatti nirodhasaccaṃ, dukkhaparijānano samudayapajahano nirodhārammaṇo ariyamaggo maggasaccaṃ. Evaṃ catusaccavasena ussakkitvā nibbutiṃ pāpuṇātīti idaṃ navasivathikapariggāhakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ yāva arahattā niyyānamukhanti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Evaṃ kho, bhikkhave</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0773"/> <p rend="centre">Navasivathikapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Ettāvatā ca ānāpānapabbaṃ, iriyāpathapabbaṃ, catusampajaññapabbaṃ, paṭikūlamanasikārapabbaṃ, dhātumanasikārapabbaṃ, navasivathikapabbānīti cuddasapabbā kāyānupassanā niṭṭhitā hoti. Tattha ānāpānapabbaṃ, paṭikūlamanasikārapabbanti imāneva dve appanākammaṭṭhānāni, sivathikānaṃ pana ādīnavānupassanāvasena vuttattā sesāni dvādasāpi upacārakammaṭṭhānānevāti.</p> <p rend="centre">Kāyānupassanā niṭṭhitā.</p> <p rend="subhead">Vedanānupassanāvaṇṇanā</p>
379. Thus, having distinguished the contemplation of the body by way of the element (*dhātu*) and attention (*manasikāra*), now, to distinguish [it] by the nine charnel ground contemplations (*sivathika*), he says, "Again, furthermore" (*puna capara*), and so on.

There, "Just as he might see" (*seyyathāpi passeyyā*) means just as he might see.

"Body" (*śarīra*) means a dead body (*matasarīraṃ*).

"Thrown in the charnel ground" (*śivathikāya chaḍḍitaṃ*) means thrown away in the cemetery (*susāne apaviddhaṃ*).

"One day dead" (*ekāhaṃ matassa assāti*) means one day dead (*ekāhamataṃ*).

"Two days dead" (*dvīhaṃ matassa assāti*) means two days dead (*dvīhamataṃ*).

"Three days dead" (*tīhaṃ matassa assāti*) means three days dead (*tīhamataṃ*).

Like a blacksmith's bellows, inflated by the wind, from which life has departed, having the nature of swelling arisen gradually and in due order, is the bloated [corpse]. The bloated itself is "the bloated corpse" (*uddhumātakaṃ*).

Alternatively, what is bloated is vile because of repulsiveness, thus it is “the bloated” (*uddhumātakaṃ*).

"The livid" (*vinīlaṃ*) is said to be of altered color, the livid itself is "the livid corpse" (*vinīlakaṃ*).

Or alternatively, what is livid is vile because of repulsiveness, thus it is “the livid” (*vinīlakaṃ*).

This is a term for a corpse that is reddish-coloured in places where flesh is prominent, whitish-coloured in places where pus has collected, but mostly blue-black, as if draped with blue-black cloth in the blue-black places.

What is festering with pus oozing out from nine or various wound openings is the festering, the festering itself is "the festering corpse" (*vipubbakaṃ*).

Or alternatively, what is festering is vile because of repulsiveness, thus it is “the festering” (*vipubbakaṃ*).

What has become a festering corpse, having gone to that state, is "become a festering corpse" (*vipubbakajātaṃ*).

"He applies this to this very body" (*so imameva kāyaṃ*) means that monk applies, brings near, with wisdom, this his own body, to that body.

How? "[Thinking,] this body too is of such a nature, of such a character, not deviating from this [law of nature]" (*ayampi kho kāyo evaṃdhammo evaṃbhāvī evaṃanatīto*).

This is what is said: Because of the existence of these three things – life (*āyu*), heat (*usmā*), and consciousness (*viññāṇa*) – this body is capable of standing and moving, and so on. But, from the disappearance of these, this body, too, is of such a nature, of just a stinking nature, "having such a condition" (*evaṃbhāvī*) i.e., will be of just such a condition, of the nature of being bloated, and so on, "not deviating from this" (*evaṃanatīto*), i.e., not transcending the condition of being bloated, and so on.

"Thus internally" (*iti ajjhattaṃ vā*) means thus, by laying hold of the state of swelling and so forth, one dwells contemplating the body in the body in regard to one's own body or another's, or at one time in regard to one's own and at another time in regard to another's.

He applies this to this very body. [Thinking,] this body too is of such a nature, of such a character, not deviating from this [law of nature].

Having such a condition, not deviating from this. Thus internally. "Being eaten" (*khajjamānaṃ*) means being eaten, being torn off and torn off again, by sitting on the stomach and so on, the flesh of the stomach, the flesh of the lips, the sockets of the eyes, and so on.

"With flesh and blood" (*samaṃsalohitaṃ*) means connected with remaining flesh and blood.

"Without flesh, smeared with blood" (*nimaṃsalohitamakkhitaṃ*) means even though the flesh is gone, the blood does not dry up; with reference to that it was said, "without flesh, smeared with blood" (*nimaṃsalohitamakkhitaṃ*).

"By another" (*aññena*) means by another directional part.

"Bone of the hand" (*hatthaṭṭhikaṃ*) means the sixty-four kinds of bones of the hand, each one separate, scattered apart.

The same method applies also to "bones of the foot" (*pādaṭṭhikā*), and so on.

Being eaten, with flesh and blood, without flesh, smeared with blood, by another, bone of the hand, bones of the foot. "Of three years" (*terovassikānī*) means having passed through the years.

"Rotten" (*pūtīnī*) means placed in the open air, by contact with wind, heat and rain, the bones themselves become rotten, but those gone into the interior of the earth remain for a long time.

"Turned to powder" (*cuṇṇakajātānī*) means having become powder, powder, they are scattered apart.

Everywhere, by the method stated, the connection should be made on the basis of being eaten, and so on, with "He applies this to this very body" (*so imameva*).

"Thus internally" (*iti ajjhattaṃ vā*) means thus, by laying hold of the state of being eaten and so forth, one dwells contemplating the body in the body from the state when it is being eaten by crows and other creatures to the state when it is dust, in one's own body or in another's, or at one time in one's own body and at another time in another's body.

Having passed through the years, rotten. Turned to powder, thus internally.

Here, having stopped, one should put together the nine charnel ground contemplations.

One day dead is just one in the method beginning with "or" (*vā*), all those stated by way of principle; being eaten by crows, and so on, is one; a skeleton, with flesh and blood, connected with sinews, is one; without flesh, smeared with blood, connected with sinews, is one; with flesh and blood gone, connected with sinews, is one; the bones with the connections gone, and so on, is one; the bones white, having the appearance of shell color, is one; heaped up, of three years, is one; rotten, turned to powder, is one.
Thus indeed, monks,” he said, concluding the contemplation of the body by showing the nine cemetery contemplations.

There, the mindfulness which lays hold of the nine cemetery contemplations is the Truth of Suffering (*duḥkhasatya*); the previous craving which originates that mindfulness is the Truth of Origin (*samudayasatya*); the non-occurrence of both that mindfulness and the craving is the Truth of Cessation (*nirodhasatya*). The Noble Eightfold Path (*āryamārga*) that understands suffering, casts out the origin, and has cessation for its object is the Truth of the Way (*mārgasatya*).

Endeavoring in this way by means of the Four Truths (*catusatya*) one arrives at peace (*nirvāṇa*). This is for the *bhikkhu* who lays hold of the nine cemetery contemplations the portal of deliverance up to *arhatship*.

Thus indeed, monks."

The section on the nine cemetery contemplations is finished.

Now, these are the fourteen sections which comprise body-contemplation (*kāyānupassanā*): The section on breathing in and breathing out (*ānāpānapabba*), on the postures (*iriyāpathapabba*), on the four kinds of clear comprehension (*catusampajaññapabba*), of reflection on repulsiveness (*paṭikūlamanasikārapabba*), on the modes of materiality (*dhātumanasikārapabba*), and on the nine cemetery contemplations (*navasivathikapabba*).

There, the section on breathing in and breathing out, and the section on reflection on repulsiveness - these two alone are attainment meditation (*appanākammaṭṭhāna*), but the remaining twelve are only access meditation (*upacārakammaṭṭhāna*) since the cemeteries are spoken of by way of contemplation of disadvantages (*ādīnavānupassanā*).

Body-contemplation is finished.

Explanation of contemplation of feeling (*vedanānupassanāvaṇṇanā*).
380
<p n="380" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">380</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Kathañca pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhu vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharati? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sukhaṃ vā vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī’ti pajānāti <pb ed="T" n="2.0333"/>. Dukkhaṃ vā vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī’ti pajānāti. Adukkhamasukhaṃ vā vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī’ti pajānāti. Sāmisaṃ vā sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘sāmisaṃ sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī’ti pajānāti, nirāmisaṃ vā sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘nirāmisaṃ sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī’ti pajānāti. Sāmisaṃ vā dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘sāmisaṃ dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī’ti pajānāti, nirāmisaṃ vā dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘nirāmisaṃ dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī’ti pajānāti. Sāmisaṃ vā adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘sāmisaṃ adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī’ti pajānāti, nirāmisaṃ vā adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘nirāmisaṃ adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī’ti pajānāti. Iti ajjhattaṃ vā vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharati, bahiddhā vā vedanāsu <pb ed="M" n="2.0237"/> vedanānupassī viharati, ajjhattabahiddhā vā vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharati. Samudayadhammānupassī vā vedanāsu viharati, vayadhammānupassī <pb ed="P" n="2.0299"/> vā vedanāsu viharati, samudayavayadhammānupassī vā vedanāsu viharati. ‘Atthi vedanā’ti vā panassa sati paccupaṭṭhitā hoti <pb ed="V" n="2.0221"/> yāvadeva ñāṇamattāya paṭissatimattāya anissito ca viharati, na ca kiñci loke upādiyati. Evampi kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharati.</p> <p rend="centre">Vedanānupassanā niṭṭhitā.</p> <p rend="subhead">Cittānupassanā</p>
“And how, monks, does a monk abide contemplating feelings as feelings? Here, monks, when a monk feels a pleasant feeling (sukhaṃ vedanaṃ), he understands: ‘I feel a pleasant feeling (sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī)’; when feeling a painful feeling (dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ), he understands: ‘I feel a painful feeling (dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī)’; when feeling a neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling (adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ), he understands: ‘I feel a neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling (adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī).’ When feeling a worldly pleasant feeling (sāmisaṃ sukhaṃ vedanaṃ), he understands: ‘I feel a worldly pleasant feeling (sāmisaṃ sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī)’; when feeling an unworldly pleasant feeling (nirāmisaṃ sukhaṃ vedanaṃ), he understands: ‘I feel an unworldly pleasant feeling (nirāmisaṃ sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī).’ When feeling a worldly painful feeling (sāmisaṃ dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ), he understands: ‘I feel a worldly painful feeling (sāmisaṃ dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī)’; when feeling an unworldly painful feeling (nirāmisaṃ dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ), he understands: ‘I feel an unworldly painful feeling (nirāmisaṃ dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī).’ When feeling a worldly neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling (sāmisaṃ adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ), he understands: ‘I feel a worldly neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling (sāmisaṃ adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī)’; when feeling an unworldly neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling (nirāmisaṃ adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ), he understands: ‘I feel an unworldly neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling (nirāmisaṃ adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī).’

In this way, he abides contemplating feelings as feelings internally (ajjhattaṃ vā vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharati), or he abides contemplating feelings as feelings externally (bahiddhā vā vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharati), or he abides contemplating feelings as feelings both internally and externally (ajjhattabahiddhā vā vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharati). Or else he abides contemplating in feelings their arising factors (samudayadhammānupassī vā vedanāsu viharati), or he abides contemplating in feelings their vanishing factors (vayadhammānupassī vā vedanāsu viharati), or he abides contemplating in feelings both their arising and vanishing factors (samudayavayadhammānupassī vā vedanāsu viharati).

Or else mindfulness that ‘there is feeling (atthi vedanā)’ is simply established in him to the extent necessary for bare knowledge (ñāṇamattāya) and mindfulness (paṭissatimattāya). And he abides independent (anissito), not clinging (anupādiyati) to anything in the world (loke).

That is how a monk abides contemplating feelings as feelings (vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharati)."

End of contemplation of feelings.

Contemplation of the Mind
<p n="380" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">380</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Evaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0327"/> bhagavā cuddasavidhena kāyānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ kathetvā idāni navavidhena vedanānupassanaṃ kathetuṃ <hi rend="bold">kathañca, bhikkhave</hi>tiādimāha. Tattha <hi rend="bold">sukhaṃ vedana</hi>nti kāyikaṃ vā cetasikaṃ vā sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘‘ahaṃ sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī’’ti pajānātīti attho. Tattha <pb ed="M" n="2.0364"/> kāmaṃ uttānaseyyakāpi dārakā thaññapivanādikāle sukhaṃ vedayamānā ‘‘sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmā’’ti pajānanti, na panetaṃ evarūpaṃ jānanaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ. Evarūpañhi jānanaṃ sattūpaladdhiṃ na jahati, attasaññaṃ na ugghāṭeti, kammaṭṭhānaṃ vā satipaṭṭhānabhāvanā vā na hoti. Imassa pana bhikkhuno jānanaṃ sattūpaladdhiṃ jahati, attasaññaṃ ugghāṭeti, kammaṭṭhānañceva satipaṭṭhānabhāvanā ca hoti. Idañhi ‘‘ko vedayati, kassa vedanā, kiṃ kāraṇā vedanā’’ti evaṃ sampajānavediyanaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha ko vedayatīti na koci satto vā puggalo vā vedayati. Kassa vedanāti na kassaci sattassa vā puggalassa vā vedanā. Kiṃ kāraṇā vedanāti vatthuārammaṇāva panassa vedanā, tasmā esa evaṃ pajānāti ‘‘taṃ taṃ sukhādīnaṃ vatthuṃ ārammaṇaṃ katvā vedanāva vedayati taṃ pana vedanāya pavattiṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0774"/> upādāya’ahaṃ vedayāmī’ti vohāramattaṃ hotī’’ti. Evaṃ vatthuṃ ārammaṇaṃ katvā vedanāva vedayatīti sallakkhento esa ‘‘sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmīti pajānātī’’ti veditabbo cittalapabbate aññataratthero viya.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0774"/> <p rend="bodytext">Thero kira aphāsukakāle balavavedanāya nitthunanto aparāparaṃ parivattati, tameko daharo āha – ‘‘kataraṃ vo, bhante, ṭhānaṃ rujjatī’’ti. Āvuso, pāṭiyekkaṃ rujjanaṭṭhānaṃ nāma natthi, vatthuṃ ārammaṇaṃ katvā vedanāva vedayatīti. Evaṃ jānanakālato paṭṭhāya adhivāsetuṃ vaṭṭati no, bhante,ti. Adhivāsemi, āvusoti. Adhivāsanā, bhante, seyyoti. Thero adhivāsesi. Vāto yāva hadayā phālesi, mañcake antāni rāsikatāni ahesuṃ. Thero daharassa dassesi ‘‘vaṭṭatāvuso, ettakā adhivāsanā’’ti. Daharo tuṇhī ahosi. Thero vīriyasamataṃ yojetvā saha paṭisambhidāhi arahattaṃ pāpuṇitvā <hi rend="bold">samasīsī</hi> hutvā parinibbāyi.</p> <hi rend="bold">samasīsī</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Yathā ca sukhaṃ, evaṃ dukkhaṃ…pe… nirāmisaṃ adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘‘nirāmisaṃ adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī’’ti pajānāti. Iti bhagavā rūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ kathetvā arūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ kathento yasmā phassavasena cittavasena vā kathiyamānaṃ pākaṭaṃ na hoti <pb ed="V" n="2.0328"/>, andhakāraṃ viya khāyati, vedanānaṃ pana uppattipākaṭatāya vedanāvasena pākaṭaṃ hoti, tasmā sakkapañhe viya idhāpi vedanāvasena arūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ kathesi. Tattha ‘‘duvidhañhi <pb ed="M" n="2.0365"/> kammaṭṭhānaṃ rūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ arūpakammaṭṭhānañcā’’tiādi kathāmaggo <hi rend="bold">sakkapañhe</hi> vuttanayeneva veditabbo.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0328"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0365"/> <hi rend="bold">sakkapañhe</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha <hi rend="bold">sukhaṃ vedana</hi>ntiādīsu ayaṃ aparopi pajānanapariyāyo, sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmīti pajānātīti sukhavedanākkhaṇe dukkhavedanāya abhāvato sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayamāno ‘‘sukhaṃ vedanaṃyeva vedayāmī’’ti pajānāti. Tena yā <pb ed="P" n="3.0775"/> pubbe bhūtapubbā dukkhavedanā, tassa idāni abhāvato imissā ca sukhāya vedanāya ito paṭhamaṃ abhāvato vedanā nāma aniccā adhuvā vipariṇāmadhammā, itiha tattha sampajāno hoti. Vuttampi cetaṃ bhagavatā –</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0775"/> <p rend="indent">‘‘Yasmiṃ, aggivessana, samaye sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedeti, neva tasmiṃ samaye dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ vedeti, na adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedeti, sukhaṃyeva tasmiṃ samaye vedanaṃ vedeti. Yasmiṃ, aggivessana, samaye dukkhaṃ…pe… adukkhamasukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedeti, neva tasmiṃ samaye sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedeti, na dukkhaṃ vedanaṃ vedeti, adukkhamasukhaṃyeva tasmiṃ samaye vedanaṃ vedeti. Sukhāpi, kho, aggivessana, vedanā aniccā saṅkhatā paṭiccasamuppannā khayadhammā vayadhammā virāgadhammā nirodhadhammā. Dukkhāpi, kho…pe… adukkhamasukhāpi kho, aggivessana, vedanā aniccā…pe… nirodhadhammā. Evaṃ passaṃ, aggivessana, sutavā ariyasāvako sukhāyapi vedanāya nibbindati, dukkhāyapi vedanāya nibbindati, adukkhamasukhāyapi vedanāya nibbindati, nibbindaṃ virajjati, virāgā vimuccati, vimuttasmiṃ ‘vimuttamī’ti ñāṇaṃ hoti, ‘khīṇā jāti, vusitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ, kataṃ karaṇīyaṃ, nāparaṃ itthattāyā’ti pajānātī’’ti (ma. ni. 2.205).</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Sāmisaṃ vā sukha</hi>ntiādīsu <hi rend="bold">sāmisā sukhā</hi> nāma pañcakāmaguṇāmisasannissitā cha gehasitasomanassavedanā. <hi rend="bold">Nirāmisā sukhā</hi> nāma cha nekkhammasitasomanassavedanā. <hi rend="bold">Sāmisā dukkhā</hi> nāma cha gehasitadomanassavedanā. <hi rend="bold">Nirāmisā dukkhā</hi> nāma cha nekkhammasitadomanassavedanā. <hi rend="bold">Sāmisā adukkhamasukhā</hi> nāma cha gehasitaupekkhāvedanā. <hi rend="bold">Nirāmisā adukkhamasukhā</hi><pb ed="M" n="2.0366"/> nāma cha nekkhammasitaupekkhāvedanā. Tāsaṃ vibhāgo <hi rend="bold">sakkapañhe</hi> vuttoyeva.</p> <hi rend="bold">Sāmisaṃ vā sukha</hi> <hi rend="bold">sāmisā sukhā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Nirāmisā sukhā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Sāmisā dukkhā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Nirāmisā dukkhā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Sāmisā adukkhamasukhā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Nirāmisā adukkhamasukhā</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0366"/> <hi rend="bold">sakkapañhe</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Iti</hi><pb ed="V" n="2.0329"/><hi rend="bold">ajjhattaṃ vā</hi>ti evaṃ sukhavedanādipariggaṇhanena attano vā vedanāsu, parassa vā vedanāsu, kālena vā attano, kālena vā parassa vedanāsu vedanānupassī viharati. <hi rend="bold">Samudayavayadhammānupassī vā</hi>ti ettha pana avijjāsamudayā vedanāsamudayotiādīhi pañcahi pañcahi ākārehi vedanānaṃ samudayañca <pb ed="P" n="3.0776"/> vayañca passanto ‘‘samudayadhammānupassī vā vedanāsu viharati, vayadhammānupassī vā vedanāsu viharati, kālena samudayadhammānupassī vā vedanāsu, kālena vayadhammānupassī vā vedanāsu viharatī’’ti veditabbo. Ito paraṃ kāyānupassanāyaṃ vuttanayameva. Kevalañhi idha vedanāpariggāhikā sati dukkhasaccanti evaṃ yojanaṃ katvā vedanāpariggāhakassa bhikkhuno niyyānamukhaṃ veditabbaṃ, sesaṃ tādisamevāti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Iti</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0329"/> <hi rend="bold">ajjhattaṃ vā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Samudayavayadhammānupassī vā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0776"/> <p rend="centre">Vedanānupassanā niṭṭhitā.</p> <p rend="subhead">Cittānupassanāvaṇṇanā</p>
380. Thus, the Blessed One, having explained the fourteenfold Arousing of Mindfulness in regard to the body, now, in order to explain the ninefold contemplation of feeling, began with the words, "And how, O bhikkhus."

There, the meaning of "pleasant feeling" (*sukhaṃ vedana*) is: The bhikkhu, when experiencing a bodily or mental pleasant feeling, knows, "I experience a pleasant feeling" (*ahaṃ sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī*).

There, certainly, even infants lying on their backs know that they experience pleasure while they experience a pleasant feeling in sucking the breast and on similar occasions, [thinking] "We experience a pleasant feeling" (*sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmā*), but this knowledge is not meant here.

Because awareness of that sort does not shed the belief in a living being, does not knock out the percept of a self (*ātman*), and neither becomes a subject of meditation nor the development of the Arousing of Mindfulness.

But the knowledge of this *bhikkhu* sheds the belief in a living being, knocks out the idea of a self (*ātman*), and is both a subject of meditation and the development of the Arousing of Mindfulness.

Indeed, the knowledge meant here is concerned with experience that is wisely understood through inquiry: "Who feels? Whose is the feeling? What is the cause of feeling?"

There, "Who feels?" No being (*sattva*) or person (*pudgala*) feels.

"Whose is the feeling?" Not of any being (*sattva*) or person (*pudgala*).

"What is the cause of feeling?" Feeling is simply due to a property or basis. Therefore, this one understands thus: "Having made that property the object, feeling itself feels; but appropriating that tendency of feeling, [one says] 'I feel' (*ahaṃ vedayāmī*)," that is mere conventional usage.

Thus, recognizing that feeling itself feels, having made a property its object, this one knows "I experience a pleasant feeling" (*sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī*)," as the Elder of Cittala Mountain [did].

The Elder, during an uncomfortable time, was groaning with intense feeling and turning over and over. A certain young bhikkhu said to him, "What place of yours, venerable sir, is hurting?"

"Friend, there is no place that is specifically painful. Feeling itself feels, having made a property its object," [replied the Elder.]

"Venerable sir, from the time one knows that, is bearing up befitting?"

"I am bearing up, friend," said the Elder.

"Bearing up is excellent, venerable sir," said the young bhikkhu.

The Elder bore up.

The wind was piercing even to the heart, and the ends of the couch were like a heap [of bones].

The Elder pointed that out to the young bhikkhu and said: "Friend, is bearing up so far befitting?"

The young bhikkhu remained silent.

The Elder, having applied concentration with energy, attained *arhatship* with Analytical Knowledge and, his life span having come to an end (*samasiisi*), passed away into *parinirvana*.

His lifespan having come to an end (*samasiisi*). Just as with pleasant feeling, so too with painful feeling...and so on... Experiencing a non-attached neither-pleasant-nor-painful feeling, one understands, "I experience a non-attached neither-pleasant-nor-painful feeling."

Thus, the Blessed One, having explained the subject of meditation regarding form (*rūpa*), and in explaining the subject of meditation apart from form (*arūpa*), because what is said in terms of contact (*sparśa*) or in terms of consciousness is not clear, but seems like darkness, [and] since, however, feeling is clear by way of its arising, it is clear by way of feeling; therefore, as in the Question of Śakra (*sakkapañha*), so also here, he explained the subject of meditation apart from form (*arūpa*) in terms of feeling.

There, "Indeed, meditation is twofold: meditation regarding form (*rūpa*) and meditation apart from form (*arūpa*)," and so forth. The course of the teaching should be understood just as it was said in the Question of Śakra (*sakkapañha*).

In the Question of Śakra (*sakkapañha*). There, in "Pleasant feeling" (*sukhaṃ vedana*), etc., this is another way of understanding: One knows "I experience a pleasant feeling" (*sukhaṃ vedanaṃ vedayāmī*). Because of the absence of painful feeling at the moment of pleasant feeling, experiencing a pleasant feeling, one knows "I experience only a pleasant feeling" (*sukhaṃ vedanaṃyeva vedayāmī*).

Therefore, owing to the absence now of whatsoever painful feeling that existed before, and owing to the absence of this pleasant feeling before this time, feeling is called an impermanent, unstable, and changeable thing. Thus there is clear comprehension.

This was also said by the Blessed One:

"Aggivessana, at the time when one experiences pleasant feeling, one does not experience painful feeling; one does not experience neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling; at that time one experiences only pleasant feeling.

Aggivessana, at the time when one experiences painful feeling...and so on...[at the time when one experiences] neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling, one does not experience pleasant feeling; one does not experience painful feeling; at that time one experiences only neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling.

Aggivessana, pleasant feeling is impermanent, conditioned, dependently arisen, subject to destruction, subject to decay, subject to fading away, subject to cessation.

Aggivessana, painful feeling...and so on...Aggivessana, neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling is impermanent...and so on...subject to cessation."
Thus seeing, Aggivessana, a learned noble disciple becomes disenchanted with pleasant feeling, becomes disenchanted with painful feeling, becomes disenchanted with neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling. As he is disenchanted, he becomes dispassionate. Through dispassion, he is liberated. When liberated, there is the knowledge that he is liberated. He understands: ‘Destroyed is birth, the holy life has been lived, what had to be done has been done, there is no further for this state of being.’

Among "pleasant worldly feeling (sāmisaṃ vā sukha)," pleasant worldly feelings (sāmisā sukhā) are the six kinds of joyful feelings connected with the senses, dependent on the five strands of sense pleasure.

"Unworldly pleasure (nirāmisā sukhā)" refers to the six kinds of joyful feeling connected with renunciation.

"Pleasant worldly feeling (sāmisā dukkhā)" refers to the six kinds of unpleasant worldly feeling.

"Unworldly painful feeling (nirāmisā dukkhā)" refers to the six kinds of unpleasant feeling connected with renunciation.

"Pleasant worldly feeling (sāmisā adukkhamasukhā)" refers to the six kinds of neutral worldly feeling.

"Unworldly neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling (nirāmisā adukkhamasukhā)" refers to the six kinds of neutral feeling connected with renunciation.

Their classification has already been mentioned in the *Sakkapañha*.

"Internally (ajjhattaṃ vā)": Thus, by laying hold of pleasant feeling etc., the *bhikkhu* dwells contemplating feelings in his own feelings or in the feelings of others, or at one time in his own feelings and at another time in the feelings of others.

"Or contemplating origination-things (samudayavayadhammānupassī vā)": Here, seeing the origination and dissolution of feelings in five ways—"with the arising of ignorance, there is the arising of feeling (avijjāsamudayā vedanāsamudayo)," etc.—the *bhikkhu* should be understood as "dwelling contemplating origination-things in feelings, dwelling contemplating dissolution-things in feelings, at one time dwelling contemplating origination-things, and at one time dwelling contemplating dissolution-things in feelings."

From here on, the exposition is just according to the method already followed in the explanation of body-contemplation.

Indeed, here, mindfulness that lays hold of feeling is the Truth of Suffering (dukkhasacca). Thus, the portal of deliverance for the *bhikkhu* who lays hold of feeling should be understood, and the rest is similar.

The contemplation of feeling is finished.

Explanation of the contemplation of the mind.
381
<p n="381" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">381</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Kathañca <pb ed="T" n="2.0334"/> pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhu citte cittānupassī viharati? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sarāgaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘sarāgaṃ citta’nti pajānāti, vītarāgaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘vītarāgaṃ citta’nti pajānāti. Sadosaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘sadosaṃ citta’nti pajānāti, vītadosaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘vītadosaṃ citta’nti pajānāti. Samohaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘samohaṃ citta’nti pajānāti, vītamohaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘vītamohaṃ citta’nti pajānāti. Saṅkhittaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘saṅkhittaṃ citta’nti pajānāti, vikkhittaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘vikkhittaṃ citta’nti pajānāti. Mahaggataṃ vā cittaṃ ‘mahaggataṃ citta’nti pajānāti, amahaggataṃ vā cittaṃ ‘amahaggataṃ citta’nti pajānāti. Sauttaraṃ vā cittaṃ ‘sauttaraṃ citta’nti pajānāti, anuttaraṃ vā cittaṃ ‘anuttaraṃ citta’nti pajānāti. Samāhitaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘samāhitaṃ citta’nti pajānāti, asamāhitaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘asamāhitaṃ citta’nti pajānāti. Vimuttaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘vimuttaṃ citta’nti pajānāti. Avimuttaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘avimuttaṃ citta’nti pajānāti. Iti ajjhattaṃ vā citte cittānupassī viharati, bahiddhā vā citte cittānupassī viharati, ajjhattabahiddhā vā citte cittānupassī viharati. Samudayadhammānupassī vā cittasmiṃ viharati, vayadhammānupassī vā cittasmiṃ viharati, samudayavayadhammānupassī vā cittasmiṃ viharati, ‘atthi citta’nti vā panassa sati paccupaṭṭhitā hoti <pb ed="P" n="2.0300"/> yāvadeva ñāṇamattāya paṭissatimattāya anissito ca viharati, na ca kiñci loke upādiyati <pb ed="T" n="2.0335"/>. Evampi kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu citte cittānupassī viharati.</p> <p rend="centre">Cittānupassanā niṭṭhitā.</p> <p rend="subhead">Dhammānupassanā nīvaraṇapabbaṃ</p>
381. How, monks, does a monk abide contemplating mind as mind? Here, monks, a monk understands a lustful mind as 'a lustful mind' (sarāgaṃ cittaṃ ‘sarāgaṃ citta’nti pajānāti), or a mind free from lust as 'a mind free from lust' (vītarāgaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘vītarāgaṃ citta’nti pajānāti).

He understands a hateful mind as 'a hateful mind' (sadosaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘sadosaṃ citta’nti pajānāti), or a mind free from hate as 'a mind free from hate' (vītadosaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘vītadosaṃ citta’nti pajānāti).

He understands a deluded mind as 'a deluded mind' (samohaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘samohaṃ citta’nti pajānāti), or a mind free from delusion as 'a mind free from delusion' (vītamohaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘vītamohaṃ citta’nti pajānāti).

He understands a constricted mind as 'a constricted mind' (saṅkhittaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘saṅkhittaṃ citta’nti pajānāti), or a scattered mind as 'a scattered mind' (vikkhittaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘vikkhittaṃ citta’nti pajānāti).

He understands an exalted mind as 'an exalted mind' (mahaggataṃ vā cittaṃ ‘mahaggataṃ citta’nti pajānāti), or an unexalted mind as 'an unexalted mind' (amahaggataṃ vā cittaṃ ‘amahaggataṃ citta’nti pajānāti).

He understands a surpassed mind as 'a surpassed mind' (sauttaraṃ vā cittaṃ ‘sauttaraṃ citta’nti pajānāti), or an unsurpassed mind as 'an unsurpassed mind' (anuttaraṃ vā cittaṃ ‘anuttaraṃ citta’nti pajānāti).

He understands a concentrated mind as 'a concentrated mind' (samāhitaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘samāhitaṃ citta’nti pajānāti), or an unconcentrated mind as 'an unconcentrated mind' (asamāhitaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘asamāhitaṃ citta’nti pajānāti).

He understands a liberated mind as 'a liberated mind' (vimuttaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘vimuttaṃ citta’nti pajānāti).

He understands an unliberated mind as 'an unliberated mind' (avimuttaṃ vā cittaṃ ‘avimuttaṃ citta’nti pajānāti).

Thus, he abides contemplating mind as mind internally, or he abides contemplating mind as mind externally, or he abides contemplating mind as mind both internally and externally.

Or he abides contemplating the arising nature in the mind (samudayadhammānupassī vā cittasmiṃ viharati), or he abides contemplating the vanishing nature in the mind (vayadhammānupassī vā cittasmiṃ viharati), or he abides contemplating both the arising and vanishing nature in the mind (samudayavayadhammānupassī vā cittasmiṃ viharati). Or mindfulness that ‘there is mind’ (atthi citta’nti vā) is simply established in him to the extent necessary for bare knowledge and mindfulness. And he abides independent, not clinging to anything in the world.

That is how, monks, a monk abides contemplating mind as mind.

The contemplation of the mind is finished.

The section on the hindrances in the contemplation of phenomena.
<p n="381" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">381</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Evaṃ navavidhena vedanānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ kathetvā idāni soḷasavidhena cittānupassanaṃ kathetuṃ <hi rend="bold">kathañca, bhikkhave</hi>tiādimāha. Tattha <hi rend="bold">sarāga</hi>nti aṭṭhavidhalobhasahagataṃ. <hi rend="bold">Vītarāga</hi>nti lokiyakusalābyākataṃ. Idaṃ pana yasmā sammasanaṃ na dhammasamodhānaṃ tasmā idha ekapadepi lokuttaraṃ na labbhati. Sesāni cattāri akusalacittāni neva purimapadaṃ na pacchimapadaṃ bhajanti. <hi rend="bold">Sadosa</hi>nti duvidhadomanassasahagataṃ. <hi rend="bold">Vītadosa</hi>nti lokiyakusalābyākataṃ. Sesāni dasa akusalacittāni neva purimapadaṃ, na pacchimapadaṃ bhajanti. <hi rend="bold">Samoha</hi>nti vicikicchāsahagatañceva, uddhaccasahagatañcāti duvidhaṃ. Yasmā pana moho sabbākusalesu uppajjati, tasmā sesānipi idha vaṭṭantiyeva. Imasmiññeva hi duke dvādasākusalacittāni pariyādinnānīti. <hi rend="bold">Vītamoha</hi>nti lokiyakusalābyākataṃ. <hi rend="bold">Saṅkhitta</hi>nti thinamiddhānupatitaṃ. Etañhi saṅkuṭitacittaṃ nāma. <hi rend="bold">Vikkhitta</hi>nti uddhaccasahagataṃ, etañhi pasaṭacittaṃ nāma.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Mahaggata</hi>nti rūpārūpāvacaraṃ. <hi rend="bold">Amahaggata</hi>nti kāmāvacaraṃ. <hi rend="bold">Sauttara</hi>nti kāmāvacaraṃ. <hi rend="bold">Anuttara</hi>nti rūpāvacaraṃ arūpāvacarañca. Tatrāpi sauttaraṃ rūpāvacaraṃ, anuttaraṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0367"/> arūpāvacarameva. <hi rend="bold">Samāhita</hi>nti yassa appanāsamādhi upacārasamādhi vā atthi. <hi rend="bold">Asamāhita</hi>nti ubhayasamādhivirahitaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0330"/>. <hi rend="bold">Vimutta</hi>nti tadaṅgavikkhambhanavimuttīhi vimuttaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Avimutta</hi>nti ubhayavimuttivirahitaṃ. Samucchedapaṭippassaddhinissaraṇavimuttīnaṃ pana idha okāsova natthi.</p> <hi rend="bold">Mahaggata</hi> <hi rend="bold">Amahaggata</hi> <hi rend="bold">Sauttara</hi> <hi rend="bold">Anuttara</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0367"/> <hi rend="bold">Samāhita</hi> <hi rend="bold">Asamāhita</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0330"/> <hi rend="bold">Vimutta</hi> <hi rend="bold">Avimutta</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Iti</hi><pb ed="P" n="3.0777"/><hi rend="bold">ajjhattaṃ vā</hi>ti evaṃ sarāgādipariggaṇhanena yasmiṃ yasmiṃ khaṇe yaṃ yaṃ cittaṃ pavattati, taṃ taṃ sallakkhento attano vā citte, parassa vā citte, kālena vā attano, kālena vā parassa citte cittānupassī viharati. <hi rend="bold">Samudayavayadhammānupassī</hi>ti ettha pana avijjāsamudayā viññāṇasamudayoti evaṃ pañcahi pañcahi ākārehi viññāṇassa samudayo ca vayo ca nīharitabbo. Ito paraṃ vuttanayameva. Kevalañhi idha cittapariggāhikā sati dukkhasaccanti evaṃ padayojanaṃ katvā cittapariggāhakassa bhikkhuno niyyānamukhaṃ veditabbaṃ. Sesaṃ tādisamevāti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Iti</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0777"/> <hi rend="bold">ajjhattaṃ vā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Samudayavayadhammānupassī</hi> <p rend="centre">Cittānupassanā niṭṭhitā.</p> <p rend="subhead">Dhammānupassanā nīvaraṇapabbavaṇṇanā</p>
381. Thus, having explained the ninefold Arousing of Mindfulness in regard to feeling, now, to explain the contemplation of consciousness in the sixteenfold way, [the Master] began with the words, "And, how, O *bhikkhus*?"

There, *sarāga* means associated with the eightfold greed.

*vītarāga* means worldly wholesome and inexpressible.

But since this is a summary and not an integration of the Dhamma, therefore, even in a single word here, the supramundane is not obtained.

The remaining four karmically bad consciousnesses do not partake of either the former state or the latter state.

*sadosa* means accompanied by twofold dejection.

*vītadosa* means worldly wholesome and inexpressible.

The remaining ten karmically bad consciousnesses do not partake of either the former state or the latter state.

*samoha* means twofold, associated with doubt and also associated with agitation.

Because, indeed, ignorance arises in all karmically bad states, therefore the other karmically bad states too should be mentioned here.

In just this division all the twelve karmically bad, unwholesome or unskillful conscious states are included.

*vītamoha* means worldly wholesome and inexpressible.

*saṅkhitta* means fallen into sloth and torpor.

This is called the shrunken state of consciousness.

*vikkhitta* means associated with agitation; this is called the dissipated mind.

*mahaggata* means the realm of form and the formless realm.

*amahaggata* means the desire realm (*kāmadhātu*).

*sauttara* means the desire realm (*kāmadhātu*).

*anuttara* means the realm of form and the formless realm.

There also, *sauttara* is the *rūpadhātu* (form realm), *anuttara* is only the *arūpadhātu* (formless realm).

*samāhita* means he who has full absorption (*appanāsamādhi*) or proximate absorption (*upacārasamādhi*).

*asamāhita* means devoid of both absorptions.

*vimutta* means liberated by liberation through the parts and suppression.

*avimutta* means devoid of both liberations.

But here there is no opportunity for eradication, tranquility, or release.

*mahaggata*
*amahaggata*
*sauttara*
*anuttara*
*samāhita*
*asamāhita*
*vimutta*
*avimutta*

Thus, "internally," the bhikkhu lives contemplating consciousness in consciousness by laying hold on the consciousness with lust and so forth when these states of consciousness proceed in his own flux or in another’s flux or by laying hold of these conscious states at one time as they proceed in his own flux and at another time as they proceed in another’s flux.

"*samudayavayadhammānupassī*" means here, ignorance is the origin, consciousness is the origin; thus, in these five ways, the origin and decay of consciousness should be inferred.

From here on the exposition should be known just by the method already mentioned.

The mindfulness which lays hold of consciousness is the Truth of Suffering. Thus, the portal of deliverance up to arahantship of the *bhikkhu* who lays hold of consciousness as a subject of meditation ought to be understood.

The rest is as before.

Thus, "internally," "*samudayavayadhammānupassī*."

The contemplation of consciousness is finished.

Explanation of the section on the hindrances in the contemplation of the Dhamma.
382
<p n="382" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">382</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Kathañca <pb ed="M" n="2.0238"/><pb ed="V" n="2.0222"/> pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati pañcasu nīvaraṇesu. Kathañca pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati pañcasu nīvaraṇesu?</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu santaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ kāmacchandaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ kāmacchando’ti pajānāti, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ kāmacchandaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ kāmacchando’ti pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa kāmacchandassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa kāmacchandassa pahānaṃ hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca pahīnassa kāmacchandassa āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Santaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ byāpādaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ byāpādo’ti pajānāti, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ byāpādaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ byāpādo’ti pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa byāpādassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa byāpādassa pahānaṃ hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca pahīnassa byāpādassa āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Santaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ thinamiddhaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ thinamiddha’nti pajānāti, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ thinamiddhaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ thinamiddha’nti pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa thinamiddhassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa thinamiddhassa <pb ed="T" n="2.0336"/> pahānaṃ hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca pahīnassa thinamiddhassa āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0336"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Santaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ uddhaccakukkuccaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0301"/> uddhaccakukkucca’nti pajānāti, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ uddhaccakukkuccaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ uddhaccakukkucca’nti pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa uddhaccakukkuccassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa uddhaccakukkuccassa pahānaṃ hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca pahīnassa uddhaccakukkuccassa āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0301"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Santaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0223"/> vā ajjhattaṃ vicikicchaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ vicikicchā’ti pajānāti, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ vicikicchaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ vicikicchā’ti pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannāya vicikicchāya uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā <pb ed="M" n="2.0239"/> ca uppannāya vicikicchāya pahānaṃ hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca pahīnāya vicikicchāya āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0223"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0239"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Iti ajjhattaṃ vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati, bahiddhā vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati, ajjhattabahiddhā vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati samudayadhammānupassī vā dhammesu viharati, vayadhammānupassī vā dhammesu viharati, samudayavayadhammānupassī vā dhammesu viharati ‘atthi dhammā’ti vā panassa sati paccupaṭṭhitā hoti yāvadeva ñāṇamattāya paṭissatimattāya anissito ca viharati, na ca kiñci <pb ed="T" n="2.0337"/> loke upādiyati. Evampi kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati pañcasu nīvaraṇesu.</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0337"/> <p rend="centre">Nīvaraṇapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.</p> <p rend="subhead">Dhammānupassanā khandhapabbaṃ</p>
382. “And how, monks, does a monk abide contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects? Here, monks, a monk abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects in terms of the five hindrances. And how, monks, does a monk abide contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects in terms of the five hindrances?

“Here, monks, if sensual desire (kāmacchanda) is present in himself, a monk understands: ‘There is sensual desire in me (atthi me ajjhattaṃ kāmacchando)’; or if sensual desire is absent in himself, a monk understands: ‘There is no sensual desire in me (natthi me ajjhattaṃ kāmacchando)’; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of unarisen sensual desire, and how there comes to be the abandoning of arisen sensual desire, and how there comes to be the future non-arising of abandoned sensual desire.

“If ill-will (byāpāda) is present in himself, a monk knows that it is present; or if ill-will is absent in himself, he knows that it is absent; and he knows how unarisen ill-will comes to arise, and he knows how the abandonment of arisen ill-will comes about, and he knows how the non-arising of the abandoned ill-will in the future will come about.

“If sloth and torpor (thinamiddha) is present in himself, a monk knows that it is present; or if sloth and torpor is absent in himself, he knows that it is absent; and he knows how unarisen sloth and torpor comes to arise, and he knows how the abandonment of arisen sloth and torpor comes about, and he knows how the non-arising of the abandoned sloth and torpor in the future will come about.

“If restlessness and remorse (uddhaccakukkucca) is present in himself, a monk knows that it is present; or if restlessness and remorse is absent in himself, he knows that it is absent; and he knows how unarisen restlessness and remorse comes to arise, and he knows how the abandonment of arisen restlessness and remorse comes about, and he knows how the non-arising of the abandoned restlessness and remorse in the future will come about.

“If doubt (vicikicchā) is present in himself, a monk understands: ‘There is doubt in me’; or if doubt is absent in himself, he understands: ‘There is no doubt in me’; and he understands how there comes to be the arising of unarisen doubt, and how there comes to be the abandoning of arisen doubt, and how there comes to be the future non-arising of abandoned doubt.

“In this way, he abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects internally, or he abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects externally, or he abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects both internally and externally. Or else, he abides contemplating in mind-objects their arising factors, or he abides contemplating in mind-objects their vanishing factors, or he abides contemplating in mind-objects both their arising and vanishing factors. Or else, mindfulness that ‘there are mind-objects (atthi dhammā)’ is present just to the extent necessary for knowledge and awareness. And he abides detached, not clinging to anything in the world. That is how a monk abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects in terms of the five hindrances.

The chapter on the hindrances is finished.

The section on the aggregates in the contemplation of mind-objects.
<p n="382" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">382</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Evaṃ soḷasavidhena cittānupassanāsatipaṭṭhānaṃ kathetvā idāni pañcavidhena dhammānupassanaṃ kathetuṃ <hi rend="bold">kathañca, bhikkhave</hi>tiādimāha. Apica bhagavatā kāyānupassanāya suddharūpapariggaho kathito, vedanācittānupassanāhi suddhaarūpapariggaho. Idāni rūpārūpamissakapariggahaṃ kathetuṃ ‘‘kathañca, bhikkhave’’tiādimāha. Kāyānupassanāya vā rūpakkhandhapariggahova kathito, vedanānupassanāya vedanākkhandhapariggahova, cittānupassanāya viññāṇakkhandhapariggahova idāni saññāsaṅkhārakkhandhapariggahampi kathetuṃ ‘‘kathañca, bhikkhave’’tiādimāha.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha <hi rend="bold">santa</hi>nti abhiṇhasamudācāravasena saṃvijjamānaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Asanta</hi>nti asamudācāravasena vā pahīnattā vā asaṃvijjamānaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Yathā cā</hi>ti yena kāraṇena kāmacchandassa uppādo hoti. <hi rend="bold">Tañca pajānātī</hi>ti tañca kāraṇaṃ pajānāti. Iti iminā nayena sabbapadesu attho veditabbo.</p> <hi rend="bold">santa</hi> <hi rend="bold">Asanta</hi> <hi rend="bold">Yathā cā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Tañca pajānātī</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha subhanimitte ayonisomanasikārena kāmacchandassa uppādo hoti. <hi rend="bold">Subhanimittaṃ</hi> nāma <pb ed="V" n="2.0331"/> subhampi subhanimittaṃ, subhārammaṇampi subhanimittaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Ayonisomanasikāro</hi><pb ed="M" n="2.0368"/> nāma anupāyamanasikāro uppathamanasikāro anicce niccanti vā, dukkhe sukhanti vā, anattani attāti vā, asubhe subhanti vā manasikāro. Taṃ tattha bahulaṃ pavattayato kāmacchando uppajjati. Tenāha bhagavā – ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, subhanimittaṃ, tattha ayonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannassa vā kāmacchandassa uppādāya uppannassa vā kāmacchandassa bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāyā’’ti <pb ed="P" n="3.0778"/> (saṃ. ni. 5.232).</p> <hi rend="bold">Subhanimittaṃ</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0331"/> <hi rend="bold">Ayonisomanasikāro</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0368"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0778"/> <p rend="bodytext">Asubhanimitte pana yonisomanasikārenassa pahānaṃ hoti. <hi rend="bold">Asubhanimittaṃ</hi> nāma asubhampi asubhārammaṇampi. <hi rend="bold">Yonisomanasikāro</hi> nāma upāyamanasikāro pathamanasikāro anicce aniccanti vā, dukkhe dukkhanti vā, anattani anattāti vā, asubhe asubhanti vā manasikāro. Taṃ tattha bahulaṃ pavattayato kāmacchando pahīyati. Tenāha bhagavā – ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, asubhanimittaṃ, tattha yonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannassa vā kāmacchandassa anuppādāya uppannassa vā kāmacchandassa pahānāyā’’ti (saṃ. ni. 5.232).</p> <hi rend="bold">Asubhanimittaṃ</hi> <hi rend="bold">Yonisomanasikāro</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Apica cha dhammā kāmacchandassa pahānāya saṃvattanti asubhanimittassa uggaho, asubhabhāvanānuyogo, indriyesu guttadvāratā, bhojane mattaññutā, kalyāṇamittatā, sappāyakathāti. Dasavidhañhi asubhanimittaṃ uggaṇhantassāpi kāmacchando pahīyati, bhāventassāpi indriyesu pihitadvārassāpi catunnaṃ pañcannaṃ ālopānaṃ okāse sati udakaṃ pivitvā yāpanasīlatāya bhojanamattaññunopi. Teneva vuttaṃ –</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Cattāro pañca ālope, abhutvā udakaṃ pive;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Alaṃ phāsuvihārāya, pahitattassa bhikkhuno’’ti. (theragā. 983);</p> <p rend="bodytext">Asubhakammikatissattherasadise asubhabhāvanārate kalyāṇamitte sevantassapi kāmacchando pahīyati, ṭhānanisajjādīsu dasaasubhanissitasappāyakathāya pahīyati, tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘cha dhammā kāmacchandassa pahānāya saṃvattantī’’ti. Tattha <hi rend="bold">mettā</hi>ti vutte appanāpi upacāropi vaṭṭati. <hi rend="bold">Cetovimuttī</hi>ti appanāva. Yonisomanasikāro vuttalakkhaṇova. Taṃ tattha bahulaṃ pavattayato byāpādo pahīyati. Tenāha bhagavā – ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, mettā cetovimutti, tattha yonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannassa vā byāpādassa anuppādāya uppannassa vā byāpādassa pahānāyā’’ti (saṃ. ni. 5.232).</p> <hi rend="bold">mettā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Cetovimuttī</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Apica cha dhammā byāpādassa pahānāya saṃvattanti mettānimittassa uggaho mettābhāvanānuyogo kammassakatāpaccavekkhaṇā paṭisaṅkhānabahulatā kalyāṇamittatā sappāyakathāti. Odissakaanodissakadisāpharaṇānañhi aññataravasena mettaṃ uggaṇhantassāpi byāpādo pahīyati, odhisoanodhisopharaṇavasena mettaṃ bhāventassāpi. ‘‘Tvaṃ etassa kuddho kiṃ karissasi, kimassa sīlādīni vināsetuṃ sakkhissasi, nanu tvaṃ attano kammena āgantvā attano kammeneva gamissasi, parassa kujjhanaṃ nāma vītaccitaṅgāra tattaaya salākagūthādīni gahetvā paraṃ paharitukāmatāsadisaṃ hoti. Esopi tava kuddho kiṃ karissati, kiṃ te sīlādīni vināsetuṃ sakkhissati, esa attano kammena āgantvā attano kammeneva gamissati, appaṭicchitapaheṇakaṃ viya paṭivātaṃ khittarajomuṭṭhi viya ca etassevesa kodho matthake patissatī’’ti evaṃ attano ca parassa ca kammassakataṃ paccavekkhatopi, ubhayakammassakataṃ paccavekkhitvā paṭisaṅkhāne ṭhitassāpi, assaguttattherasadise mettābhāvanārate kalyāṇamitte sevantassāpi byāpādo pahīyati.
