Āharahatthaka tatravaṭṭaka alaṃsāṭaka kākamāsaka bhuttavamitakabhojanaṃ bhuñjitvā rattiṭṭhānadivāṭṭhāne nisinnassa hi samaṇadhammaṃ karoto thinamiddhaṃ mahāhatthī viya ottharantaṃ āgacchati, catupañcaālopaokāsaṃ pana ṭhapetvā pānīyaṃ pivitvā yāpanasīlassa bhikkhuno taṃ na hotīti evaṃ atibhojane nimittaṃ gaṇhantassāpi thinamiddhaṃ pahīyati. Yasmiṃ iriyāpathe thinamiddhaṃ okkamati, tato aññaṃ parivattentassāpi, rattiṃ candālokadīpālokaukkāloke <pb ed="P" n="3.0781"/> divā sūriyālokaṃ manasikarontassāpi, abbhokāse <pb ed="M" n="2.0371"/> vasantassāpi, mahākassapattherasadise pahīnathinamiddhe kalyāṇamitte sevantassāpi thinamiddhaṃ pahīyati. Ṭhānanisajjādīsu dhutaṅganissitasappāyakathāyapi pahīyati. Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘cha dhammā thinamiddhassa pahānāya saṃvattantī’’ti. Imehi pana chahi dhammehi pahīnassa thinamiddhassa arahattamaggena āyatiṃ anuppādo hotīti pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0781"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0371"/> <p rend="bodytext">Cetaso avūpasame ayonisomanasikārena uddhaccakukkuccassa uppādo hoti. <hi rend="bold">Avūpasamo</hi> nāma avūpasantākāro, uddhaccakukkuccamevetaṃ atthato. Tattha ayonisomanasikāraṃ bahulaṃ pavattayato uddhaccakukkuccaṃ uppajjati. Tenāha – ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, cetaso avūpasamo, tattha ayonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannassa vā uddhaccakukkuccassa <pb ed="V" n="2.0334"/> uppādāya, uppannassa vā uddhaccakukkuccassa bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāyā’’ti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Avūpasamo</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0334"/> <p rend="bodytext">Samādhisaṅkhāte pana cetaso vūpasame yonisomanasikārenassa pahānaṃ hoti. Tenāha – ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, cetaso vūpasamo, tattha yonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannassa vā uddhaccakukkuccassa anuppādāya, uppannassa vā uddhaccakukkuccassa pahānāyā’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Apica cha dhammā uddhaccakukkuccassa pahānāya saṃvattanti bahussutatā paripucchakatā vinaye pakataññutā vuddhasevitā kalyāṇamittatā sappāyakathāti. Bāhussaccenapi hi ekaṃ vā dve vā tayo vā cattāro vā pañca vā nikāye pāḷivasena atthavasena ca uggaṇhantassāpi uddhaccakukkuccaṃ pahīyati. Kappiyākappiyaparipucchābahulassāpi, vinayapaññattiyaṃ ciṇṇavasibhāvatāya pakataññunopi, vuḍḍhe mahallakatthere upasaṅkamantassāpi, upālittherasadise vinayadhare kalyāṇamitte sevantassāpi uddhaccakukkuccaṃ pahīyati, ṭhānanisajjādīsu kappiyākappiyanissitasappāyakathāyapi pahīyati. Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘cha dhammā uddhaccakukkuccassa pahānāya saṃvattantī’’ti. Imehi pana chahi dhammehi pahīne uddhaccakukkucce uddhaccassa arahattamaggena <pb ed="P" n="3.0782"/>, kukkuccassa anāgāmimaggena āyatiṃ anuppādo hotīti pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0782"/> <p rend="bodytext">Vicikicchāṭhānīyesu dhammesu ayonisomanasikārena vicikicchāya uppādo hoti. <hi rend="bold">Vicikicchāṭhānīyā dhammā</hi> nāma punappunaṃ vicikicchāya kāraṇattā <pb ed="M" n="2.0372"/> vicikicchāva. Tattha ayonisomanasikāraṃ bahulaṃ pavattayato vicikicchā uppajjati. Tenāha – ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, vicikicchāṭhānīyā dhammā, tattha ayonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannāya vā vicikicchāya uppādāya, uppannāya vā vicikicchāya bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāyā’’ti (saṃ. ni. 5.232).</p> <hi rend="bold">Vicikicchāṭhānīyā dhammā</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0372"/> <p rend="bodytext">Kusalādidhammesu yonisomanasikārena panassā pahānaṃ hoti, tenāha – ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, kusalākusalā dhammā sāvajjānavajjā dhammā sevitabbāsevitabbā dhammā hīnapaṇītā dhammā kaṇhasukkasappaṭibhāgā dhammā. Tattha yonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro, anuppannāya vā vicikicchāya anuppādāya; uppannāya vā vicikicchāya pahānāyā’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Apica <pb ed="V" n="2.0335"/> cha dhammā vicikicchāya pahānāya saṃvattanti bahussutatā, paripucchakatā, vinaye pakataññutā, adhimokkhabahulatā, kalyāṇamittatā, sappāyakathāti. Bāhussaccenapi hi ekaṃ vā…pe… pañca vā nikāye pāḷivasena ca atthavasena ca uggaṇhantassāpi vicikicchā pahīyati, tīṇi ratanāni ārabbha paripucchābahulassāpi, vinaye ciṇṇavasībhāvassāpi, tīsu ratanesu okappaniyasaddhāsaṅkhātaadhimokkhabahulassāpi, saddhādhimutte vakkalittherasadise kalyāṇamitte sevantassāpi vicikicchā pahīyati, ṭhānanisajjādīsu tiṇṇaṃ ratanānaṃ guṇanissitasappāyakathāyapi pahīyati. Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘cha dhammā vicikicchāya pahānāya saṃvattantī’’ti. Imehi pana chahi dhammehi pahīnāya vicikicchāya sotāpattimaggena āyatiṃ anuppādo hotīti pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0335"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi>ti evaṃ pañcanīvaraṇapariggaṇhanena attano vā dhammesu, parassa vā dhammesu, kālena vā attano, kālena vā parassa dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati. Samudayavayā panettha subhanimittaasubhanimittādīsu ayonisomanasikārayonisomanasikāravasena <pb ed="P" n="3.0783"/> pañcasu nīvaraṇesu vuttāyeva nīharitabbā. Ito paraṃ vuttanayameva. Kevalañhi idha nīvaraṇapariggāhikā <pb ed="M" n="2.0373"/> sati dukkhasaccanti evaṃ yojanaṃ katvā nīvaraṇapariggāhakassa bhikkhuno niyyānamukhaṃ veditabbaṃ. Sesaṃ tādisamevāti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0783"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0373"/> <p rend="centre">Nīvaraṇapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.</p> <p rend="subhead">Khandhapabbavaṇṇanā</p>
382. Having explained the Arousing of Mindfulness in regard to consciousness in the sixteenfold way, now, in order to expound the fivefold contemplation of mental objects *dhamma*, he said, “And, how, o *bhikkhus*."

Further, the Blessed One taught the laying hold of pure corporeality or materiality in the instruction on body-contemplation, and in the instruction on the contemplation of feeling and consciousness, the laying hold of the purely spiritual.

Now in order to teach the laying hold of a mixture of the material and the spiritual, he said, “And, how, o *bhikkhus*."

Or in the contemplation on the body the laying hold of the aggregate of corporeality or materiality was spoken of; in the contemplation on feeling, the laying hold of the aggregate of feeling; in the contemplation on mind, the laying hold of the aggregate of consciousness; and now in order to speak of even the laying hold of the aggregates of perception *saṃjñā* and formations *saṃskāra*, he said “And, how, o *bhikkhus*."

There, "existing" means existing by way of constant practice.

"Non-existing" means not existing by way of lack of practice, or because it has been abandoned.

"Just as," means for what reason craving for sensual pleasure *kāmacchanda* arises.

"And he understands that," means he understands that reason.

Thus, the meaning should be understood in all passages by this method.

Existing, non-existing, just as, and he understands that. There, wrong reflection *ayoniso manasikāra* on a sensuously auspicious object causes the arising of craving for sensual pleasure *kāmacchanda*.

"Auspicious sign" *subhanimitta* means either sensuality itself *subha*, or that which produces sensuality, the sensuality-object *subhārammaṇa*.

Wrong reflection *ayoniso manasikāra* means inexpedient reflection *anupāyamanasikāra*, reflection on the wrong track *uppathamanasikāra*. Or it is reflection which considers the impermanent *anicca* as permanent *nicca*, pain *duḥkha* as pleasure *sukha*, non-soul *anatta* as soul *atta*, the bad *asubha* as good *subha*.

Sense-desire *kāmacchanda* arises when that occurs much.

Therefore the Blessed One said that the condition for the arising of fresh sense-desire *kāmacchanda* and for the increase and expansion of existing sense-desire is plentiful wrong reflection on a sensuously auspicious or promising object.

Auspicious sign, wrong reflection

But in the inauspicious object, its abandonment is through right reflection *yoniso manasikāra*.

"Inauspicious sign" *asubhanimitta* means either the inauspicious itself *asubha*, or that which is the object of the inauspicious *asubhārammaṇa*.

Right reflection *yoniso manasikāra* is expedient reflection *upāyamanasikāra*, reflection going on the right track *pathamanasikāra*. It is reflection that considers the facts of impermanence *anicca*, suffering *dukkha*, soullessness *anatta* and of impurity *asubha*, according to reality.

When that occurs much there, sense-desire *kāmacchanda* is knocked out.

Therefore the Blessed One said that the condition for keeping out new sense-desire *kāmacchanda* and for casting out old sense-desire is abundant right reflection on the sensuously inauspicious or unpromising object.

Inauspicious sign, right reflection

Furthermore, six things conduce to the rejection of sensuous desire *kāmacchanda*: taking up the sensuously inauspicious object *asubhanimitta*, application to developing meditation on the sensuously inauspicious *asubhabhāvanā*, guarding the doors of the senses *indriya*, knowing moderation in food *bhojana*, good friendship *kalyāṇamittatā*, and suitable talk *sappāyakathā*.

For when one takes up the tenfold object sensuously inauspicious, impure, or bad *asubhanimitta*, sense-desire is knocked out; also by him who develops it, by him who guards the controlling faculties of sense by closing the six sense doors *indriya*, and by him who knows the measure of food for sustenance, even when there is opportunity for four or five morsels *ālopa*, having drunk water, and by him who is of a sustaining nature in food.

Therefore it was said:

“Having eaten four or five morsels, drink water;

Enough for ease of dwelling is that for the monk who is intent."

(Theragāthā. 983);

Sense-desire is also knocked out for one who frequents a good friend who is like the Elder Tissa, who is devoted to meditation on the sensuously inauspicious object, by suitable talk dependent on the ten inauspicious [objects] *dasāsubhanissita* in places of sitting, etc.; therefore it was said: “Six things conduce to the rejection of sense-desire *kāmacchanda*.”

But through these six things, the yogi understands that sense-desire having been cast out, there is no more arising in the future through the attainment of the path of arahantship.

Indeed, wrong reflection *ayoniso manasikāra* on an object of resentment produces anger *byāpāda*.
There, aversion, too, [is] an aversion-sign; an aversion-object, too, [is] an aversion-sign. Wrong reflection has just the same character everywhere. And when it occurs much in that sign, anger arises. Therefore, the Blessed One said: "There is, *bhikkhus*, an aversion-sign; frequently giving careless attention to it is the nutriment for the arising of unarisen ill will and for the increase and expansion of arisen ill will" (SN 5.232).

But by right reflection on the mind-deliverance of loving-kindness there is abandonment of it [ill-will]. There, when "*mettā*," loving-kindness, is said, both attainment and proximity-concentration are suitable. "*Cetovimuttī*," mind-deliverance, [means] attainment alone. Right reflection has the same character throughout. When it occurs strong there, anger is removed. Therefore, the Blessed One said: “There is, O *bhikkhus*, the liberation of the mind through love. Intense right reflection on love is the condition for keeping out new anger and for throwing out anger that is already in the heart" (SN 5.232).

Moreover, six things conduce to the rejection of ill will: taking up the sign of loving-kindness, application to the cultivation of loving-kindness, investigation of ownership of karma, frequency of reflection, good friendship, and suitable talk. Verily, in one who takes up the love subject of meditation by way of spreading it particularly or separately, anger will be put down. Or if one cultivates the love subject of meditation by way of spreading it generally, without particularization or directional restriction in space, then too anger will be put down. "What will you do to him by becoming angry? Will you be able to destroy things like his virtue? Have you not been born here just by your own actions, and will you not also get reborn hereafter by your own actions? Getting angry with another is comparable to the state of him who wishes to strike another with glowing coals, red-hot crowbar, excreta and such other damaging things after taking them up in his bare hands. And what can another who is angry with you do to you? Can he destroy your virtue or any other similar thing of yours? He, too, has been born here as a result of his actions, and will be reborn hereafter just according to his actions. Like a present not accepted is that anger of his, and like a handful of dust thrown against the wind, that anger of his alights on his own head." In this way, even in one who reflects on one’s own action as one’s own property and also another person’s action as that person's, even in one who reflects on the actions of both as their own and abides in reflection, even in one who keeps the company of good friends sympathetic towards those who delight in the cultivation of loving-kindness like the Elder Assagutta, anger is rejected. It is also rejected by stimulating talk connected with the thought of love when in any one of the four postures. Therefore, it was said, “Six things conduce to the rejection of ill will." However, by these six things, one knows that the ill will that has been rejected is unarisen in the future by the path of the non-returner.

Sloth and Torpor: Through wrong reflection on a state of boredom and the like, sloth and torpor come to be. Lassitude (*tandī*) is bodily laziness. Languidity of body (*vijambhitā*) is the bending of the body torpidly in getting up and in similar actions. Lethargy after a meal (*bhattasammado*) is the intoxication of a meal, the burning from a meal. The mind’s sluggishness (*cetaso līnattaṃ*) is the dullness of the mind. In these [states] of boredom and the like, sloth and torpor come to be in one who frequently practices wrong reflection. Therefore, the Blessed One said that much wrong reflection on boredom, lassitude, languidity of body, lethargy after a meal, and the mind’s sluggishness, is a condition for the production of fresh sloth and torpor and the increase and expansion of sloth and torpor already come into being (SN.
5.232).

*Arati*, *tandī*, *vijambhitā*, *bhattasammado*, *cetaso līnattaṃ*

But by right reflection on the element of inceptive energy, its rejection occurs. *Ārambhadhātu* means initial inceptive energy. *Nikkamadhātu* means stronger than that, owing to going forth from laziness. *Parakkama-dhātu* means still stronger than that, owing to assailing further and further places. By exercising right reflection intensely on this threefold strenuousness sloth and torpor are cast out. Therefore the Blessed One said that the condition for keeping out new sloth and torpor, and for casting out sloth and torpor that is old, is abundant right reflection on the element of inceptive energy, of exertion and of progressive endeavor (SN 5.232).

*Ārambhadhātu*, *nikkamadhātu*, *parakkama-dhātu*

Moreover, six things are conducive to the casting out of sloth and torpor: paying attention to the sign through overeating, changing over from posture to posture, reflecting on the light-perception, living in the open air, sympathetic and helpful companionship and stimulating talk. For sloth and torpor, like a great elephant, comes overwhelming him who, after eating food, whether good or bad, just enough to fill the hand, the bowl or the alms-bowl, or enough to fill a crow’s measure, is seated by night or day, and tries to fulfil the duties of a recluse; but for the bhikkhu who puts aside a space of four or five mouthfuls, drinks water and is of frugal habit, that does not occur. Thus sloth and torpor disappear in him who pays attention to the sign that is overeating. Sloth and torpor disappear in him who changes over from the posture which induces sloth and torpor to another; in him who reflects on the brightness or the light of the moon, a lamp or a torch by night, and on the light or brightness of the sun by day; in him who lives in the open; in him who associates with sympathetic and helpful companions, like the Elder Mahā Kassapa, who have dispelled sloth and torpor; and by stimulating talk connected with a strict recluse-regimen. Therefore it is said: Six things lead to the casting out of sloth and torpor. The *yogī* understands thus: sloth and torpor cast out by these six things are stopped from arising forever in the future by the attainment of the path of arahantship.

Mental agitation and worry arise through wrong reflection on mental agitation. *Avūpasamo* means a state of non-tranquillity; in meaning that is just flurry and worry. Intense wrong reflection on that mental agitation produces flurry and worry. Therefore the Blessed One said that wrong reflection on mental agitation when plentifully done produces fresh flurry and worry and increases and expands flurry and worry already in existence.

*Avūpasamo*

But by right reflection on mental tranquillity designated as concentration, its rejection occurs. Therefore it is said: The condition for the keeping out of fresh mental agitation and worry and the dispelling of agitation and worry already in the mind, is an abundance of right reflection on mental tranquillity.

Six things are conducive to the casting out of agitation and worry: knowledge, questioning, understanding of disciplinary rules; association with those more experienced and older than oneself in the practice of things like virtue; sympathetic and helpful companionship and stimulating talk. In explanation it is said as follows: Agitation and worry disappear in him who learns in the spirit and in the letter one, two, three, four or five collections of Scripture. This is how one gets over agitation and worry by knowledge. Agitation and worry disappear in him who questions much about what is befitting and not befitting, according to the practice of the Sangha. And also in him who has got the mastery of the Discipline due to practical application of and conversance with the nature of the Rule of the Sangha. Then these twin evils disappear in him who goes to the presence of the elders and senior *theras*, and in him who keeps the company of sympathetic and helpful companions, like the Elder Upāli, who is a master of the Discipline. They also disappear by stimulating talk connected with what is befitting and what is not. Therefore it is said: Six things are conducive to the casting out of agitation and worry. The *yogī* understands thus: agitation, however, cast out by these six things finally ceases to arise in the future through the attainment of the path of arahantship, and worry cast out by these things finally ceases to arise in the future through the attainment of the path of the non-returner.

In the case of things which are founded on doubt, doubt arises through wrong reflection. Things which are founded on doubt are known as just doubt owing to the state of being the reason of doubt again and again.
Regarding doubt (vicikicchā):

When wrong reflection (*ayoniso-manasikāra*) is frequently practiced concerning that, doubt arises. Therefore, it was said: "Monks, there are things that are the basis of doubt. In that case, frequently applying wrong reflection is a nourishment for the arising of doubt that has not yet arisen, and for the increase and expansion of doubt that has already arisen" (Saṃyutta Nikāya 5.232).

*Vicikicchāṭhānīyā dhammā* (things that are the basis of doubt):

However, its abandonment occurs by right reflection (*yoniso-manasikāra*) on wholesome qualities (*kusalādidhammesu*). Therefore, it was said: "Monks, there are wholesome and unwholesome qualities (*kusalākusalā dhammā*), blameworthy and blameless qualities (*sāvajjānavajjā dhammā*), qualities to be cultivated and not to be cultivated (*sevitabbāsevitabbā dhammā*), inferior and superior qualities (*hīnapaṇītā dhammā*), qualities with a dark and bright counterpart (*kaṇhasukkasappaṭibhāgā dhammā*). In that case, frequently applying right reflection is a nourishment for the non-arising of doubt that has not yet arisen, and for the abandonment of doubt that has already arisen."

And again, six things lead to the abandonment of doubt: being well-learned (*bahussutatā*), questioning (*paripucchakatā*), understanding the nature of the Discipline (*vinaye pakataññutā*), much conviction (*adhimokkhabahulatā*), sympathetic companionship (*kalyāṇamittatā*), and stimulating talk (*sappāyakathā*). Doubt is abandoned even for one who learns one, or...or five Nikāyas by way of the Pali text and by way of the meaning, even for one who is much engaged in questioning concerning the Three Jewels, even for one who has long been familiar with the Discipline, even for one who has much conviction consisting of faith in the Three Jewels, even for one who cultivates sympathetic friends like the venerable Vakkali who is liberated by faith, [and] even by stimulating talk dependent on the qualities of the Three Jewels in places of sitting and lying down, etc. Therefore, it was said: "Six things lead to the abandonment of doubt."

However, one understands that the doubt abandoned by these six things does not arise in the future by the path of stream-entry (*sotāpattimagga*).

Thus, "internally" (*ajjhattaṃ vā*): in this way, by attending to the five hindrances, one dwells observing phenomena in one's own qualities, or in the qualities of others, or in one's own qualities at times, or in the qualities of others at times. Here, origination and dissolution should be drawn out in the five hindrances just as they were stated by way of wrong reflection and right reflection on attractive signs (*subhanimitta*) and unattractive signs (*asubhanimitta*), etc. From here on, the exposition is just according to the manner already stated. Here, mindfulness which lays hold of the hindrances is the Truth of Suffering. Thus, the portal of deliverance of the *bhikkhu* who lays hold of the hindrances should be understood. The rest is just like that.

Thus, "internally" (*iti ajjhattaṃ vā*).

The chapter on the hindrances is finished.

Explanation of the chapter on the aggregates (*khandhapabba*)
383
<p n="383" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">383</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati pañcasu upādānakkhandhesu. Kathañca pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati pañcasu upādānakkhandhesu? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu – ‘iti rūpaṃ, iti rūpassa samudayo, iti rūpassa atthaṅgamo; iti vedanā, iti vedanāya samudayo, iti vedanāya atthaṅgamo; iti saññā, iti saññāya samudayo, iti saññāya atthaṅgamo; iti saṅkhārā, iti <pb ed="P" n="2.0302"/> saṅkhārānaṃ samudayo, iti saṅkhārānaṃ atthaṅgamo, iti viññāṇaṃ, iti viññāṇassa samudayo, iti viññāṇassa atthaṅgamo’ti, iti ajjhattaṃ vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati, bahiddhā vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati, ajjhattabahiddhā vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati. Samudayadhammānupassī vā dhammesu viharati, vayadhammānupassī vā dhammesu viharati, samudayavayadhammānupassī vā dhammesu viharati. ‘Atthi dhammā’ti vā panassa sati paccupaṭṭhitā hoti yāvadeva ñāṇamattāya paṭissatimattāya, anissito ca viharati, na ca kiñci loke upādiyati <pb ed="V" n="2.0224"/>. Evampi kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati pañcasu upādānakkhandhesu.</p> <p rend="centre">Khandhapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.</p> <p rend="subhead">Dhammānupassanā āyatanapabbaṃ</p>
“Again, monks, a monk abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects with reference to the five aggregates of grasping (pañcasu upādānakkhandhesu).

“And how, monks, does a monk abide contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects with reference to the five aggregates of grasping? Here, monks, a monk understands: ‘Such is material form (rūpaṃ), such its origin (rūpassa samudayo), such its disappearance (rūpassa atthaṅgamo); such is feeling (vedanā), such its origin (vedanāya samudayo), such its disappearance (vedanāya atthaṅgamo); such is perception (saññā), such its origin (saññāya samudayo), such its disappearance (saññāya atthaṅgamo); such are the mental formations (saṅkhārā), such their origin (saṅkhārānaṃ samudayo), such their disappearance (saṅkhārānaṃ atthaṅgamo); such is consciousness (viññāṇaṃ), such its origin (viññāṇassa samudayo), such its disappearance (viññāṇassa atthaṅgamo).’ In this way, he abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects internally (ajjhattaṃ vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati), or externally (bahiddhā vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati), or both internally and externally (ajjhattabahiddhā vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati).

He abides contemplating arising phenomena (samudayadhammānupassī) in mind-objects, he abides contemplating vanishing phenomena (vayadhammānupassī) in mind-objects, he abides contemplating both arising and vanishing phenomena (samudayavayadhammānupassī) in mind-objects.

Or else, mindfulness that ‘there are mind-objects (atthi dhammā)’ is present just to the extent necessary for knowledge and awareness (ñāṇamattāya paṭissatimattāya). And he abides detached (anissito), not grasping at anything in the world (loke upādiyati).

That is how a monk abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects in respect of the five aggregates of grasping (pañcasu upādānakkhandhesu).

The chapter on the aggregates is finished (khandhapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ).

The section on contemplation of mind-objects; the chapter on the sense bases (dhammānupassanā āyatanapabbaṃ).
<p n="383" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">383</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Evaṃ pañcanīvaraṇavasena dhammānupassanaṃ vibhajitvā idāni pañcakkhandhavasena vibhajituṃ <hi rend="bold">puna capara</hi>ntiādimāha. Tattha <hi rend="bold">pañcasu upādānakkhandhesū</hi>ti upādānassa khandhā upādānakkhandhā, upādānassa paccayabhūtā dhammapuñjā dhammarāsayoti attho. Ayamettha saṅkhepo. Vitthārato pana khandhakathā visuddhimagge vuttā.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Iti rūpa</hi>nti idaṃ rūpaṃ, ettakaṃ rūpaṃ, na ito paraṃ rūpaṃ atthīti sabhāvato rūpaṃ pajānāti. Vedanādīsupi eseva nayo. Ayamettha saṅkhepo, vitthārena pana rūpādīni visuddhimagge <pb ed="V" n="2.0336"/> khandhakathāyameva vuttāni. <hi rend="bold">Iti rūpassa samudayo</hi>ti evaṃ avijjāsamudayādivasena pañcahākārehi rūpassa samudayo. <hi rend="bold">Iti rūpassa atthaṅgamo</hi>ti evaṃ avijjānirodhādivasena pañcahākārehi rūpassa atthaṅgamo. Vedanādīsupi eseva nayo. Ayamettha saṅkhepo, vitthāro pana visuddhimagge udayabbayañāṇakathāya vutto.</p> <hi rend="bold">Iti rūpa</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0336"/> <hi rend="bold">Iti rūpassa samudayo</hi> <hi rend="bold">Iti rūpassa atthaṅgamo</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi>ti evaṃ pañcakkhandhapariggaṇhanena attano vā dhammesu, parassa vā dhammesu, kālena vā attano, kālena vā parassa dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati. Samudayavayā panettha ‘‘avijjāsamudayā rūpasamudayo’’tiādīnaṃ pañcasu khandhesu vuttānaṃ paññāsāya lakkhaṇānaṃ vasena nīharitabbā. Ito paraṃ vuttanayameva. Kevalañhi idha khandhapariggāhikā sati dukkhasaccanti evaṃ yojanaṃ katvā khandhapariggāhakassa bhikkhuno niyyānamukhaṃ veditabbaṃ. Sesaṃ tādisamevāti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi> <p rend="centre">Khandhapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.</p> <p rend="subhead">Āyatanapabbavaṇṇanā</p>
383. Having thus distinguished the contemplation of the Dhamma by way of the five hindrances, he now, in order to distinguish it by way of the five aggregates, says, "Furthermore…"
There, "in the five aggregates of clinging" means aggregates of clinging are aggregates of clinging; it means a mass of Dhammas, a heap of Dhammas, which are the condition for clinging.
This is a brief explanation.
The talk on the aggregates, however, is stated in detail in the Path of Purity (Visuddhimagga).

"Thus is material form": He understands material form according to its nature, [thinking,] "This is material form, so much is material form, there is no more material form than this."
In regard to feeling and the things that come afterwards, the same is the method of exegesis.
This is a brief explanation, but the material form and the rest are stated at length in the Path of Purity in the talk on the aggregates itself.
"Thus is the arising of material form": Thus is the arising of material form in five ways, beginning with the arising of ignorance.
"Thus is the passing away of material form": Thus is the passing away of material form in five ways, beginning with the cessation of ignorance.
In regard to feeling and the things that come afterwards, the same is the method of exegesis.
This is a brief explanation, but the detailed explanation is stated in the Path of Purity in the talk on the knowledge of arising and passing away.

Thus is material form
Thus is the arising of material form
Thus is the passing away of material form

Thus, "internally": Thus, by laying hold of the five aggregates, he dwells contemplating the Dhamma in his own mental objects or in the mental objects of others, or at one time in his own mental objects and at another time in the mental objects of others.
Herein, the origination and dissolution should be brought forward and connected by way of the fifty characteristics of the five aggregates, with the extended application of the words: “From the arising of ignorance the arising of material form comes to be.”
From here on the exposition is just according to the manner already stated.
Indeed, the mindfulness that lays hold of the aggregates is the Truth of Suffering. Thus, the portal of deliverance for the *bhikkhu* who lays hold of the aggregates should be understood.
The rest is as before.

Thus, "internally"
The section on the aggregates is finished.

Explanation of the section on the sense-fields
384
<p n="384" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">384</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Puna <pb ed="T" n="2.0338"/><pb ed="M" n="2.0240"/> caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati chasu ajjhattikabāhiresu āyatanesu. Kathañca pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati chasu ajjhattikabāhiresu āyatanesu?</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu cakkhuñca pajānāti, rūpe ca pajānāti, yañca tadubhayaṃ paṭicca uppajjati saṃyojanaṃ tañca pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa saṃyojanassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa saṃyojanassa pahānaṃ hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca pahīnassa saṃyojanassa āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Sotañca pajānāti, sadde ca pajānāti, yañca tadubhayaṃ paṭicca uppajjati saṃyojanaṃ tañca pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa saṃyojanassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa saṃyojanassa pahānaṃ hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca pahīnassa saṃyojanassa āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Ghānañca pajānāti, gandhe ca pajānāti, yañca tadubhayaṃ paṭicca uppajjati saṃyojanaṃ tañca pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa saṃyojanassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa saṃyojanassa pahānaṃ hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca pahīnassa saṃyojanassa āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Jivhañca pajānāti, rase ca pajānāti, yañca tadubhayaṃ paṭicca uppajjati saṃyojanaṃ tañca pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa saṃyojanassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti <pb ed="V" n="2.0225"/>, yathā ca uppannassa saṃyojanassa pahānaṃ hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca pahīnassa saṃyojanassa āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0225"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Kāyañca pajānāti, phoṭṭhabbe ca pajānāti, yañca tadubhayaṃ paṭicca uppajjati saṃyojanaṃ tañca pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa saṃyojanassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa saṃyojanassa pahānaṃ hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca pahīnassa saṃyojanassa āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Manañca <pb ed="M" n="2.0241"/> pajānāti, dhamme ca pajānāti, yañca tadubhayaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0303"/> paṭicca uppajjati saṃyojanaṃ tañca pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa saṃyojanassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa saṃyojanassa pahānaṃ hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca pahīnassa saṃyojanassa āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0241"/> <pb ed="P" n="2.0303"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Iti ajjhattaṃ vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati, bahiddhā vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati, ajjhattabahiddhā vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati. Samudayadhammānupassī vā dhammesu viharati, vayadhammānupassī vā dhammesu viharati, samudayavayadhammānupassī vā dhammesu viharati. ‘Atthi <pb ed="T" n="2.0339"/> dhammā’ti vā panassa sati paccupaṭṭhitā hoti yāvadeva ñāṇamattāya paṭissatimattāya, anissito ca viharati, na ca kiñci loke upādiyati. Evampi kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati chasu ajjhattikabāhiresu āyatanesu.</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0339"/> <p rend="centre">Āyatanapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.</p> <p rend="subhead">Dhammānupassanā bojjhaṅgapabbaṃ</p>
384. And further, monks, a monk abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects in respect of the six internal and external sense-bases.

And how, monks, does a monk abide contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects in terms of the six internal and external sense-bases?

Here, monks, a monk understands the eye (cakṣu), he understands forms (rūpa), and he understands the fetter (saṃyojana) that arises dependent on both; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen fetter, and how there comes to be the abandoning of the arisen fetter, and how there comes to be the future non-arising of the abandoned fetter.

He knows the ear (śrotra) and knows sounds (śabda). and he knows the fetter (saṃyojana) that arises dependent on both; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen fetter, and how there comes to be the abandoning of the arisen fetter, and how there comes to be the future non-arising of the abandoned fetter.

He knows the nose (ghāṇa), and knows smells (gandha). and he knows the fetter (saṃyojana) that arises dependent on both; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen fetter, and how there comes to be the abandoning of the arisen fetter, and how there comes to be the future non-arising of the abandoned fetter.

He knows the tongue (jihvā), and knows tastes (rasa), and he knows the fetter (saṃyojana) that arises dependent on both; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen fetter, and how there comes to be the abandoning of the arisen fetter, and how there comes to be the future non-arising of the abandoned fetter.

He knows the body (kāya) and knows tangibles (phoṭṭhabba). and he knows the fetter (saṃyojana) that arises dependent on both; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen fetter, and how there comes to be the abandoning of the arisen fetter, and how there comes to be the future non-arising of the abandoned fetter.

He understands the mind (manas), he understands mind-objects (dharma), and he understands the fetter (saṃyojana) that arises dependent on both; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen fetter, and how there comes to be the abandoning of the arisen fetter, and how there comes to be the future non-arising of the abandoned fetter.

In this way he abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects internally, or he abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects externally, or he abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects both internally and externally. He abides contemplating arising phenomena in mind-objects, he abides contemplating vanishing phenomena in mind-objects, he abides contemplating both arising and vanishing phenomena in mind-objects.

Or else, mindfulness that “there are mind-objects” is present just to the extent necessary for knowledge and awareness. And he abides detached, not grasping at anything in the world.

That is how a bhikkhu abides contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects in terms of the six internal and external bases.

The section on the sense-bases is complete.

The section on contemplation of mind-objects related to the awakening factors.
<p n="384" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">384</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Evaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0784"/><pb ed="M" n="2.0374"/> pañcakkhandhavasena dhammānupassanaṃ vibhajitvā idāni āyatanavasena vibhajituṃ <hi rend="bold">puna capara</hi>ntiādimāha. Tattha <hi rend="bold">chasu ajjhattikabāhiresu āyatanesū</hi>ti cakkhu sotaṃ ghānaṃ jivhā kāyo manoti imesu chasu ajjhattikesu, rūpaṃ saddo gandho raso phoṭṭhabbo dhammoti imesu chasu bāhiresu. <hi rend="bold">Cakkhuñca pajānātī</hi>ti cakkhupasādaṃ yāthāvasarasalakkhaṇavasena pajānāti. <hi rend="bold">Rūpe ca pajānātī</hi>ti bahiddhā catusamuṭṭhānikarūpañca yāthāvasarasalakkhaṇavasena pajānāti. <hi rend="bold">Yañca tadubhayaṃ paṭicca uppajjati saṃyojana</hi>nti yañca taṃ cakkhuṃ ceva rūpe cāti ubhayaṃ paṭicca. Kāmarāgasaṃyojanaṃ paṭigha, māna, diṭṭhi, vicikicchā, sīlabbataparāmāsa, bhavarāga, issā, macchariya, avijjāsaṃyojananti dasavidhaṃ saṃyojanaṃ uppajjati, tañca yāthāvasarasalakkhaṇavasena pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Kathaṃ panetaṃ uppajjatīti? Cakkhudvāre tāva āpāthagataṃ iṭṭhārammaṇaṃ kāmassādavasena assādayato abhinandato kāmarāgasaṃyojanaṃ uppajjati. Aniṭṭhārammaṇe kujjhato paṭighasaṃyojanaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0337"/> uppajjati. ‘‘Ṭhapetvā maṃ ko añño etaṃ ārammaṇaṃ vibhāvetuṃ samattho atthī’’ti maññato mānasaṃyojanaṃ uppajjati. Etaṃ rūpārammaṇaṃ niccaṃ dhuvanti gaṇhato diṭṭhisaṃyojanaṃ uppajjati. ‘‘Etaṃ rūpārammaṇaṃ satto nu kho, sattassa nu kho’’ti vicikicchato vicikicchāsaṃyojanaṃ uppajjati. ‘‘Sampattibhave vata no idaṃ sulabhaṃ jāta’’nti bhavaṃ patthentassa bhavarāgasaṃyojanaṃ uppajjati. ‘‘Āyatimpi evarūpaṃ sīlabbataṃ samādiyitvā sakkā laddhu’’nti sīlabbataṃ samādiyantassa sīlabbataparāmāsasaṃyojanaṃ uppajjati. ‘‘Aho vata taṃ rūpārammaṇaṃ aññe na labheyyu’’nti usūyato issāsaṃyojanaṃ uppajjati. Attanā laddhaṃ rūpārammaṇaṃ aññassa maccharāyato macchariyasaṃyojanaṃ uppajjati. Sabbeheva sahajātaaññāṇavasena avijjāsaṃyojanaṃ uppajjati.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0337"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Yathā ca anuppannassā</hi>ti yena kāraṇena asamudācāravasena anuppannassa tassa dasavidhassāpi saṃyojanassa uppādo hoti, tañca kāraṇaṃ pajānāti. <hi rend="bold">Yathā ca uppannassā</hi>ti <pb ed="P" n="3.0785"/> appahīnaṭṭhena pana samudācāravasena vā uppannassa tassa dasavidhassāpi saṃyojanassa yena kāraṇena pahānaṃ hoti, tañca kāraṇaṃ pajānāti. <hi rend="bold">Yathā ca pahīnassā</hi>ti tadaṅgavikkhambhanappahānavasena pahīnassāpi tassa dasavidhassa saṃyojanassa <pb ed="M" n="2.0375"/> yena kāraṇena āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti, tañca pajānāti. Kena kāraṇena panassa āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti? Diṭṭhivicikicchāsīlabbataparāmāsaissāmacchariyabhedassa tāva pañcavidhassa saṃyojanassa sotāpattimaggena āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti. Kāmarāgapaṭighasaṃyojanadvayassa oḷārikassa sakadāgāmimaggena, aṇusahagatassa anāgāmimaggena, mānabhavarāgāvijjāsaṃyojanattayassa arahattamaggena āyatiṃ anuppādo hoti. <hi rend="bold">Sotañca pajānāti sadde cā</hi>tiādīsupi eseva nayo. Apicettha <hi rend="bold">āyatanakathā</hi> vitthārato visuddhimagge āyatananiddese vuttanayeneva veditabbā.</p> <hi rend="bold">Yathā ca anuppannassā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Yathā ca uppannassā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0785"/> <hi rend="bold">Yathā ca pahīnassā</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0375"/> <hi rend="bold">Sotañca pajānāti sadde cā</hi> <hi rend="bold">āyatanakathā</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi>ti evaṃ ajjhattikāyatanapariggaṇhanena attano vā dhammesu bāhirāyatanapariggaṇhanena parassa vā dhammesu, kālena vā attano, kālena vā parassa dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati. Samudayavayā panettha ‘‘avijjāsamudayā cakkhusamudayo’’ti rūpāyatanassa rūpakkhandhe, arūpāyatanesu manāyatanassa viññāṇakkhandhe, dhammāyatanassa sesakkhandhesu vuttanayena nīharitabbā. Lokuttaradhammā na gahetabbā. Ito paraṃ vuttanayameva. Kevalañhi idha āyatanapariggāhikā sati dukkhasaccanti evaṃ yojanaṃ katvā āyatanapariggāhakassa bhikkhuno niyyānamukhaṃ veditabbaṃ. Sesaṃ tādisamevāti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi> <p rend="centre">Āyatanapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.</p> <p rend="subhead">Bojjhaṅgapabbavaṇṇanā</p>
384. Thus, having divided the contemplation of phenomena (dhammānupassanā) by way of the five aggregates (pañcakkhandha), now, in order to divide it by way of the sense-bases (*āyatana*), [he] said, “*puna capara*,” and so forth.

Therein, "*chasu ajjhattikabāhiresu āyatanesu*": the eye (cakkhu), the ear (sotaṃ), the nose (ghānaṃ), the tongue (jivhā), the body (kāyo), and the mind (mano)—these are the six internal (*ajjhattika*) sense-bases; form (*rūpaṃ*), sound (*saddo*), smell (*gandho*), taste (*raso*), tactile object (*phoṭṭhabbo*), and mental object (*dhammo*)—these are the six external (*bāhira*) sense-bases.

"*cakkhuñca pajānāti*": he understands the eye faculty (*cakkhupasādaṃ*) according to its distinct function and characteristic (yāthāvasarasalakkhaṇavasena).

"*rūpe ca pajānāti*": he understands external (bahiddhā) form (*rūpañca*) which is caused by the four conditions (catusamuṭṭhānikarūpañca) according to its distinct function and characteristic (*yāthāvasarasalakkhaṇavasena*).

"*yañca tadubhayaṃ paṭicca uppajjati saṃyojana*": “the fetter (*saṃyojana*) that arises dependent on both (ubhayaṃ paṭicca)” means that which arises dependent on both the eye (cakkhuṃ) and forms (rūpe).

The fetter of sense-desire (kāmarāgasaṃyojanaṃ), resentment (paṭigha), pride (māna), speculative theory (diṭṭhi), doubt (vicikicchā), belief in rites and ceremonies (sīlabbataparāmāsa), the desire to go on existing (bhavarāga), envy (issā), avarice (macchariya), and the fetter of ignorance (avijjāsaṃyojanaṃ)— thus, the tenfold (dasavidhaṃ) fetter (saṃyojanaṃ) arises, and he understands that according to its distinct function and characteristic (*yāthāvasarasalakkhaṇavasena pajānāti*).

How does that arise? In the eye-door, when one savors (assādayato) and delights in (abhinandato) an agreeable (iṭṭha) object (ārammaṇaṃ) that has come into the range of perception (āpāthagataṃ) with the enjoyment of sense-desire (kāmassādavasena), the fetter of sense-desire (*kāmarāgasaṃyojanaṃ*) arises.

When one is angry (kujjhato) at a disagreeable (aniṭṭha) object (ārammaṇe), the fetter of resentment (paṭighasaṃyojanaṃ) arises.

The fetter of pride (mānasaṃyojanaṃ) arises in him who thinks (maññato): “No one but me (ṭhapetvā maṃ ko añño) is able to wisely consider (vibhāvetuṃ samattho atthī) this object (etaṃ ārammaṇaṃ)."

The fetter of speculative theory (diṭṭhisaṃyojanaṃ) arises in him who takes (gaṇhato) this material form (rūpārammaṇaṃ) to be permanent (niccaṃ) and everlasting (dhuvaṃ).

The fetter of doubt (vicikicchāsaṃyojanaṃ) arises in him who thinks doubtfully (vicikicchato): “Is this material form (*etaṃ rūpārammaṇaṃ*) a being (satto nu kho), or a being’s (sattassa nu kho)?"

The fetter of the desire to go on existing (bhavarāgasaṃyojanaṃ) arises in him who wishes for continued existence (bhavaṃ patthentassa) thinking: "To be sure (vata), in a favorable state of existence (sampattibhave) this (idaṃ) will become easy of access (sulabhaṃ jāta)."

The fetter of rites and ceremonies (sīlabbataparāmāsasaṃyojanaṃ) arises in him who undertakes to perform rites and ceremonies (sīlabbataṃ samādiyantassa) thinking: “In the future (āyatimpi) it will be possible (sakkā laddhu) to obtain such an object as this (evarūpaṃ sīlabbataṃ samādiyitvā) by taking up the observance of rites and ceremonies."

The fetter of envy (issāsaṃyojanaṃ) arises in him who contemplates grudgingly (usūyato): "Should no others (aññe na labheyyu) get that material form (taṃ rūpārammaṇaṃ), it would be good, indeed (aho vata)."

The fetter of avarice (macchariyasaṃyojanaṃ) arises in one who stints for another (aññassa maccharāyato) the material form (rūpārammaṇaṃ) belonging to one (attanā laddhaṃ).

The fetter of ignorance (*avijjāsaṃyojanaṃ*) arises, with all sensuous passion and the like (sabbeheva sahajātaaññāṇavasena), by way of the relation of conascent nescience.

"*yathā ca anuppannassā*": He understands the cause (kāraṇena) owing to which the arising (uppādo hoti) of the non-arisen (anuppannassa) fetter (saṃyojanassa), even of that tenfold one (tassa dasavidhassāpi), had not occurred owing to some cause of non-occurrence (asamudācāravasena).

"*yathā ca uppannassā*": He understands the cause (kāraṇena) owing to which the abandoning (pahānaṃ hoti) of the arisen (uppannassa) fetter (saṃyojanassa), even of that tenfold one (tassa dasavidhassāpi), come to be through occurrence or through previous non-abandoning (appahīnaṭṭhena pana samudācāravasena vā).

"*yathā ca pahīnassā*": He understands the cause (kāraṇena) owing to which the non-arising (anuppādo hoti) in the future (āyatiṃ) of that (tassa) fetter (*saṃyojanassa*), even of that tenfold one (dasavidhassa), that is abandoned (pahīnassāpi) by way of temporary suppression of its constituent parts (tadaṅgavikkhambhanappahānavasena).

By what cause (kena kāraṇena) is its non-arising (anuppādo hoti) in the future (āyatiṃ)? The non-arising (anuppādo hoti) in the future (āyatiṃ) of the fivefold fetter (pañcavidhassa saṃyojanassa) of speculative theory (diṭṭhi), doubt (vicikicchā), belief in rites and ceremonies (sīlabbataparāmāsa), envy (issā), and avarice (macchariyabhedassa) is by way of the path of stream-winning (*sotāpattimaggena*).

The non-arising (anuppādo hoti) in the future (āyatiṃ) of the twofold (dvayassa) fetter (saṃyojanassa) of sensuous passion (kāmarāga) and resentment (paṭigha) of a gross kind (oḷārikassa) is by way of the path of once-returning (*sakadāgāmimaggena*), [and the non-arising] of the subtle residuum which goes along with it (aṇusahagatassa) is by way of the path of never-returning (*anāgāmimaggena*), [and the non-arising] in the future of the threefold (ttayassa) fetter (saṃyojanassa) of pride (māna), the desire to go on existing (bhavarāga), and ignorance (avijjā) is by way of the path of arahantship (*arahattamaggena*).

"*sotañca pajānāti sadde cā*", etc.: The same is the method of exposition in the case of the ear (sotañca) and sounds (sadde).

Moreover, here (*ettha*) the discourse on the sense-bases (*āyatanakathā*) should be understood in detail (vitthārato) just by the method already stated (vuttanayeneva) in the section on the sense-bases (*āyatananiddese*) in the *Visuddhimagga*.

"*yathā ca anuppannassā yathā ca uppannassā yathā ca pahīnassā sotañca pajānāti sadde cā āyatanakathā*": "*iti ajjhattaṃ vā*": Thus (*evaṃ*), contemplating phenomena (dhammānupassī) in phenomena, either internally (*ajjhattikāyatanapariggaṇhanena attano vā dhammesu*) by laying hold of the internal sense-bases, or externally (bāhirāyatanapariggaṇhanena parassa vā dhammesu) by laying hold of the external sense-bases, or in both ways (kālena vā attano, kālena vā parassa dhammesu).

Here (*ettha*) origination and dissolution (samudayavayā) should be brought forward and connected (nīharitabbā) according to the method already stated (vuttanayena) in the aggregate of materiality (*rūpakkhandhe*) for the sense-base of material form (*rūpāyatanassa*) [with the statement], “From the origin of ignorance (avijjāsamudayā) [comes] the origin of the eye (cakkhusamudayo),” in the aggregate of consciousness (*viññāṇakkhandhe*) for the mental sense-base (*manāyatanassa*), and in the remaining aggregates (*sesakkhandhesu*) for the sense-base of mental objects (*dhammāyatanassa*).

The supramundane states (*lokuttaradhammā*) should not be taken (na gahetabbā).

From here on (ito paraṃ) the exposition is just according to the manner already stated (vuttanayameva).

Indeed (kevalañhi), here (idha), the mindfulness which lays hold of the sense-bases (*āyatanapariggāhikā sati*) is the Truth of Suffering (dukkhasaccaṃ). Having interpreted (katvā) thus (evaṃ), the portal of deliverance (*niyyānamukhaṃ*) should be known (veditabbaṃ) for the bhikkhu (bhikkhuno) who lays hold of the sense-bases (*āyatanapariggāhakassa*).

The rest (*sesaṃ*) is just like that (tādisameva).

"*iti ajjhattaṃ vā*": The section on the sense-bases (*āyatanapabbaṃ*) is finished (niṭṭhitaṃ).

Explanation of the section on the enlightenment factors (bojjhaṅgapabbavaṇṇanā).
385
<p n="385" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">385</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati sattasu bojjhaṅgesu. Kathañca pana, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati sattasu bojjhaṅgesu? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu santaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ satisambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ satisambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ satisambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ satisambojjhaṅgo’ti <pb ed="V" n="2.0226"/> pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa satisambojjhaṅgassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa satisambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya pāripūrī hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Santaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa <pb ed="M" n="2.0242"/> dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya pāripūrī hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0242"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Santaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ vīriyasambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ vīriyasambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ vīriyasambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ vīriyasambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa vīriyasambojjhaṅgassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa vīriyasambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya pāripūrī hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Santaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ pītisambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ pītisambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ pītisambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ pītisambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa pītisambojjhaṅgassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa pītisambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya pāripūrī hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Santaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0304"/> vā ajjhattaṃ passaddhisambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ passaddhisambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ passaddhisambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ passaddhisambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa passaddhisambojjhaṅgassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa passaddhisambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya pāripūrī hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0304"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Santaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ samādhisambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ samādhisambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ samādhisambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ samādhisambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa samādhisambojjhaṅgassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa samādhisambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya pāripūrī hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Santaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ upekkhāsambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘atthi me ajjhattaṃ upekkhāsambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti <pb ed="V" n="2.0227"/>, asantaṃ vā ajjhattaṃ upekkhāsambojjhaṅgaṃ ‘natthi me ajjhattaṃ upekkhāsambojjhaṅgo’ti pajānāti, yathā ca anuppannassa <pb ed="T" n="2.0340"/> upekkhāsambojjhaṅgassa uppādo hoti tañca pajānāti, yathā ca uppannassa upekkhāsambojjhaṅgassa bhāvanāya pāripūrī hoti tañca pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0227"/> <pb ed="T" n="2.0340"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Iti ajjhattaṃ vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati, bahiddhā vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati, ajjhattabahiddhā vā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati. Samudayadhammānupassī <pb ed="M" n="2.0243"/> vā dhammesu viharati, vayadhammānupassī vā dhammesu viharati, samudayavayadhammānupassī vā dhammesu viharati ‘atthi dhammā’ti vā panassa sati paccupaṭṭhitā hoti yāvadeva ñāṇamattāya paṭissatimattāya anissito ca viharati, na ca kiñci loke upādiyati. Evampi kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati sattasu bojjhaṅgesu.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0243"/> <p rend="centre">Bojjhaṅgapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ. <note>bojjhaṅgapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ, paṭhamabhāṇavāraṃ (syā.)</note></p> <note>bojjhaṅgapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ, paṭhamabhāṇavāraṃ (syā.)</note> <p rend="subhead">Dhammānupassanā saccapabbaṃ</p>
385. “Again, monks, a monk dwells contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects with regard to the seven factors of enlightenment ( *saptasu bodhyaṅgeṣu*).

And how, monks, does a monk dwell contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects with regard to the seven factors of enlightenment? Here, monks, if the mindfulness enlightenment factor ( *smṛtisambodhyanga*) is present in himself, he understands: ‘There is the mindfulness enlightenment factor in me’; or if the mindfulness enlightenment factor is absent in himself, he understands: ‘There is no mindfulness enlightenment factor in me’; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen mindfulness enlightenment factor, and how the arisen mindfulness enlightenment factor comes to fulfilment by development.

“If the investigation-of-states enlightenment factor ( *dharmavicayasambodhyanga*) is present in himself, he understands: ‘There is the investigation-of-states enlightenment factor in me’; or if the investigation-of-states enlightenment factor is absent in himself, he understands: ‘There is no investigation-of-states enlightenment factor in me’; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen investigation-of-states enlightenment factor, and how the arisen investigation-of-states enlightenment factor comes to fulfilment by development.

“If the energy enlightenment factor ( *vīryasambodhyanga*) is present in himself, he understands: ‘There is the energy enlightenment factor in me’; or if the energy enlightenment factor is absent in himself, he understands: ‘There is no energy enlightenment factor in me’; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen energy enlightenment factor, and how the arisen energy enlightenment factor comes to fulfilment by development.

“If the rapture enlightenment factor ( *prītisambodhyanga*) is present in himself, he understands: ‘There is the rapture enlightenment factor in me’; or if the rapture enlightenment factor is absent in himself, he understands: ‘There is no rapture enlightenment factor in me’; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen rapture enlightenment factor, and how the arisen rapture enlightenment factor comes to fulfilment by development.

“If the tranquility enlightenment factor ( *praśrabdhisambodhyanga*) is present in himself, he understands: ‘There is the tranquility enlightenment factor in me’; or if the tranquility enlightenment factor is absent in himself, he understands: ‘There is no tranquility enlightenment factor in me’; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen tranquility enlightenment factor, and how the arisen tranquility enlightenment factor comes to fulfilment by development.

“If the concentration enlightenment factor ( *samādhisambodhyanga*) is present in himself, he understands: ‘There is the concentration enlightenment factor in me’; or if the concentration enlightenment factor is absent in himself, he understands: ‘There is no concentration enlightenment factor in me’; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen concentration enlightenment factor, and how the arisen concentration enlightenment factor comes to fulfilment by development.

“If the equanimity enlightenment factor ( *upekṣāsambodhyanga*) is present in himself, he understands: ‘There is the equanimity enlightenment factor in me’; or if the equanimity enlightenment factor is absent in himself, he understands: ‘There is no equanimity enlightenment factor in me’; and he also understands how there comes to be the arising of the unarisen equanimity enlightenment factor, and how the arisen equanimity enlightenment factor comes to fulfilment by development.

“In this way, he dwells contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects internally, or he dwells contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects externally, or he dwells contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects both internally and externally. He dwells contemplating arising phenomena in mind-objects, he dwells contemplating vanishing phenomena in mind-objects, he dwells contemplating both arising and vanishing phenomena in mind-objects. Or else mindfulness that ‘there are mind-objects ( *dhammā* )’ is established in him to the extent necessary for bare knowledge and mindfulness. And he dwells independent, not clinging to anything in the world. That is how, monks, a monk dwells contemplating mind-objects as mind-objects with regard to the seven factors of enlightenment.

The chapter on the Factors of Enlightenment is finished.

The section on Contemplation of Mind-Objects Concerning the Truths.
<p n="385" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">385</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Evaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0338"/> cha ajjhattikabāhirāyatanavasena dhammānupassanaṃ vibhajitvā idāni bojjhaṅgavasena vibhajituṃ <hi rend="bold">puna capara</hi>ntiādimāha. Tattha <hi rend="bold">bojjhaṅgesū</hi>ti bujjhanakasattassa aṅgesu. <hi rend="bold">Santa</hi>nti paṭilābhavasena saṃvijjamānaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Satisambojjhaṅga</hi>nti satisaṅkhātaṃ sambojjhaṅgaṃ. Ettha hi sambujjhati āraddhavipassakato paṭṭhāya yogāvacaroti <hi rend="bold">sambodhi.</hi> Yāya vā so satiādikāya sattadhammasāmaggiyā sambujjhati <pb ed="P" n="3.0786"/> kilesaniddāto uṭṭhāti, saccāni vā paṭivijjhati, sā dhammasāmaggī sambodhi. Tassa sambodhissa, tassā vā sambodhiyā aṅganti <hi rend="bold">sambojjhaṅgaṃ.</hi> Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘satisaṅkhātaṃ sambojjhaṅga’’nti. Sesasambojjhaṅgesupi imināva nayena vacanattho veditabbo.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Asanta</hi>nti <pb ed="M" n="2.0376"/> appaṭilābhavasena avijjamānaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Yathā ca anupannassā</hi>tiādīsu pana satisambojjhaṅgassa tāva ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, satisambojjhaṅgaṭṭhānīyā dhammā, tattha yonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannassa vā satisambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya, uppannassa vā satisambojjhaṅgassa bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya bhāvanāya pāripūriyā saṃvattatī’’ti (saṃ. ni. 5.232) evaṃ uppādo hoti. Tattha satiyeva satisambojjhaṅgaṭṭhānīyā dhammā. Yonisomanasikāro vuttalakkhaṇoyeva. Taṃ tattha bahulaṃ pavattayato satisambojjhaṅgo uppajjati.</p> <hi rend="bold">Asanta</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0376"/> <hi rend="bold">Yathā ca anupannassā</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Apica cattāro dhammā satisambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya saṃvattanti satisampajaññaṃ muṭṭhassatipuggalaparivajjanatā upaṭṭhitassatipuggalasevanatā tadadhimuttatāti. Abhikkantādīsu hi sattasu ṭhānesu satisampajaññena, bhattanikkhittakākasadise muṭṭhassatipuggale parivajjanena, tissadattattheraabhayattherasadise upaṭṭhitassatipuggale sevanena, ṭhānanisajjādīsu satisamuṭṭhāpanatthaṃ ninnapoṇapabbhāracittatāya ca satisambojjhaṅgo uppajjati. Evaṃ catūhi kāraṇehi uppannassa panassa arahattamaggena bhāvanāpāripūri hotīti pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgassa</hi> pana ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, kusalākusalā dhammā…pe… kaṇhasukkasappaṭibhāgā dhammā, tattha yonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannassa vā dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya, uppannassa vā dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgassa bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya bhāvanāya pāripūriyā saṃvattatī’’ti evaṃ uppādo hoti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgassa</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Apica <pb ed="V" n="2.0339"/> satta dhammā dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya saṃvattanti paripucchakatā vatthuvisadakiriyā <pb ed="P" n="3.0787"/> indriyasamattapaṭipādanā duppaññapuggalaparivajjanā paññavantapuggalasevanā gambhīrañāṇacariyapaccavekkhaṇā tadadhimuttatāti. Tattha <hi rend="bold">paripucchakatā</hi>ti khandhadhātuāyatanaindriyabalabojjhaṅgamaggaṅgajhānaṅgasamathavipassanānaṃ atthasannissitaparipucchābahulatā. <hi rend="bold">Vatthuvisadakiriyā</hi>ti ajjhattikabāhirānaṃ vatthūnaṃ visadabhāvakaraṇaṃ. Yadā hissa kesanakhalomāni dīghāni honti, sarīraṃ vā ussannadosañceva sedamalamakkhitañca, tadā ajjhattikaṃ vatthu avisadaṃ hoti aparisuddhaṃ. Yadā pana cīvaraṃ jiṇṇaṃ kiliṭṭhaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0377"/> duggandhaṃ hoti, senāsanaṃ vā uklāpaṃ, tadā bāhiravatthu avisadaṃ hoti aparisuddhaṃ. Tasmā kesādichedāpanena uddhaṃvirecanaadhovirecanādīhi sarīrasallahukabhāvakaraṇena ucchādananahāpanena ca ajjhattikavatthu visadaṃ kātabbaṃ. Sūcikammadhovanarajanaparibhaṇḍakaraṇādīhi bāhiravatthu visadaṃ kātabbaṃ. Etasmiñhi ajjhattikabāhire vatthumhi avisade uppannesu cittacetasikesu ñāṇampi avisadaṃ hoti aparisuddhaṃ aparisuddhāni dīpakapallavaṭṭitelāni nissāya uppannadīpasikhāya obhāso viya. Visade pana ajjhattikabāhire vatthumhi uppannesu cittacetasikesu ñāṇampi visadaṃ hoti parisuddhāni dīpakapallavaṭṭitelāni nissāya uppannadīpasikhāya obhāso viya. Tena vuttaṃ ‘‘vatthuvisadakiriyā dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya saṃvattatī’’ti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0339"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0787"/> <hi rend="bold">paripucchakatā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Vatthuvisadakiriyā</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0377"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Indriyasamattapaṭipādanā</hi> nāma saddhādīnaṃ indriyānaṃ samabhāvakaraṇaṃ. Sace hissa saddhindriyaṃ balavaṃ hoti, itarāni mandāni, tato vīriyindriyaṃ paggahakiccaṃ, satindriyaṃ upaṭṭhānakiccaṃ, samādhindriyaṃ avikkhepakiccaṃ, paññindriyaṃ dassanakiccaṃ kātuṃ na sakkoti. Tasmā taṃ dhammasabhāvapaccavekkhaṇena vā, yathā vā manasikaroto balavaṃ jātaṃ, tathā amanasikārena hāpetabbaṃ. Vakkalittheravatthu cettha nidassanaṃ. Sace pana vīriyindriyaṃ balavaṃ hoti, atha saddhindriyaṃ adhimokkhakiccaṃ kātuṃ na sakkoti, na itarāni itarakiccabhedaṃ. Tasmā taṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0788"/> passaddhādibhāvanāya hāpetabbaṃ. Tatrāpi soṇattherassa vatthu dassetabbaṃ. Evaṃ sesesupi ekassa balavabhāve sati itaresaṃ attano kiccesu asamatthatā veditabbā.</p> <hi rend="bold">Indriyasamattapaṭipādanā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0788"/> <p rend="bodytext">Visesato panettha saddhāpaññānaṃ samādhivīriyānañca samataṃ pasaṃsanti. Balavasaddho hi mandapañño mudhappasanno hoti, avatthusmiṃ pasīdati. Balavapañño mandasaddho kerāṭikapakkhaṃ bhajati, bhesajjasamuṭṭhito viya rogo atekiccho hoti. Cittuppādamatteneva kusalaṃ hotīti atidhāvitvā dānādīni akaronto niraye uppajjati. Ubhinnaṃ samatāya vatthusmiṃyeva pasīdati. Balavasamādhiṃ pana mandavīriyaṃ samādhissa kosajjapakkhattā kosajjaṃ abhibhavati. Balavavīriyaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0340"/> mandasamādhiṃ vīriyassa uddhaccapakkhattā uddhaccaṃ abhibhavati. Samādhi pana vīriyena saṃyojito kosajje patituṃ na labhati, vīriyaṃ samādhinā saṃyojitaṃ uddhacce patituṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0378"/> na labhati. Tasmā tadubhayaṃ samaṃ kātabbaṃ. Ubhayasamatāya hi appanā hoti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0340"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0378"/> <p rend="bodytext">Apica samādhikammikassa balavatīpi saddhā vaṭṭati. Evaṃ saddahanto okappento appanaṃ pāpuṇissati. Samādhipaññāsu pana samādhikammikassa ekaggatā balavatī vaṭṭati. Evañhi so appanaṃ pāpuṇāti. Vipassanākammikassa paññā balavatī vaṭṭati. Evañhi so lakkhaṇapaṭivedhaṃ pāpuṇāti. Ubhinnaṃ pana samatāyapi appanā hotiyeva. Sati pana sabbattha balavatī vaṭṭati. Sati hi cittaṃ uddhaccapakkhikānaṃ saddhāvīriyapaññānaṃ vasena uddhaccapātato, kosajjapakkhikena ca samādhinā kosajjapātato rakkhati. Tasmā sā loṇadhūpanaṃ viya sabbabyañjanesu, sabbakammikaamacco viya ca, sabbarājakiccesu sabbattha icchitabbā. Tenāha – ‘‘sati ca pana sabbatthikā vuttā bhagavatā, kiṃ kāraṇā? Cittañhi satipaṭisaraṇaṃ, ārakkhapaccupaṭṭhānā ca sati, na vinā satiyā cittassa paggahaniggaho hotī’’ ti. <hi rend="bold">Duppaññapuggalaparivajjanā</hi> nāma khandhādibhede anogāḷhapaññānaṃ dummedhapuggalānaṃ ārakā parivajjanaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Paññavantapuggalasevanā</hi> nāma samapaññāsalakkhaṇapariggāhikāya <pb ed="P" n="3.0789"/> udayabbayapaññāya samannāgatapuggalasevanā. <hi rend="bold">Gambhīrañāṇacariyapaccavekkhaṇā</hi> nāma gambhīresu khandhādīsu pavattāya gambhīrapaññāya pabhedapaccavekkhaṇā. <hi rend="bold">Tadadhimuttatā</hi> nāma ṭhānanisajjādīsu dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgasamuṭṭhāpanatthaṃ ninnapoṇapabbhāracittatā. Evaṃ uppannassa pannassa arahattamaggena bhāvanāpāripūri hotīti pajānāti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Duppaññapuggalaparivajjanā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Paññavantapuggalasevanā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0789"/> <hi rend="bold">Gambhīrañāṇacariyapaccavekkhaṇā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Tadadhimuttatā</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Vīriyasambojjhaṅgassa</hi> ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, ārambhadhātu nikkamadhātu parakkamadhātu, tattha yonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannassa vā vīriyasambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya, uppannassa vā vīriyasambojjhaṅgassa bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya bhāvanāya pāripūriyā saṃvattatī’’ti evaṃ uppādo hoti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Vīriyasambojjhaṅgassa</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Apica ekādasa dhammā vīriyasambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya saṃvattanti apāyabhayapaccavekkhaṇatā ānisaṃsadassāvitā gamanavīthipaccavekkhaṇatā piṇḍapātāpacāyanatā dāyajjamahattapaccavekkhaṇatā satthumahattapaccavekkhaṇatā jātimahattapaccavekkhaṇatā sabrahmacārimahattapaccavekkhaṇatā kusītapuggalaparivajjanatā āraddhavīriyapuggalasevanatā tadadhimuttatāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha <pb ed="V" n="2.0341"/><pb ed="M" n="2.0379"/> nirayesu pañcavidhabandhanakammakāraṇato paṭṭhāya mahādukkhānubhavanakālepi, tiracchānayoniyaṃ jālakhipanakumīnādīhi gahitakālepi, pājanakaṇṭakādippahāratunnassa sakaṭavahanādikālepi, pettivisaye anekānipi vassasahassāni ekaṃ buddhantarampi khuppipāsāhi āturībhūtakālepi, kālakañcikaasuresu saṭṭhihatthaasītihatthappamāṇena aṭṭhicammamatteneva attabhāvena vātātapādidukkhānubhavanakālepi na sakkā vīriyasambojjhaṅgaṃ uppādetuṃ, ayameva te bhikkhu kālo vīriyakaraṇāyāti evaṃ apāyabhayaṃ paccavekkhantassāpi vīriyasambojjhaṅgo uppajjati.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0341"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0379"/> <p rend="bodytext">Na sakkā kusītena navalokuttaradhammaṃ laddhuṃ, āraddhavīriyeneva sakkā ayamānisaṃso vīriyassāti <pb ed="P" n="3.0790"/> evaṃ ānisaṃsadassāvinopi uppajjati. Sabbabuddhapaccekabuddhamahāsāvakehi te gatamaggo gantabbo, so ca na sakkā kusītena gantunti evaṃ gamanavīthiṃ paccavekkhantassāpi uppajjati. Ye taṃ piṇḍapātādīhi upaṭṭhahanti, ime te manussā neva ñātakā, na dāsakammakarā, nāpi taṃ nissāya jīvissāmāti te paṇītāni cīvarādīni denti. Atha kho attano kārānaṃ mahapphalataṃ paccāsīsamānā denti. Satthārāpi ‘‘ayaṃ ime paccaye paribhuñjitvā kāyadaḷhībahulo sukhaṃ viharissatī’’ti na evaṃ sampassatā tuyhaṃ paccayā anuññātā. Atha kho ‘‘ayaṃ ime paribhuñjamāno samaṇadhammaṃ katvā vaṭṭadukkhato muccissatī’’ti te paccayā anuññātā, so dāni tvaṃ kusīto viharanto na taṃ piṇḍaṃ apacāyissati. Āraddhavīriyasseva hi piṇḍapātāpacāyanaṃ nāma hotīti evaṃ piṇḍapātāpacāyanaṃ paccavekkhantassāpi uppajjati ayyamittattherassa viya.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0790"/> <p rend="bodytext">Thero kira kassakaleṇe nāma paṭivasati. Tassa ca gocaragāme ekā mahāupāsikā theraṃ puttaṃ katvā paṭijaggati. Sā ekadivasaṃ araññaṃ gacchantī dhītaraṃ āha – ‘‘amma, asukasmiṃ ṭhāne purāṇataṇḍulā, asukasmiṃ sappi, asukasmiṃ khīraṃ, asukasmiṃ phāṇitaṃ, tava bhātikassa ayyamittassa āgatakāle bhattaṃ pacitvā khīrasappiphāṇitehi saddhiṃ dehi, tvañca bhuñjeyyāsi. Ahaṃ pana hiyyo pakkapārivāsikabhattaṃ kañjiyena bhuttāmhī’’ti. Divā kiṃ bhuñjissasi ammā,ti? Sākapaṇṇaṃ pakkhipitvā kaṇataṇḍulehi ambilayāguṃ pacitvā ṭhapehi ammā,ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Thero <pb ed="M" n="2.0380"/> cīvaraṃ pārupitvā pattaṃ nīharantova taṃ saddaṃ sutvā attānaṃ ovadi ‘‘mahāupāsikā kira kañjiyena pārivāsikabhattaṃ bhuñji, divāpi kaṇapaṇṇambilayāguṃ bhuñjissati <pb ed="V" n="2.0342"/>, tuyhaṃ atthāya pana purāṇataṇḍulādīni ācikkhati, taṃ nissāya kho panesā neva khettaṃ na vatthuṃ na bhattaṃ na vatthaṃ paccāsīsati, tisso pana sampattiyo patthayamānā deti, tvaṃ etissā tā sampattiyo dātuṃ sakkhissasi, na sakkhissasīti, ayaṃ kho pana piṇḍapāto tayā sarāgena sadosena <pb ed="P" n="3.0791"/> samohena na sakkā gaṇhitu’’nti pattaṃ thavikāya pakkhipitvā gaṇṭhikaṃ muñcitvā nivattitvā kassakaleṇameva gantvā pattaṃ heṭṭhāmañce cīvaraṃ cīvaravaṃse ṭhapetvā ‘‘arahattaṃ apāpuṇitvā na nikkhamissāmī’’ti vīriyaṃ adhiṭṭhahitvā nisīdi. Dīgharattaṃ appamatto hutvā nivutthabhikkhu vipassanaṃ vaḍḍhetvā purebhattameva arahattaṃ patvā vikasamānamiva padumaṃ mahākhīṇāsavo sitaṃ karontova nisīdi. Leṇadvāre rukkhamhi adhivatthā devatā –</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0380"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0342"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0791"/> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Namo te purisājañña, namo te purisuttama;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Yassa te āsavā khīṇā, dakkhiṇeyyosi mārisā’’ti. –</p> <p rend="bodytext">Udānaṃ udānetvā ‘‘bhante, piṇḍāya paviṭṭhānaṃ tumhādisānaṃ arahantānaṃ bhikkhaṃ datvā mahallakitthiyo dukkhā muccissantī’’ti āha. Thero uṭṭhahitvā dvāraṃ vivaritvā kālaṃ olokento ‘‘pātoyevā’’ti ñatvā pattacīvaramādāya gāmaṃ pāvisi.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Dārikāpi bhattaṃ sampādetvā ‘‘idāni me bhātā āgamissati, idāni āgamissatī’’ti dvāraṃ olokayamānā nisīdi. Sā there gharadvāraṃ sampatte pattaṃ gahetvā sappiphāṇitayojitassa khīrapiṇḍapātassa pūretvā hatthe ṭhapesi. Thero ‘‘sukhaṃ hotū’’ti anumodanaṃ katvā pakkāmi. Sāpi taṃ olokayamānā aṭṭhāsi. Therassa hi tadā ativiya parisuddho chavivaṇṇo ahosi, vippasannāni indriyāni, mukhaṃ bandhanā pavuttatālapakkaṃ viya ativiya virocittha.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Mahāupāsikā araññā āgantvā ‘‘kiṃ, amma, bhātiko te āgato’’ti pucchi. Sā sabbaṃ taṃ pavattiṃ ārocesi. Upāsikā ‘‘ajja <pb ed="M" n="2.0381"/> mama puttassa pabbajitakiccaṃ matthakaṃ patta’’nti ñatvā ‘‘abhiramati te, amma, bhātā buddhasāsane, na ukkaṇṭhatī’’ti āha.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0381"/> <p rend="bodytext">Mahantaṃ kho panetaṃ satthudāyajjaṃ yadidaṃ satta ariyadhanāni nāma, taṃ na sakkā kusītena gahetuṃ. Yathā hi vippaṭipannaṃ puttaṃ mātāpitaro ‘‘ayaṃ amhākaṃ aputto’’ti <pb ed="P" n="3.0792"/> paribāhiraṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0343"/> karonti, so tesaṃ accayena dāyajjaṃ na labhati, evaṃ kusītopi idaṃ ariyadhanadāyajjaṃ na labhati, āraddhavīriyova labhatīti <hi rend="bold">dāyajjamahattaṃ</hi> paccavekkhatopi uppajjati.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0792"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0343"/> <hi rend="bold">dāyajjamahattaṃ</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Mahā kho pana te satthā, satthuno hi te mātukucchismiṃ paṭisandhigaṇhanakālepi abhinikkhamanepi abhisambodhiyampi dhammacakkappavattanayamakapāṭihāriyadevorohanaāyusaṅkhāravossajjanesupi parinibbānakālepi dasasahassilokadhātu akampittha, yuttaṃ nu te evarūpassa satthu sāsane pabbajitvā kusītena bhavitunti evaṃ <hi rend="bold">satthumahattaṃ</hi> paccavekkhatopi uppajjati.</p> <hi rend="bold">satthumahattaṃ</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Jātiyāpi tvaṃ idāni na lāmakajātiko, asambhinnāya mahāsammatapaveṇiyā āgataukkākarājavaṃse jātosi, suddhodanamahārājassa ca mahāmāyādeviyā ca nattā, rāhulabhaddassa kaniṭṭho, tayā nāma evarūpena jinaputtena hutvā na yuttaṃ kusītena viharitunti evaṃ <hi rend="bold">jātimahattaṃ</hi> paccavekkhatopi uppajjati.</p> <hi rend="bold">jātimahattaṃ</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Sāriputtamahāmoggallānā ceva asīti ca mahāsāvakā vīriyeneva lokuttaradhammaṃ paṭivijjhiṃsu, tvaṃ etesaṃ sabrahmacārīnaṃ maggaṃ paṭipajjasi, na paṭipajjasīti evaṃ <hi rend="bold">sabrahmacārimahattaṃ</hi> paccavekkhatopi uppajjati.</p> <hi rend="bold">sabrahmacārimahattaṃ</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Kucchiṃ pūretvā ṭhitaajagarasadise vissaṭṭhakāyikacetasikavīriye kusītapuggale parivajjantassāpi āraddhavīriye pahitatte puggale sevantassāpi ṭhānanisajjādīsu vīriyuppādanatthaṃ ninnapoṇapabbhāracittassāpi uppajjati. Evaṃ uppannassa panassa arahattamaggena bhāvanāpāripūri hotīti pajānāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Pītisambojjhaṅgassa</hi> ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, pītisambojjhaṅgaṭṭhānīyā dhammā, tattha yonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannassa vā pītisambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya, uppannassa vā pītisambojjhaṅgassa bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya bhāvanāya pāripūriyā saṃvattatī’’ti evaṃ uppādo hoti <pb ed="M" n="2.0382"/>. Tattha pītiyeva pītisambojjhaṅgaṭṭhānīyā dhammā nāma. Tassā uppādakamanasikāro yonisomanasikāro nāma.</p> <hi rend="bold">Pītisambojjhaṅgassa</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0382"/> <p rend="bodytext">Apica ekādasa dhammā pītisambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya saṃvattanti buddhānussati, dhamma, saṅgha <pb ed="V" n="2.0344"/>, sīla, cāga, devatānussati <pb ed="P" n="3.0793"/> upasamānussati lūkhapuggalaparivajjanatā siniddhapuggalasevanatā pasādanīyasuttantapaccavekkhaṇatā tadadhimuttatāti. Buddhaguṇe anussarantassāpi hi yāva upacārā sakalasarīraṃ pharamāno pītisambojjhaṅgo uppajjati, dhammasaṅghaguṇe anussarantassāpi, dīgharattaṃ akhaṇḍaṃ katvā rakkhitaṃ catupārisuddhisīlaṃ paccavekkhantassāpi, gihinopi dasasīlaṃ pañcasīlaṃ pañcavekkhantassāpi, dubbhikkhabhayādīsu paṇītabhojanaṃ sabrahmacārīnaṃ datvā ‘‘evaṃ nāma adamhā’’ti cāgaṃ paccavekkhantassāpi, gihinopi evarūpe kāle sīlavantānaṃ dinnadānaṃ paccavekkhantassāpi, yehi guṇehi samannāgatā devatā devattaṃ pattā, tathārūpānaṃ guṇānaṃ attani atthitaṃ paccavekkhantassāpi, samāpattiyā vikkhambhitā kilesā saṭṭhipi sattatipi vassāni na samudācarantīti paccavekkhantassāpi, cetiyadassanabodhidassanatheradassanesu asakkaccakiriyāya saṃsūcitalūkhabhāve buddhādīsu pasādasinehābhāvena gadrabhapiṭṭhe rajasadise lūkhapuggale parivajjantassāpi, buddhādīsu pasādabahule muducitte siniddhapuggale sevantassāpi, ratanattayaguṇaparidīpake pasādanīyasuttante paccavekkhantassāpi, ṭhānanisajjādīsu pītiuppādanatthaṃ ninnapoṇapabbhāracittassāpi uppajjati. Evaṃ uppannassa panassa arahattamaggena bhāvanāpāripūri hotīti pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0344"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0793"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Passaddhisambojjhaṅgassa</hi> ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, kāyapassaddhi cittapassaddhi, tattha yonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannassa vā passaddhisambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya, uppannassa vā passaddhisambojjhaṅgassa bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya bhāvanāya pāripūriyā saṃvattatī’’ti evaṃ uppādo hoti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Passaddhisambojjhaṅgassa</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Apica satta dhammā passaddhisambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya saṃvattanti paṇītabhojanasevanatā utusukhasevanatā iriyāpathasukhasevanatā majjhattapayogatā sāraddhakāyapuggalaparivajjanatā passaddhakāyapuggalasevanatā tadadhimuttatāti. Paṇītañhi siniddhaṃ sappāyabhojanaṃ bhuñjantassāpi, sītuṇhesu <pb ed="P" n="3.0794"/> ca utūsu ṭhānādīsu ca iriyāpathesu sappāyautuñca iriyāpathañca sevantassāpi <pb ed="M" n="2.0383"/> passaddhi uppajjati. Yo pana mahāpurisajātiko sabbautuiriyāpathakkhamo hoti, na taṃ sandhāyetaṃ vuttaṃ. Yassa sabhāgavisabhāgatā atthi, tasseva visabhāge utuiriyāpathe vajjetvā sabhāge sevantassa uppajjati. Majjhattapayogo vuccati attano ca parassa ca kammassakatāpaccavekkhaṇā. Iminā majjhattapayogena uppajjati. Yo leḍḍudaṇḍādīhi paraṃ viheṭhayamāno vicarati, evarūpaṃ sāraddhakāyaṃ puggalaṃ parivajjantassāpi, saṃyatapādapāṇiṃ passaddhakāyaṃ puggalaṃ sevantassāpi, ṭhānanisajjādīsu passaddhiuppādanatthāya ninnapoṇapabbhāracittassāpi <pb ed="V" n="2.0345"/> uppajjati. Evaṃ uppannassa panassa arahattamaggena bhāvanāpāripūri hotīti pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0794"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0383"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0345"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Samādhisambojjhaṅgassa</hi> ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, samathanimittaṃ abyagganimittaṃ, tattha yonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro, anuppannassa vā samādhisambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya, uppannassa vā samādhisambojjhaṅgassa bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya bhāvanāya pāripūriyā saṃvattatī’’ti evaṃ uppādo hoti. Ayaṃ vuccati – ‘‘samaye ajjhupekkhanatā’’ti. <hi rend="bold">Asamāhitapuggalaparivajjanatā</hi> nāma upacāraṃ vā appanaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0346"/> vā appattānaṃ vikkhittacittānaṃ puggalānaṃ ārakā parivajjanaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Samāhitapuggalasevanā</hi> nāma upacārena vā appanāya vā samāhitacittānaṃ sevanā bhajanā payirupāsanā. <hi rend="bold">Tadadhimuttatā</hi> nāma ṭhānanisajjādīsu samādhiuppādanatthaṃyeva ninnapoṇapabbhāracittatā. Evaṃ uppannassa panassa arahattamaggena bhāvanāpāripūri hotīti pajānāti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Samaye ajjhupekkhanatā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Asamāhitapuggalaparivajjanatā</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0346"/> <hi rend="bold">Samāhitapuggalasevanā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Tadadhimuttatā</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Upekkhāsambojjhaṅgassa</hi> ‘‘atthi, bhikkhave, upekkhāsambojjhaṅgaṭṭhānīyā dhammā, tattha yonisomanasikārabahulīkāro, ayamāhāro anuppannassa vā upekkhāsambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya, uppannassa vā upekkhāsambojjhaṅgassa bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya bhāvanāya pāripūriyā saṃvattatī’’ti evaṃ uppādo hoti. Tattha upekkhāva upekkhāsambojjhaṅgaṭṭhānīyā dhammā nāma.</p> <hi rend="bold">Upekkhāsambojjhaṅgassa</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Apica pañca dhammā upekkhāsambojjhaṅgassa uppādāya saṃvattanti sattamajjhattatā saṅkhāramajjhattatā sattasaṅkhārakelāyanapuggalaparivajjanatā <pb ed="P" n="3.0796"/> sattasaṅkhāramajjhattapuggalasevanatā tadadhimuttatāti. Tattha dvīhākārehi <hi rend="bold">sattamajjhattataṃ</hi> samuṭṭhāpeti ‘‘tvaṃ attano kammena āgantvā attanova kammena gamissasi, esopi attanova kammena āgantvā attanova kammena gamissati, tvaṃ kaṃ kelāyasī’’ti evaṃ kammassakatāpaccavekkhaṇena, ‘‘paramatthato sattoyeva natthi, so tvaṃ kaṃ kelāyasī’’ti evaṃ nissattapaccavekkhaṇena cāti. Dvīhevākārehi <hi rend="bold">saṅkhāramajjhattataṃ</hi> samuṭṭhāpeti – ‘‘idaṃ cīvaraṃ anupubbena vaṇṇavikāratañceva jiṇṇabhāvañca upagantvā pādapuñchanacoḷakaṃ hutvā yaṭṭhikoṭiyā chaḍḍanīyaṃ bhavissati, sace panassa sāmiko <pb ed="M" n="2.0385"/> bhaveyya, nāssa evaṃ vinassituṃ dadeyyā’’ti evaṃ assāmikabhāvapaccavekkhaṇena ca, ‘‘anaddhaniyaṃ idaṃ tāvakālika’’nti evaṃ tāvakālikabhāvapaccavekkhaṇena cāti. Yathā ca cīvare, evaṃ pattādīsupi yojanā kātabbā.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0796"/> <hi rend="bold">sattamajjhattataṃ</hi> <hi rend="bold">saṅkhāramajjhattataṃ</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0385"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Sattasaṅkhārakelāyanapuggalaparivajjanatā</hi>ti ettha yo puggalo gihi vā attano puttadhītādike, pabbajito vā attano antevāsikasamānupajjhāyakādike mamāyati, sahattheneva nesaṃ kesacchedanasūcikammacīvaradhovanarajanapattapacanādīni karoti, muhuttampi apassanto ‘‘asuko sāmaṇero kuhiṃ asuko daharo kuhi’’nti bhantamigo viya ito cito ca oloketi, aññena kesacchedanādīnaṃ atthāya ‘‘muhuttaṃ asukaṃ pesethā’’ti yāciyamānopi ‘‘amhepi taṃ attano kammaṃ na kārema, tumhe naṃ gahetvā kilamessathā’’ti na deti, ayaṃ sattakelāyano nāma.</p> <hi rend="bold">Sattasaṅkhārakelāyanapuggalaparivajjanatā</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Yo <pb ed="V" n="2.0347"/> pana cīvarapattathālakakattarayaṭṭhiādīni mamāyati, aññassa hatthena parāmasitumpi na deti, tāvakālikaṃ yācito ‘‘mayampi idaṃ mamāyantā na paribhuñjāma, tumhākaṃ kiṃ dassāmā’’ti vadati, ayaṃ <hi rend="bold">saṅkhārakelāyano</hi> nāma. Yo pana tesu dvīsupi vatthūsu majjhatto udāsino, ayaṃ <hi rend="bold">sattasaṅkhāramajjhatto</hi> nāma. Iti ayaṃ upekkhāsambojjhaṅgo evarūpaṃ sattasaṅkhārakelāyanapuggalaṃ ārakā parivajjantassāpi, sattasaṅkhāramajjhattapuggalaṃ sevantassāpi, ṭhānanisajjādīsu taduppādanatthaṃ ninnapoṇapabbhāracittassāpi <pb ed="P" n="3.0797"/> uppajjati. Evaṃ uppannassa panassa arahattamaggena bhāvanāpāripūri hotīti pajānāti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0347"/> <hi rend="bold">saṅkhārakelāyano</hi> <hi rend="bold">sattasaṅkhāramajjhatto</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0797"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi>ti evaṃ attano vā satta sambojjhaṅge pariggaṇhitvā, parassa vā, kālena vā attano, kālena vā parassa sambojjhaṅge pariggaṇhitvā dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati. Samudayavayā panettha sambojjhaṅgānaṃ nibbattinirodhavasena veditabbā. Ito paraṃ vuttanayameva. Kevalañhi idha bojjhaṅgapariggāhikā sati dukkhasaccanti evaṃ yojanaṃ katvā bojjhaṅgapariggāhakassa bhikkhuno niyyānamukhaṃ veditabbaṃ. Sesaṃ tādisamevāti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Iti ajjhattaṃ vā</hi> <p rend="centre">Bojjhaṅgapabbaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.</p> <p rend="subhead">Catusaccapabbavaṇṇanā</p>
385. Thus, having divided the contemplation of phenomena (dhammānupassanā) by way of the six internal and external sense bases, now, in order to divide it by way of the enlightenment factors (bojjhaṅga), he says beginning with *puna capara*. There, *bojjhaṅgesu* means among the limbs of the awaking mind. *Santa* means existing, being present through attainment. *Satisambojjhaṅga* means the enlightenment factor known as mindfulness (smṛti).
